# Full Project Review — soos (2026-09-29)

- **Base commit**: `fbb99c4` (`origin/main`, after PR #142)
- **Branch**: `chore/full-project-review`
- **Method**: 8 independent domain reviewers (read-only, every finding tied to opened `file:line` evidence) followed by one adversarial verifier per domain that re-opened the code and re-ran commands to refute each finding. Headline findings were additionally spot-checked by the orchestrator (see §1.3).
- **GitHub tracking**: #270 (one issue per defect, label `review-2026-09-29`; issue numbers are listed on every finding and in Appendix B).
- **Purpose**: input for the correction pass. Each finding lists evidence, impact, a concrete fix with the test that must prove it, and the verifier's independent notes. Severities below are the **verifier-corrected** ones.

## 1. Executive Summary

### 1.1 Statistics (verified findings, REFUTED excluded)

| Area | CRITICAL | MAJOR | MINOR | SUGGESTION | Total |
|---|---:|---:|---:|---:|---:|
| Anti-Spoofing (PAD) | 2 | 4 | 7 | 2 | 15 |
| Camera Detection & GUI | 2 | 7 | 7 | 1 | 17 |
| Installation & Onboarding | 2 | 8 | 5 | 1 | 16 |
| PAM Module & IPC Protocol | 1 | 4 | 9 | 3 | 17 |
| Privileged Daemon | 1 | 7 | 8 | 3 | 19 |
| Storage & CLIs | 2 | 9 | 10 | 1 | 22 |
| Vision & Inference Core | 1 | 2 | 10 | 2 | 15 |
| Tests, CI, Tooling & Documentation | 1 | 5 | 8 | 1 | 15 |
| **Total** | 12 | 46 | 64 | 14 | 136 |

Refuted by verifiers: 1 (Appendix A). Several defects were found independently by more than one reviewer; they are cross-referenced in §1.4 and should be fixed once.

### 1.2 Headline Findings (fix first)

1. **Anti-spoofing is inverted in production.** The daemon (`crates/daemon/src/pipeline.rs:189-193`) and `soos-enroll` (`crates/enrollment-cli/src/service.rs:773`) construct `OrtPadDetector::new_with_class_index(.., 2)`, while the project default and upstream MiniFASNet convention use class 1 = live (`crates/inference-ort/src/pad.rs:82`). The walkthrough-72 fix only changed the constant; the call sites still override it. The GUI uses 1, so what the user calibrates in the GUI is not what PAM enforces. (PAD-01, CAM-09, DMN-01, STO-01, VIS-01.)
2. **PAD is "first passing frame wins".** The dispatcher multi-frame loop evaluates up to ~15–40 frames per request and returns Allow on the first frame that passes; spoof frames are never aggregated or vetoed, and the rate limiter counts requests, not frames (PAD-02).
3. **`panic = "abort"` in `[profile.release]` defeats every `catch_unwind` in `pam_soos.so`.** A panic in the module aborts `gdm`/`sudo`/`login` instead of returning `PAM_IGNORE` — the central fail-safe invariant is not delivered by the shipped binary (PAM-01, TCI-01).
4. **Raw camera frames are served to any `soos`-group peer.** `RequestKind::PreviewFrame` has no UID/root/session/opt-in check (CAM-01, DMN-02); `PasswordFailed` events trust the payload UID (PAM-04, DMN-03).
5. **Packages ship a shared master key.** `build_deb.sh`/`build_arch.sh` run `install.sh --destdir`, which generates `/var/lib/soos/master.key` inside the staging tree, so every installation of a given package shares one AES key (ONB-01).
6. **Camera selection is non-deterministic across components.** Daemon, GUI-direct and enrollment CLI use three different resolvers (IR vs RGB, `/dev/videoN` vs by-id), the daemon never re-enumerates, and the GUI fights the daemon for the device (CAM-02…CAM-07). This explains the reported wrong-camera and intermittent GUI failures.
7. **Installation is not autonomous and partly broken.** Missing dependency declarations (libclang, OpenSSL headers, python ≥ 3.11 for `download_models.sh`, GUI runtime libs), `install.sh` exits 0 with missing artifacts and never builds, Fedora `authselect` profile cannot be activated as documented, Debian notify hook placed after `pam_deny`, uninstall cannot roll back PAM (ONB-02…ONB-10). A full spec for a one-command bootstrap is in §3.3.
8. **Evidence of PAD quality is not real.** No test exercises the real ONNX models; APCER/BPCER claims are tautological, and the attested "MobileFaceNet ~3.6 MB" ArcFace is actually a 136 MB ResNet34 NHWC network (PAD-06, VIS-03).

### 1.3 Orchestrator Spot-Checks

Re-verified directly on `fbb99c4`:
- `crates/daemon/src/pipeline.rs:189-193` → `OrtPadDetector::new_with_class_index(pad_session, config.vision.pad_threshold, 2)`; `crates/enrollment-cli/src/service.rs:773` → `new_with_class_index(pad_session, 0.80, 2)`.
- `Cargo.toml:126` → `panic = "abort"` under `[profile.release]`; `crates/pam/Cargo.toml:13` → `crate-type = ["cdylib", "rlib"]`.
- `scripts/build_deb.sh:100` runs `scripts/install.sh --destdir`, and `scripts/install.sh:235-243` generates `master.key` under the (staging) state dir.
- `crates/daemon/src/dispatcher.rs:346-360` handles `PreviewFrame` without any peer authorization before serving frames.
- `/var/lib/soos/models/arcface_w600k_mbf.onnx` on the development host is 136,619,444 bytes.

### 1.4 Cross-Area Duplicates (fix once)

| Defect | Finding IDs |
|---|---|
| MiniFASNet live class index 2 hardcoded in daemon/enrollment | PAD-01, CAM-09, DMN-01, STO-01, VIS-01 |
| `panic = "abort"` vs `catch_unwind` in the PAM cdylib | PAM-01, TCI-01 |
| Unauthorized PreviewFrame raw-frame streaming | CAM-01, DMN-02 |
| PasswordFailed event trusts payload UID | PAM-04, DMN-03 |

| GUI import writes plaintext embedding to /tmp | CAM-08, STO-12 |

### 1.5 Recommended Correction Order

1. **Security invariants**: live class index (single source of truth + invariant test), PAM panic strategy (`panic = "unwind"` for the PAM crate or a dedicated profile, with a test that a panic returns `PAM_IGNORE` from the release `.so`), PreviewFrame and PasswordFailed authorization, shared master key in packages, plaintext embedding temp files, debug-vision cleartext frame.
2. **Anti-spoofing redesign** (§3.1 plan): multi-frame aggregation with spoof veto, validated thresholds, IR handling, real-model fixtures and metrics.
3. **Camera determinism & GUI robustness** (§3.2 plan): one shared resolver, persisted stable device ID, real health reporting, GUI error surfacing, no device fights.
4. **One-command bootstrap & packaging fixes** (§3.3 plan), including PAM activation/rollback correctness per distro.
5. **Daemon/PAM robustness**: cumulative read deadline, per-UID connection caps, `spawn_blocking` for inference, session binding.
6. **Storage/CLI** correctness, then **tests/CI/docs drift** (verification matrix citations, timeouts, multi-distro matrix in CI).

Each fix follows the repository workflow (`.agents/skills/dev-workflow`): topic branch, spec, tests first, audit, implementation, fingerprint-bound candid review, traceability.

## 2. How to Read a Finding

`ID` · **verified severity** · verifier verdict (`CONFIRMED` / `PARTIAL`) · category · effort (S/M/L). *Evidence* is the reviewer's observation; *Verifier notes* are the independent re-check (including corrections — when they conflict with the reviewer's text, the verifier notes prevail).

## 3. Focus Areas — Improvement Plans

### 3.1 Anti-Spoofing (PAD)

#### Proposed plan (reviewer)

Ordered design to materially improve spoof resistance within the constraints (ORT CPU, no OpenCV, PAM deadline 1000-2500 ms, daemon budget 900 ms+).

Step 0 — Establish ground truth (prerequisite for everything else; cost S, latency 0).
- Capture on the target laptop, with the shipped SCRFD boxes and the Rust crop, a corpus of 80x80 crops: RGB sensor and IR sensor, >=20 bona fide (varied lighting/pose), >=20 printed photos (matte + glossy), >=20 screen replays (phone OLED, laptop LCD, video). Store crops (not full frames) as test fixtures with the raw MiniFASNetV2 logits.
- Run the real model (ignored-by-default cargo test gated on SOOS_MODELS_DIR) and record argmax per class. This settles the class order empirically (expected: index 1 = live per upstream test.py) and produces the first real APCER/BPCER numbers. Write the ADR and fix PAD-01/PAD-05 in the same PR.
- Required tests: `pad_real_model_tests.rs` (class order, APCER/BPCER ceilings), golden logits regression.

Step 1 — Fix the decision logic (PAD-02, PAD-04, PAD-10, PAD-16; cost M, latency +0 to +60 ms).
- Add `pad_score: f32` to `AuthContext`; policy applies `pad_threshold` (removes dead config, makes the policy crate testable for PAD).
- Add a clockless `PadAggregator` (soos-policy): window n=5, require k=3 consecutive frames with `is_live && p_live >= threshold` and match >= threshold, spoof veto (any spoof-classified frame within the window => Deny/PadFailed for the request). Dispatcher only Allows on aggregator consensus. Expected benefit: per-request APCER drops from 1-(1-p)^N to roughly p^k with veto; BPCER impact small because genuine faces are stable across consecutive frames. Latency: +2 frames (~60 ms at 30 fps) before Allow — fits the 900 ms budget.
- Validate TOML thresholds through the builder with floors.
- Tests: alternating spoof/live must Deny; 3 consecutive live => Allow; proptest 'window containing a spoof never Allows'; config rejection tests.

Step 2 — Two-scale fusion as upstream (PAD-07; cost M, latency +8-15 ms).
- Add 4_0_0_80x80_MiniFASNetV1SE.onnx to manifest.toml (SHA-256, Apache-2.0 source). Pipeline builds two crops (2.7x and 4.0x via `expand_bbox_for_pad`), runs both sessions, averages softmax vectors, thresholds the averaged live probability. Expected benefit: restores the upstream operating point (97.8% TPR @ 1e-5 FPR claimed by upstream for the fused pipeline) instead of a single-scale approximation; the 4.0 crop specifically captures device bezels/paper edges (replay/print cues).
- Tests: manifest count, two-crop scales asserted via a recording MockPadDetector, fusion arithmetic unit test, latency test under 60 ms for both PADs.

Step 3 — Format-aware IR path (PAD-03; cost L, latency +2-5 ms).
- Thread `PixelFormat`/monochrome flag into `VisionPipeline::process_frame` and PAD. For Grey frames: (a) do not silently reuse RGB thresholds; (b) add an IR liveness gate using NIR physics: mean intensity/contrast of the face crop versus the 4.0x context (screens appear as flat dark rectangles under NIR; prints lack the specular skin/eye response), plus MiniFASNet run with an IR-calibrated threshold derived from the Step 0 IR corpus; (c) prefer running PAD on the RGB sibling when both sensors exist (camera-v4l already enumerates both), matching on IR. Document IR emitter requirements (linux-enable-ir-emitter) in Docs/CAMERA_V4L_CRATE.md and the installer.
- Tests: Grey fixture through pipeline must take the IR path; IR corpus APCER/BPCER ceilings.

Step 4 — Quality gates and crop fidelity (PAD-08, PAD-13; cost S, latency +1 ms).
- `min_face_width` (>= 12% of frame width) and Laplacian-variance blur gate before PAD; centre-aligned bilinear sampling and `img_w-1` bounds matching the upstream crop; golden crop test against the Python reference.

Step 5 — Temporal / challenge cues (cost M-L, latency 0 to +300 ms depending on mode).
- Passive temporal cues at no user cost, computed from the SCRFD 5-point landmarks already available per frame: landmark micro-motion variance over the window (a static print has near-zero non-rigid motion; a replayed video has motion but usually fails Step 2/3), eye-region intensity change (blink proxy) over 1-2 s when budget permits (GDM 2500 ms). Screen-specific cue: temporal flicker / moire energy in the 4.0x context crop (high-frequency band ratio between consecutive frames), computable with a small separable filter in pure Rust.
- Optional active challenge for high-value services (sudo/polkit): 'turn head slightly' verified by the existing `estimate_head_pose` yaw delta > 8 degrees within 1.5 s; only when timeout_ms >= 2000. Expected benefit: defeats static prints and most replays independent of the CNN; cost: UX and latency, so keep it policy-driven per service.
- Tests: synthetic frame sequences (static vs jittered landmarks) through the aggregator; pose-delta challenge unit tests.

Step 6 — Calibration, metrics and evidence (PAD-06, PAD-11, PAD-14; cost S-M).
- `soos-enroll verify --pad-report` prints per-frame PAD scores and class probabilities; adversarial_test.sh drives it, never fabricates counts in --mock, and writes APCER/BPCER to a JSON report that the walkthrough/matrix cites. Add ISO/IEC 30107-3 metric definitions (APCER per attack type, BPCER, at the shipped threshold) to Docs/VISION_CRATE.md. Store an evidence snapshot on PadFailed (opt-in, capped).
- Tests: CLI report test with MockPadDetector spoof; script invariant test updated to require a 'SIMULATION' banner in mock mode.

Sequencing rationale: Step 0 and Step 1 are cheap and remove the two defects that dominate today's exposure (inverted class in the auth path, any-frame-wins). Step 2 is the single biggest model-side gain and mirrors upstream exactly. Step 3 addresses the user's actual hardware (IR default). Steps 4-6 harden and make the improvement measurable so future changes cannot regress silently.

#### Plan critique (verifier)

Overall the sequencing (ground truth first, then decision logic, then model-side gains) is right, and Steps 0/1 target the two verified defects (PAD-01, PAD-02). Problems and gaps:

1. Step 0 assumes the shipped ONNX follows minivision test.py semantics (index 1 = live). The manifest source (models/manifest.toml:44) is the QingHeYang ONNX fork, and AI/DECISIONS.md:20 claims that fork uses [Live, Print, Replay]. Neither claim is verified; the plan correctly says 'settle empirically', but it must not pre-commit to 'expected: index 1' when writing tests. Also, the golden corpus must not be committed to git: 80x80 face crops of a real person are biometric data at rest (AGENTS.md: never store/log frames or biometrics). Keep the corpus outside the repo (env-var path, ignored test), commit only argmax labels and logits digests.

2. Step 1 latency assumptions are wrong. 'k=3 consecutive frames => +~60 ms at 30 fps' ignores that each loop iteration runs SCRFD 640x640 + PAD + the embedding model + a 25 ms sleep (dispatcher.rs:778). The installed embedding model is 136,619,444 bytes (sha256 matches manifest ffe014...), not the ~3.6 MB MobileFaceNet documented in AI/ARCHITECTURE.md:189, so per-frame cost is likely well above 60 ms on CPU. With DECISION_BUDGET_MS = 900 (daemon/pipeline.rs) and a 1000 ms PAM deadline, requiring 3 consecutive full-pipeline frames may time out on slower hosts. Measure per-frame latency first; consider running only detect+PAD until PAD consensus and embedding only on the last frame(s).

3. The 'spoof veto for the whole request' interacts badly with warmup_frames = 0 (config.rs:244, intentional per CLP2): the first frames after wake may be AEC-unconverged and could veto a genuine user for the entire request, raising BPCER. Bound the veto to the sliding window (or to high-confidence spoof only) rather than the whole request.

4. 'Validate TOML thresholds through the builder with floors': `ThresholdConfigBuilder::build` (threshold.rs:106-138) only rejects NaN/inf/outside [0,1]; 0.0 passes. The floor must be implemented in daemon config (or a new policy validator), and the plan should say so explicitly.

5. Step 2 (two-scale fusion): availability of a 4.0 V1SE ONNX export in the QingHeYang fork with a compatible license is unverified. `pad_bbox_scale` is a single config field (pipeline.rs:36) and `PadDetector` has one `evaluate_liveness`; fusion needs per-model crop scales and a fused decision type. `manifest_tests::test_manifest_v2_model_count_and_checksum_attestation` (manifest_tests.rs:118) asserts the model count and must be updated as part of the feature (new criterion), not weakened. Benefit is unmeasured; treat as MINOR until Step 0 data shows single-model APCER is inadequate.

6. Step 3 (IR path): the NIR physics claim ('screens appear as flat dark rectangles') only holds when the IR emitter is active. Nothing in the repo handles or documents emitters (`grep -i emitter` -> no hits), and on Linux the emitter typically requires third-party tooling (linux-enable-ir-emitter, per-device calibration, not in standard distro repos) — an installer dependency risk the plan glosses over. Without the emitter the IR sensor sees ambient NIR and the assumption fails. 'Run PAD on the RGB sibling while matching on IR' requires the daemon to own two /dev/video nodes concurrently; whether V4lCameraManager supports two simultaneous devices was not verified by the reviewer (or by me) and must be checked before planning on it.

7. Step 5: SCRFD 5-point landmarks give eye centres only, not eyelid contours, so a 'blink proxy' from landmark motion is weak; intensity-based blink detection on an 80x80 crop is noisy. `estimate_head_pose` exists (crates/vision/src/pose.rs:46) so the yaw-delta challenge is feasible, but it must remain policy-driven per service because sudo/polkit PAM timeouts are typically 1000 ms.

8. Missing steps: (a) fix PAD-01 immediately (remove the hardcoded 2 in daemon/pipeline.rs:189-193 and enrollment-cli/service.rs:773) — this is a one-line change and should not wait for the full corpus; (b) add PAD self-test at startup (PAD-09) so a wrong index/output shape fails closed with a log line; (c) update the four conflicting docs/matrix rows (PAD-05) in the same PR as the code fix, including the non-existent test names at VERIFICATION_MATRIX.md:393; (d) drop PAD-15 from the plan — warmup_frames = 0 is an accepted, tested contract.

### 3.2 Camera Detection & GUI

#### Proposed plan (reviewer)

Ordered design for deterministic camera selection and GUI robustness (each step lists the tests that prove it; all tests hermetic unless marked HW).

1. Shared resolver in soos-camera-v4l (new module `resolver.rs`).
   - `trait DeviceEnumerator { fn enumerate(&self) -> Vec<CameraCandidate>; }` with `SysfsV4lEnumerator` (production: /sys/class/video4linux + query_caps device_caps + enum_formats + enum_framesizes + /dev/v4l/by-id reverse map + bus_info/driver) and `FakeEnumerator(Vec<CameraCandidate>)` for tests.
   - `CameraCandidate { node: PathBuf, by_id: Option<PathBuf>, stable_id: String (by-id name, else "bus_info|card"), card, driver, bus_info, device_caps, formats: Vec<PixelFormat>, raw_fourccs: Vec<[u8;4]>, frame_sizes: Vec<(u32,u32)>, sensor: SensorType, score: i32 }`.
   - `CameraSelectionSource { Cli(Option<PathBuf>), ConfigFile(Option<&Path>), Persisted(Option<&Path>) }` and one function `resolve_camera(sources, preference, &dyn DeviceEnumerator) -> Result<Selection, ResolveError>` with `Selection { device: PathBuf /* by-id when available */, stable_id, candidate, reason: SelectionReason }`. Sentinels unified: "auto", "default", "", "/dev/v4l/by-id/default-camera" all mean auto. Explicit paths are validated (must exist, must have VIDEO_CAPTURE|STREAMING device_caps) and never silently replaced; failure is an error, not a substitution.
   - Daemon (`pipeline.rs`), enrollment CLI (`service.rs`), GUI (`main.rs`) call only this function; delete `resolve_camera_device_from_config` internals and the by-id-first branch.
   - Tests: `resolver_tests::{test_auto_prefers_ir_with_rgb_and_ir, test_identical_card_names_use_by_id_and_formats, test_ir_with_yuyv_and_small_square_frames_classified_ir, test_y16_only_node_kept, test_metadata_node_excluded_by_device_caps, test_explicit_missing_path_is_error_not_substitution, test_all_sentinels_equivalent, test_selection_returns_by_id_when_available, test_daemon_cli_gui_same_selection_for_same_inputs}`; invariant test that no crate other than camera-v4l reads /sys/class/video4linux or /dev/v4l/by-id.

2. Persisted stable ID.
   - `/var/lib/soos/camera.toml` (`stable_id`, `bus_info`, `card`, `selected_at`, `selected_by`), written by `soos-admin camera select <id|auto>` and by successful enrollment; read by the resolver as highest-priority source after CLI. If the persisted id is absent from enumeration, log a warning with the candidate list and fall back to heuristics (never pick a different camera silently for auth: return `ResolveError::PersistedCameraMissing` in the daemon, which keeps retrying — see step 4).
   - Tests: `test_persisted_id_wins_over_heuristics`, `test_persisted_id_missing_daemon_retries_not_substitutes`, `test_enrollment_records_stable_id`, file-permission test (0644 root, atomic rename).

3. Capability probing and classification.
   - Extend `fourcc_to_pixel_format` with Y8I/Y16/Y10/Y12 → `Grey16`/`Grey` family; scoring: by-id/card contains "ir"/"infrared" (+40), grey-only fourccs (+30), frame sizes ⊆ {≤640×360, square} (+10), colour fourccs (−20); `Unknown` only when score in a dead band. Expose `probe_device(&Path) -> ProbeReport` (device_caps, granted format after S_FMT dry-run via TRY_FMT, fps via G_PARM).
   - Tests: table-driven names from walkthrough 72 and host probe; `test_try_fmt_reports_granted_fourcc` (HW, `#[ignore]`).

4. Supervisor hardening (`v4l_impl.rs`).
   - `run_v4l_supervisor` takes `Arc<dyn CameraResolver>`; on DeviceNotFound/UnsupportedCapability/DeviceBusy it re-resolves before each retry (bounded by backoff). Map EBUSY/ENODEV from S_FMT/REQBUFS/STREAMON through `from_io_error` (retain `raw_os_error`). Verify `actual_format.fourcc`, reject or de-stride `bytesperline`, skip ERROR-flagged buffers, size-check uncompressed frames, call `set_params` for fps, poll timeout 250 ms with starvation counter, and publish `CameraStatus` (Starting/Warmup/Ready/Suspended/Error{kind,since,retries}, device, stable_id, format, fps) via a new default trait method `status()`.
   - Tests: `error_recovery_tests::{test_set_format_ebusy_maps_to_device_busy, test_supervisor_reresolves_after_enodev}` (fake resolver), `frame_validation_tests::{test_short_yuyv_rejected, test_error_flag_skipped, test_fourcc_mismatch_is_set_format_error}`, mock parity tests for `status()`; HW `#[ignore]` drop-latency < 500 ms.

5. Health and daemon diagnostics.
   - 500 ms poller copies `camera.status()` into `HealthState` (`camera_ready`, `camera_state`, `camera_device`); `StatusResponse` gains a bounded `camera_state: u8` + `camera_device` string; `soos-admin status` prints them.
   - Tests: `health_tests::test_camera_ready_tracks_manager_status`, dispatcher status roundtrip test, protocol bounded-size test.

6. Diagnostics command.
   - `soos-admin camera list|probe <path>|select <id|auto>` (human + `--json`), reusing steps 1-3; installer runs `soos-admin camera list` at the end and prints the selection and reason; `soos-enroll` prints selected device/stable_id before capture.
   - Tests: CLI snapshot tests with `FakeEnumerator`; invariant test that admin-cli stays non-biometric (no frame access).

7. GUI robustness.
   - Init `tracing_subscriber` (stderr, `RUST_LOG`). New `CameraSource` state machine on a background thread: probe classifies EACCES (show "add user to soos group and re-login", stay in `NoAccess`), ECONNREFUSED/ENOENT + `systemctl is-active` false → Direct, connected → Ipc; re-evaluates every 2 s and on Pause/Resume; swaps the manager through `Arc<ArcSwap<Arc<dyn CameraManager>>>` consumed by the worker. Daemon-state polling and pkexec calls move off the UI thread (result channel). Header shows `CameraStatus` (device, format, fps, last error). Embedding hand-off to `pkexec soos-enroll import --stdin` over a pipe with zeroization; /tmp key/store fallback replaced by explicit `--dev-store` flag with a red banner.
   - Tests: `camera_source_tests::{test_eacces_never_goes_direct, test_direct_refused_while_daemon_active, test_switches_to_ipc_when_daemon_starts, test_pause_switches_to_direct_after_release}` with fake probe/controller; `layout_tests::test_status_banner_shows_camera_error`; test that `ui()` performs no `Command` spawn (trait injection); `test_finalize_writes_no_temp_file`.

8. Preview hardening (daemon).
   - `[preview] enabled = false` default; PreviewFrame requires `peer.uid == uid_hint`, active logind session, one client at a time; daemon downscales to ≤640-px RGB before encoding; oversize → `format: 255` response, never a disconnect; ADR entry in AI/DECISIONS.md.
   - Tests: `dispatcher_tests::{test_preview_disabled_by_default, test_preview_requires_active_session, test_preview_uid_mismatch_rejected, test_preview_oversize_returns_empty_response}`.

9. Provisioning and docs.
   - Installer writes `/etc/soos/daemon.toml` with `camera_device = "auto"`, `sensor_preference = "prefer_ir"`, `[preview] enabled = <gui installed>`; `soos-admin add-user` prints the re-login requirement; fix Docs/CAMERA_V4L_CRATE.md defaults, ENROLLMENT_CLI.md, matrix test names; add ADRs (auto-selection semantics, preview). Invariant test: every `test_*` name cited in AI/VERIFICATION_MATRIX.md exists.

10. Single-constant PAD live class (CAM-09) shipped alongside, since GUI liveness feedback is part of the camera UX: shared `soos_inference_ort::pad::DEFAULT_MINIFASNET_LIVE_CLASS_INDEX` used by daemon/CLI/GUI, invariant grep test.

#### Plan critique (verifier)

Overall the plan targets the right root causes, but several assumptions need correction before it is executed.

Step 1 (shared resolver): 'explicit path must exist or error, never substitute' is right for enrollment/GUI, but for the daemon an error at startup turns a transiently absent camera into a systemd restart loop (Restart=on-failure, RestartSec=2, no StartLimit in packaging/soos-daemon.service). The daemon must keep spawning the supervisor and rely on step 4 re-resolution; only a malformed/non-/dev path should be fatal. Keep the existing FHS validation (enrollment-cli args.rs:228 validate_camera_device_path) in the shared function. Enumeration opens every /dev/video* node with O_RDWR (device.rs:38); doing that on every backoff is cheap but must be bounded by the backoff, and it should skip nodes whose device_caps lack VIDEO_CAPTURE before enum_formats (already the case).

Step 2 (persisted stable id): `soos-admin camera select` runs unprivileged; writing /var/lib/soos/camera.toml needs pkexec/root, and the daemon's ProtectSystem=strict/ReadWritePaths already allows /var/lib/soos. by-id names embed a serial that is frequently identical across units of the same model ('SN0001' on this host), so 'stable_id' is stable per machine only; bus_info changes with USB port. Acceptable, but say so.

Step 3 (classification/probing): adding Grey16/Y8I/Y16 variants touches the shared PixelFormat enum used by protocol PreviewResponse.format (u8 0-4 mapping in dispatcher.rs:363-368 and ipc_camera.rs:170-177) and vision color.rs; 16-bit IR must be converted to 8-bit before SCRFD. Verify that v4l 0.14 exposes VIDIOC_TRY_FMT and per-format enum_framesizes before promising a 'dry-run'; if TRY_FMT is not wrapped it needs a raw ioctl (unsafe, allowed in camera-v4l but must be documented). Frame-size heuristics ('square/<=360p') are weak; make them the lowest-weight signal.

Step 4 (supervisor): 250 ms poll with an 8-timeout starvation counter is fine, but UVC cameras drop to 7.5-10 fps in low light, so a single timeout must never be an error (plan already counts). set_params via Capture::set_params(Parameters::with_fps) exists in v4l 0.14; log the granted rate since drivers silently clamp.

Step 5 (health/status): StatusResponse is a postcard struct with no self-description; adding fields is a wire-format change that requires a protocol version bump or a new response kind, plus updating admin-cli status.rs decoding and the protocol bounded-size tests; note pam does not consume StatusResponse so PAM is unaffected.

Step 6 (diagnostics command): probe must use only QUERYCAP/ENUM_FMT/TRY_FMT; S_FMT on a node the daemon streams returns EBUSY and would also violate the daemon-exclusive-ownership rule.

Step 7 (GUI): swapping managers via ArcSwap requires dropping the old direct V4lCameraManager (join, up to the DQBUF timeout) BEFORE calling pkexec systemctl start, otherwise Resume collides exactly as CAM-02; sequence must be stop-direct -> resume-daemon -> connect-IPC. pkexec inherits stdin, so `soos-enroll import --stdin` works, but the CLI currently has only --file (args.rs:144-146) and the import must also cap/validate stdin length. With [preview] enabled=false (step 8) and the daemon active, the GUI must show 'preview disabled by policy' rather than fall back to direct capture.

Step 8 (preview hardening): moving the PreviewFrame block after Step 6/6b is sufficient for uid and session checks (verify_peer_credentials already exists); note session enforcement is gated by dispatcher.enforce_active_session and tests use a temp logind dir. 'One client at a time' needs a slot released on disconnect (handle_connection exit), not just on error. Downscaling in the daemon adds CPU on the auth path; do it only when the frame exceeds the limit or cap preview at ~15 Hz.

Step 9 (provisioning): install.sh, packaging/debian/postinst and packaging/arch/soos.install all need the same idempotent 'write daemon.toml only if absent' logic (three places, none exist today).

Step 10 (PAD constant): the shared constant alone is insufficient. History shows the value flip-flopped 0 -> 2 -> 1 across #117/#127/#135 with no model-backed test; the plan assumes an 'existing fixture test against the real ONNX' that does not exist. First add a real-model test (fixture live/print/replay crops against /var/lib/soos/models/minifasnet_v2_80x80.onnx, #[ignore] or gated on model presence) to fix the index empirically, then remove the literals in pipeline.rs:192 and service.rs:773. Given the daemon is the authentication path, this should be the first item shipped, not the last, and DECISIONS.md:19 must be corrected in the same change.

Missing from the plan: a hotplug signal is not needed if re-resolution happens on every backoff, but the daemon should also re-resolve after Suspend->resume; and the walkthrough/ADR for /dev/videoN auto-selection should record that by-id links are preferred only when they resolve to the selected node.

### 3.3 Installation & Onboarding

#### Proposed plan (reviewer)

## One-command bootstrap specification — `scripts/bootstrap.sh`

### 0. Entry points
- Canonical: `git clone --depth 1 https://github.com/Mysticaly622/soos && cd soos && sudo ./scripts/bootstrap.sh` (single command after clone).
- Optional one-liner: `curl -fsSLo soos-bootstrap.sh https://github.com/Mysticaly622/soos/releases/latest/download/bootstrap.sh && sha256sum -c <(curl -fsSL .../bootstrap.sh.sha256) && sudo bash soos-bootstrap.sh`. Never document `curl | sudo bash`. The script is written as functions with a final `main "$@"` line so a truncated download executes nothing. Script must pass `shellcheck --severity=error` (already gated by CI lint job) and `bash -n`.
- `set -Eeuo pipefail`, `IFS=$'\n\t'`, `umask 022`, ERR trap printing the failing step, the log path and the rescue instructions (§9). All output English. Every mutation goes through `run()` which honours `--dry-run` (prints the command) and appends to the log.

### 1. Flags (all non-interactive-safe)
`--yes/-y` (accept everything; required for unattended), `--dry-run`, `--distro <debian|ubuntu|fedora|rhel|arch>` (override detection), `--from-source` (default) | `--from-package` (build .deb/.rpm/.pkg via scripts/build_packages.sh then install with the distro tool), `--prefix`, `--no-gui` (skip soos-gui and its runtime libs), `--no-pam` (install everything but do not touch the PAM stack; prints the enable command), `--pam-services <list>` (Arch: which /etc/pam.d files include system-auth to verify), `--enable-gdm`, `--no-daemon-start`, `--skip-models` | `--models-from <dir>` (offline: copy + verify via download_models.sh file:// path), `--offline` (fails fast if any network step would be needed: rustup, crates, ORT binaries, models), `--use-system-rust`, `--rust-version <x.y.z>` (default from rust-toolchain.toml), `--enroll <user>` (runs `soos-admin add-user` + `soos-enroll enroll --username`; interactive unless `--mock`), `--uninstall [--purge-data]`, `--log-file` (default `/var/log/soos-bootstrap.log`), `--verbose`.
Exit codes: 0 ok, 2 usage, 10 unsupported distro, 20 package install failed, 30 toolchain failed, 40 build failed, 50 install failed, 60 model verification failed (fail-closed, nothing enabled), 70 PAM canary failed (PAM untouched/rolled back), 80 daemon not ready.

### 2. Preflight
- Must run as root (`EUID -eq 0`) but builds run as the invoking user: `BUILD_USER=${SUDO_USER:-root}`, `runuser -u "$BUILD_USER" -- cargo ...` with `CARGO_HOME`/`RUSTUP_HOME` of that user (never build as root in $HOME/root).
- Detect distro from `/etc/os-release` (`ID`, `ID_LIKE`, `VERSION_ID`); supported: debian>=12, ubuntu>=24.04, fedora>=40, rhel/rocky/alma>=9 (EPEL for clang if needed), arch. Anything else → exit 10 with the manual package list printed.
- Detect arch (`x86_64`, `aarch64`), systemd (`/run/systemd/system`), PAM module dir (Debian: `/usr/lib/$(dpkg-architecture -qDEB_HOST_MULTIARCH)/security`; Fedora: `/usr/lib64/security`; Arch: `/usr/lib/security`; verify by locating `pam_unix.so`), camera nodes (`/dev/video*`, `v4l2-ctl --list-devices` if present; absence is a warning, not an error), Wayland/X11 session for GUI (informational), disk space (>= 4 GiB free for target/), network (unless `--offline`).
- Refuse to run inside a container without `--i-am-in-a-container` (systemctl/PAM steps skipped there); print plan and ask for confirmation unless `--yes`.

### 3. Package installation (idempotent, `-y`, minimal)
Build-time + runtime lists (derived from the locked dependency graph: pam-bindings → libpam; v4l2-sys-mit → bindgen → libclang; ort-sys → ureq/native-tls → OpenSSL headers + pkg-config; glutin/winit → dlopen'ed EGL/GL/Wayland/X11/xkbcommon):
- **Debian/Ubuntu (apt-get, `DEBIAN_FRONTEND=noninteractive`)**: `build-essential pkg-config libpam0g-dev libclang-dev clang libssl-dev curl ca-certificates git libpam-runtime` ; GUI runtime (unless `--no-gui`): `libxkbcommon0 libwayland-client0 libwayland-egl1 libegl1 libgl1 libx11-6 libxcursor1 libxi6 libxrandr1 libxrender1` ; optional: `v4l-utils pamtester dpkg-dev` (for `--from-package`).
- **Fedora/RHEL (dnf, `--setopt=install_weak_deps=False`)**: `gcc gcc-c++ make pkgconf-pkg-config pam-devel clang-devel clang-libs openssl-devel curl ca-certificates git authselect shadow-utils` ; GUI: `libxkbcommon wayland-devel? (runtime: wayland) mesa-libEGL mesa-libGL libX11 libXcursor libXi libXrandr` ; optional: `v4l-utils rpm-build systemd-rpm-macros`.
- **Arch (pacman `-S --needed --noconfirm`)**: `base-devel pam clang openssl curl ca-certificates git` ; GUI: `libxkbcommon wayland libglvnd mesa libx11 libxcursor libxi libxrandr` ; optional: `v4l-utils`.
- Do NOT install python; download_models.sh is rewritten to awk/sed (ONB-09).
- Verify after install: `pkg-config --exists openssl libpam`(Debian) / presence of `libclang.so*` / `pam_appl.h`.

### 4. Rust toolchain policy
- If `--use-system-rust` or system `cargo`+`rustc` >= MSRV (declare `rust-version` in Cargo.toml) → use it. Else install rustup for `$BUILD_USER`: download `https://static.rust-lang.org/rustup/dist/<triple>/rustup-init` + `.sha256`, verify, `rustup-init -y --no-modify-path --profile minimal --default-toolchain none`, then `rustup toolchain install $(pinned) --component clippy,rustfmt`. Pin `rust-toolchain.toml` to an exact version (`channel = "1.xx.y"`) so CI and bootstrap agree. `--offline` → require pre-installed toolchain.
- ORT binaries: document that `ort-sys` downloads ONNX Runtime during the build; support `ORT_LIB_LOCATION` passthrough for offline/air-gapped builds, and print the URL/hash ort-sys reports in the log.

### 5. Build
- `runuser -u $BUILD_USER -- cargo build --release --locked --workspace` (exclude `soos-gui` with `--workspace --exclude soos-gui` when `--no-gui`). On failure exit 40 with the last 50 log lines. Never fall back to debug artifacts (ONB-06).
- Optional post-build smoke: `target/release/soos-admin --version`, `soos-enroll --version`, `nm -D target/release/libpam_soos.so | grep pam_sm_authenticate`.

### 6. Install (via a fixed `scripts/install.sh`)
- install.sh changes: root check for live mode; fail on missing artifacts; `--artifact-dir`; distro-aware PAM template selection (ONB-13); NO master.key generation when DESTDIR is set (ONB-01); create `/etc/soos/` (0755) and an annotated `/etc/soos/daemon.toml.example` (camera device, thresholds); do not chmod pre-existing system dirs; models step before `systemctl enable`; keep `--dry-run`.
- Group: `groupadd -r soos` if absent; `usermod -aG soos $SUDO_USER` only when `--enroll` or `--add-user` is given (membership grants facial auth, so it must be explicit).
- Key: generate `/var/lib/soos/master.key` with `openssl rand 32`/`/dev/urandom` via `install -m 0600` from a temp file created with `umask 077` (never chmod after write), only on the live host.
- Models: `scripts/download_models.sh --target-dir /var/lib/soos/models` (rewritten without python; `--proto '=https' --tlsv1.2 --max-time --max-filesize`); then `--check-only` must pass or exit 60 before anything is enabled.
- Unit: install `packaging/soos-daemon.service` (with `ConditionPathExists=/var/lib/soos/models/manifest.toml`, `StartLimitIntervalSec=60`, `StartLimitBurst=5`) to `/usr/lib/systemd/system` for packages and `/etc/systemd/system` for source installs; `systemctl daemon-reload && systemctl enable --now soos-daemon`; readiness: poll `/run/soos/daemon.sock` (mode 0660 root:soos) up to 30 s, then `soos-admin status --format json`; missing camera → warn and continue (daemon fails closed → PAM_IGNORE), print `soos-admin logs -n 50`.

### 7. PAM activation with canary and rollback (skipped with `--no-pam`)
- Precondition printed in bold: keep a root shell open (`sudo -s`) in another terminal; bootstrap also spawns nothing that closes the current session.
- Snapshot: copy every file it may touch to `/var/lib/soos/state/pam-backup-<ts>/` (`/etc/pam.d/*`, `/etc/nsswitch.conf`, `authselect current --raw`, `pam-auth-update` state), store sha256 manifest; symlink `/var/lib/soos/state/last-backup`.
- Debian/Ubuntu: install profiles (fixed soos-notify as Primary/250, ONB-03) and `pam-auth-update --package --enable soos soos-notify`.
- Fedora/RHEL: build a complete custom profile from the currently selected base (`authselect create-profile soos --base-on <current base> --symlink-meta --symlink-nsswitch --symlink-pam` then patch system-auth/password-auth by inserting the two pam_soos lines around pam_unix), declare features in README, `authselect select custom/soos <saved features> --force`, `authselect check` must pass and `/etc/nsswitch.conf` non-comment line count must equal the pre-change count (ONB-02).
- Arch: insert the snippet lines into `/etc/pam.d/system-auth` immediately before the first `auth ... pam_unix.so` line, atomic write (temp + rename), keep `.pacnew` untouched; print which services include system-auth (`grep -l system-auth /etc/pam.d/*`).
- GDM (`--enable-gdm`): `soos-admin gdm enable` after gdm.rs is fixed to back up and write atomically; the disable flag `/etc/soos/gdm.disable` path is printed.
- Canary (mandatory before declaring success): compile `tests/docker/pam_test_runner.c` to `/usr/libexec/soos/pam-check` (or ship it as `soos-admin pam-check`), create a throwaway service `/etc/pam.d/soos-canary` that `include`s the distro stack, run it for a temporary user with a random password (`useradd -M -s /usr/sbin/nologin soos-canary`): (a) with the daemon stopped → password must succeed and wrong password must fail; (b) with the daemon running → same result (no enrollment → Deny → PAM_IGNORE). Any failure → automatic restore from the snapshot (`pam-auth-update --package --remove`, `authselect select <saved> --force`, `cp` backups, `sha256sum -c`) and exit 70. Delete the canary user and service afterwards.
- Also verify `/etc/soos/disabled` kill-switch is documented: `touch /etc/soos/disabled` makes pam_soos return PAM_IGNORE immediately (crates/pam/src/config.rs:60).

### 8. Enrollment (optional `--enroll <user>`)
`soos-admin add-user <user>`; `soos-enroll enroll --username <user>` (interactive, requires camera; `--mock` for CI); verify `stat -c '%a %U:%G' /var/lib/soos/biometrics/<uid>.cbor.enc` = `600 root:root`; `soos-admin test-pam --uid <uid>` expecting Allow only with a live face. Print the exact next commands otherwise.

### 9. Logging, summary and rescue guidance
- Everything `tee`'d to `/var/log/soos-bootstrap.log` (0600); a machine-readable `/var/lib/soos/state/bootstrap.json` (distro, versions, paths, PAM mode, backup dir, timestamp) consumed by `--uninstall` and `soos-admin status`.
- Final summary: installed files, PAM state, daemon state, camera detected, how to disable (`touch /etc/soos/disabled`), how to uninstall, how to rescue: `systemd.unit=rescue.target` + `bash /var/lib/soos/state/last-backup/restore.sh` (generated by the snapshot step; restore.sh is self-contained and does not depend on soos binaries).

### 10. Uninstall path
`sudo ./scripts/bootstrap.sh --uninstall [--purge-data]` → fixed `scripts/uninstall.sh`: stop/disable unit; restore PAM from the recorded snapshot (`pam-auth-update --package --remove`, `authselect select <saved profile + features> --force`, remove gdm-password lines, restore Arch system-auth from backup, verify sha256 equality), then remove binaries/module/unit/profiles/templates; keep `/var/lib/soos` unless `--purge-data` (which shreds master.key and templates); remove group only with `--purge-data`. Package-based installs delegate to `apt/dnf/pacman remove` after PAM restore.

### 11. Tests that must validate the bootstrap (Docker matrix, wired into CI)
1. `tests/docker/Dockerfile.bootstrap.{ubuntu24,debian12,fedora40,arch}`: bare base images (no toolchain, no build deps); run `scripts/bootstrap.sh --yes --i-am-in-a-container --skip-models --models-from tests/fixtures/models --no-daemon-start` (fixture models with matching manifest checksums, or a test manifest pointing at `file://` fixtures). Asserts: exit 0; every documented package list was sufficient (no extra installs allowed: the script must not call the package manager outside its list); `target/release/{soos-daemon,soos-admin,soos-enroll,libpam_soos.so}` exist; installed paths and modes (0700/0755/0600/0750, root:soos on /run/soos when group exists); `pam_soos.so` loads (`pam_test_runner` fallback test T7); no `master.key` inside any package artifact (`dpkg-deb -c`/`tar -tf` grep must be empty) and two fresh installs produce different keys.
2. PAM activation tests per distro: Debian → `pam-auth-update` ordering assertion (soos line before unix, notify line before pam_deny); Fedora → `authselect select custom/soos with-faillock --force` rc=0, `authselect check` rc=0, faillock preauth/authfail present, nsswitch line count unchanged; Arch → snippet inserted exactly once before pam_unix, idempotent on second run.
3. Canary tests: with mock_daemon.py in `allow` mode (socket created as root:soos 0660, never 0777) → PAM_SUCCESS with 0 prompts; in `timeout`/absent mode → password fallback works, wrong password rejected (reuse T1-T9 from test_suite.sh, with Dockerfile.fedora/arch fixed).
4. Idempotency: run bootstrap twice; second run must report 'already up to date' and produce no diff in `/etc/pam.d`, `/etc/nsswitch.conf`, `/var/lib/soos/master.key` (sha256 compare).
5. Dry-run: `--dry-run` from a bare image must leave `find / -newer /tmp/marker` (excluding /proc,/sys,/tmp,/var/log) empty.
6. Uninstall: after bootstrap + uninstall, sha256 of `/etc/pam.d/*`, `/etc/nsswitch.conf` and `authselect current --raw` equal the pre-bootstrap snapshot; binaries/unit/module gone; `/var/lib/soos` preserved unless `--purge-data`.
7. Failure injection: corrupt one model checksum → bootstrap exits 60 and the unit is NOT enabled and PAM is untouched; break the canary (e.g. inject a `pam_deny` line) → exit 70 and automatic restore verified by sha256.
8. Static: `shellcheck --severity=error scripts/bootstrap.sh`, `bash -n`, invariant test asserting bootstrap's package lists match Docs (single source: `scripts/lib/deps.sh` sourced by both bootstrap.sh and the Dockerfiles via `ARG`), and that no Dockerfile uses `RUN echo "...\n"`.
9. CI wiring: new job `bootstrap-matrix` (ubuntu on every PR; debian/fedora/arch on push to main and nightly), plus `packages` job running `tests/docker/test_packages.sh`; VERIFICATION_MATRIX rows PK1-PK7/DV1-DV4 updated to cite these jobs.

### 12. Ordered delivery (each step a `fix/` or `feat/` branch through the TDD cycle)
1. `fix/packaging-master-key` (ONB-01, tests first: staged tree must not contain master.key).
2. `fix/pam-profiles` (ONB-02, ONB-03, ONB-08 backup/restore + gdm.rs atomic write).
3. `fix/docker-matrix` (ONB-04, ONB-05, ONB-10 chmod 777 + real `--mock` enrollment, CI jobs).
4. `fix/install-script-hardening` (ONB-06, ONB-09, ONB-13, ONB-15, ONB-12).
5. `feat/bootstrap-script` (this spec: `scripts/bootstrap.sh`, `scripts/lib/{deps,log,pam}.sh`, Dockerfile.bootstrap.*, README Quick start, Docs/ONBOARDING.md, walkthrough NN_one_command_bootstrap.md).
6. `chore/packaging-metadata` (ONB-14, ONB-16, ONB-11 doc fixes, rust-toolchain pin, dependency docs).

#### Plan critique (verifier)

The plan is broadly sound and well-sequenced (master key first, PAM profiles second, test matrix third), but it has several wrong or missing assumptions:

1. Fedora profile (section 7 / fix/pam-profiles): the plan treats ONB-02 as a missing feature declaration, but the shipped templates use `{?with-faillock:...}`, which is not authselect syntax at all; the lines are emitted literally and Linux-PAM installs must-fail handlers, so `authselect select custom/soos --force` locks every account out (verified with pamtester in fedora:40). The fix MUST rewrite the conditionals as trailing `{include if "with-faillock"}` and add `with-faillock::` to README. Also `authselect create-profile --symlink-pam` makes system-auth/password-auth symlinks to the base profile, so they cannot be patched in place: copy those two files and symlink only nsswitch.conf/fingerprint-auth/smartcard-auth/postlogin/dconf-*. The bootstrap must handle `authselect current` reporting "No existing configuration detected" (opted-out hosts) and RHEL's older authselect versions.

2. Debian notify placement (section 7): moving `soos-notify` to `Auth-Type: Primary` is plausible, but pam-auth-update rewrites the `success=N` jump on pam_unix so the notify line is skipped on success and reached on failure only if its control is `[default=ignore]`/`optional`; this needs an actual generated-file assertion (line index before `pam_deny`) as the plan proposes, plus a mock-daemon test. Note `pam-auth-update --package --enable` from postinst requires `libpam-runtime` and works non-interactively only with `--package`.

3. Dependency lists (section 3): the plan adds libssl-dev/openssl-devel everywhere; a simpler alternative is to switch `ort` from the default `tls-native` to `tls-rustls` (or disable `download-binaries` and pass `ORT_LIB_LOCATION`, whose name is confirmed in ort-sys build/vars.rs:3), which removes the OpenSSL build dependency altogether. Fedora GUI runtime package names are wrong/uncertain (`wayland-devel?`): use `libwayland-client libwayland-egl libxkbcommon mesa-libEGL mesa-libGL libX11 libXcursor libXi libXrandr`. Arch `pam`/`openssl` are already in `base`; `clang-devel` on RHEL 9 is in AppStream, not EPEL. The plan should verify each list by an actual `cargo build --release --locked --workspace` in a bare image (its own test 1 does this; good).

4. Rust policy (section 4): Cargo.toml declares no `rust-version`, so "system cargo >= MSRV" has nothing to compare against; add `rust-version` to `[workspace.package]` and pin `rust-toolchain.toml` in the same change, and make CI use the pin (clippy -D warnings can break on new stable lints).

5. Build as `$SUDO_USER` via `runuser`: correct in spirit, but `cargo` must be resolvable in that user's PATH/CARGO_HOME, `target/` must already be writable by that user, and `--locked` needs the registry available offline for `--offline`.

6. Canary (section 7): `tests/docker/pam_test_runner.c` accepts `<service> <user> [password]` and returns PAM_CONV_ERR when no password is given, so it can drive both cases; but the "daemon running -> no enrollment -> Deny -> PAM_IGNORE" expectation depends on policy behavior outside this area and should be asserted against the real daemon in `--mock-camera` mode, not assumed. `soos-admin logs -n 50` and `soos-admin status --format json` exist as subcommands (args.rs:45,51), but the exact flags must be checked before scripting them.

7. Container tests (section 11): `--i-am-in-a-container` "skips systemctl/PAM steps", yet tests 2, 3, 6 and 7 require PAM activation and rollback inside containers; add a separate `--pam-in-container` override, and note fedora:40 base lacks `authselect` (must be dnf-installed in Dockerfile.bootstrap.fedora). Test 1's "must not call the package manager outside its list" is unverifiable as stated; diff `dpkg -l`/`rpm -qa`/`pacman -Q` before and after instead.

8. ONB-01 fix: `test_install_script_creates_required_directories` (tests/invariants/src/lib.rs:729) asserts master.key IS created under --destdir; under the repo's zero-test-weakening rule this must be handled as an explicit spec change (BACKLOG acceptance line + VERIFICATION_MATRIX PK1 update) before the test is altered. Also add that dpkg/pacman overwrite non-conffile files on upgrade, so any key shipped in a package would also be replaced on upgrade (data loss), which strengthens the case.

9. Unit location (section 6): install.sh currently writes the unit under `${SYSCONFDIR}/systemd/system`, so build_deb.sh ships it as a dpkg conffile in /etc; the plan's split (/usr/lib for packages, /etc for source installs) requires a `--unitdir` flag in install.sh, which is not listed.

10. Missing steps: document the ORT binary download (or vendoring) in Docs, add the root `Dockerfile` (not only tests/docker/*) to the libssl/dependency fix, and make `download_models.sh --check-only` runnable without python before the T9 test relies on it. Effort estimates for fix/pam-profiles should be raised to M/L given the Fedora lockout, restore logic and per-distro tests.

## 4. Findings by Area

### 4.1 Anti-Spoofing (PAD)

*Coverage*: Read in full: crates/inference-ort/src/pad.rs, crates/vision/src/{pipeline.rs,crop.rs,align.rs,color.rs}, crates/daemon/src/{dispatcher.rs,pipeline.rs,config.rs}, crates/policy/src/{lib.rs,decision.rs,threshold.rs,error.rs} (+rate_limit.rs config), crates/gui/src/{worker.rs,main.rs} and PAD parts of app.rs, crates/enrollment-cli/src/{service.rs (verify + build_full_service), guided_enrollment.rs (process_sample), main.rs (exit codes)}, crates/camera-v4l/src/sensor.rs (classify/select) and config default, models/manifest.toml, tests: inference-ort/tests/pad_tests.rs, vision/tests/pad_tests.rs, daemon/tests/pipeline_integration_tests.rs (test_15, test_48), tests/physical/adversarial_test.sh, tests/fixtures/mod.rs (signatures). Docs: AGENTS.md, project-facts.md, walkthroughs 30/58/66/71(partially, via facts)/72/73, AI/ARCHITECTURE.md + AI/DECISIONS.md PAD lines, AI/VERIFICATION_MATRIX.md PAD rows, Docs/VISION_CRATE.md §2.6, Docs/INFERENCE_ORT_CRATE.md PAD lines, Docs/POLICY_CRATE.md. Git history of the live-class-index (commits 5659151/#124, 084ce5c/#127, 72469ba/#135, 94d9ebe/#140). Upstream verification: fetched minivision-ai/Silent-Face-Anti-Spoofing test.py and src/anti_spoof_predict.py (label==1 is Real; softmax; ToTensor only; sum of two models 2.7 V2 + 4.0 V1SE). NOT covered: no empirical run of the real ONNX model (no onnxruntime/onnx Python on host, no bona fide/attack captures available; models are installed at /var/lib/soos/models but the class semantics cannot be established without real face captures); crates/inference-ort/src/detector.rs internals (SCRFD) only grepped for face-size gates; PAM crate only grepped for PadFailed handling; walkthrough 71 read only through project-facts.

#### PAD-01 — Daemon and enrollment-cli hardcode live_class_index = 2 (upstream ScreenReplay class), contradicting the project default (1) and upstream MiniFASNet convention

GitHub issue: #146

**CRITICAL** · CONFIRMED · security · effort S

- **Locations**: `crates/daemon/src/pipeline.rs:189-193`, `crates/enrollment-cli/src/service.rs:773`, `crates/inference-ort/src/pad.rs:81-82`, `crates/inference-ort/src/pad.rs:145-168`, `crates/gui/src/main.rs:288`
- **Evidence**: crates/daemon/src/pipeline.rs:189-193: `OrtPadDetector::new_with_class_index(pad_session, config.vision.pad_threshold, 2)`. crates/enrollment-cli/src/service.rs:773: `OrtPadDetector::new_with_class_index(pad_session, 0.80, 2)`. crates/inference-ort/src/pad.rs:82: `pub const DEFAULT_MINIFASNET_LIVE_CLASS_INDEX: usize = 1;` with doc comment "Class 0: PrintPhoto, Class 1: Genuine Live, Class 2: ScreenReplay". GUI uses `OrtPadDetector::new(pad_session, 0.80)` (default = 1). Git history: `git log -S'new_with_class_index' -- crates/daemon/src/pipeline.rs` -> 5659151 (#124, set 1); `git show 084ce5c` (#127, walkthrough 66) changed daemon/enroll/gui to 2; `git show 72469ba -- crates/inference-ort/src/pad.rs` (#135, walkthrough 72 'fix screen replay bypass') only flipped the constant 2->1 and touched daemon/pipeline.rs solely for DECISION_BUDGET_MS (verified with `git show 72469ba -- crates/daemon/src/pipeline.rs`); `git show 94d9ebe -- crates/gui/src/main.rs` (#140) moved only the GUI to the default. Upstream minivision-ai/Silent-Face-Anti-Spoofing test.py (fetched): `label = np.argmax(prediction)` ... `if label == 1: print("Image '{}' is Real Face...")`, i.e. index 1 = live. Walkthrough 72 explicitly states index 2 = ScreenReplay and claims the daemon was fixed; it was not. With index 2 the daemon computes `p_live = probs[2]`, so a frame the model classifies as screen replay passes PAD and a genuine face (class 1) is reported as spoof.
- **Impact**: In the only security-relevant path (PAM -> daemon), the anti-spoof stage is inverted relative to the project's own contract and upstream semantics: screen-replay-classified frames are treated as live, genuine faces as spoof. Whichever interpretation is empirically right on this hardware, the GUI (what the user calibrates with) and the daemon (what authenticates) disagree, so any tuning done in the GUI does not reflect what PAM enforces. This is the most likely root cause of the user's 'spoof recognition is bad' report.
- **Recommendation**: Remove all explicit class-index overrides in production construction sites; construct with `OrtPadDetector::new` everywhere so `DEFAULT_MINIFASNET_LIVE_CLASS_INDEX` is the single source of truth. Add a daemon test (`pipeline_init_tests`) asserting `initialize_pipeline(...).vision.pad()` was built with `live_class_index() == DEFAULT_MINIFASNET_LIVE_CLASS_INDEX` (expose via a getter on VisionPipeline or a builder hook), plus an invariant test in tests/invariants/src/lib.rs rejecting `new_with_class_index(` / `with_live_class_index(` outside tests. Then settle the empirical truth once with the real model on real captures (see PAD-06) and record it in an ADR.
- **Verifier notes**: Re-opened crates/daemon/src/pipeline.rs:189-193 (`OrtPadDetector::new_with_class_index(pad_session, config.vision.pad_threshold, 2)`), crates/enrollment-cli/src/service.rs:773 (`new_with_class_index(pad_session, 0.80, 2)`), crates/inference-ort/src/pad.rs:81-82 (`DEFAULT_MINIFASNET_LIVE_CLASS_INDEX: usize = 1`, doc says class 2 = ScreenReplay). `grep -rn 'new_with_class_index|OrtPadDetector::new' crates --include='*.rs'` shows exactly these three production sites; the GUI site is crates/gui/src/main.rs:92 (`OrtPadDetector::new(pad_session, 0.80)`), NOT main.rs:288 as cited. Git verified: `git show 084ce5c` flipped daemon+CLI 1->2; `git show 72469ba -- crates/daemon/src/pipeline.rs` only touched DECISION_BUDGET_MS (220->900) and pad.rs only flipped the constant 2->1; AI/walkthroughs/75_...md:25 explicitly states the GUI was moved to 1 because 2 was wrong, yet daemon and CLI still carry 2. Walkthrough 72:43 claims the daemon path was verified, which cannot be true for the hardcoded 2. Caveat: the empirical class order of the shipped ONNX (source_url in models/manifest.toml:44 is the QingHeYang fork, and AI/DECISIONS.md:20 claims that fork uses [Live, Print, Replay]) is unproven either way, but the daemon/GUI disagreement alone means one of the two security-relevant/calibration paths is inverted. Severity CRITICAL stands.

#### PAD-02 — Multi-frame loop is 'first passing frame wins': PAD is never aggregated, giving an attacker dozens of independent trials per request

GitHub issue: #147

**CRITICAL** · CONFIRMED · security · effort M

- **Locations**: `crates/daemon/src/dispatcher.rs:627-779`, `crates/daemon/src/dispatcher.rs:683`, `crates/daemon/src/dispatcher.rs:750-770`, `crates/daemon/src/dispatcher.rs:785-786`
- **Evidence**: dispatcher.rs:628 `while auth_start.elapsed() < total_budget { ... }`; each fresh frame is evaluated independently (line 668-732), `(sim, 1u8, output.pad_result.is_live)` at 683, `engine.evaluate(&ctx)` at 743, and `if verdict == Verdict::Allow { ... return }` at 750-770. A `PadFailed` frame (685-692) only sets `last_ctx` and the loop continues (`tokio::time::sleep(25ms)` at 778). No counter, no consensus, no veto. The rate limiter records a single attempt per request (757 or 786) regardless of how many frames were evaluated. With total_budget up to `connection_timeout - 50ms` (GDM uses timeout_ms=2500 per walkthrough 73) and ~30-60 ms per frame, one request yields ~15-40 independent PAD trials.
- **Impact**: Per-request attack presentation acceptance rate becomes 1-(1-p)^N where p is the per-frame false-live probability of MiniFASNet (non-negligible for glossy prints/high-DPI screens, and for IR input, see PAD-03). A single flickering 'live' frame out of N grants PAM_SUCCESS. Rate limiting (5 attempts/window) does not bound this because it counts requests, not frames.
- **Recommendation**: Introduce a `PadAggregator` (zero-I/O, in soos-policy or soos-vision): sliding window over the last n evaluated frames; Allow only if (a) >= k consecutive frames (k=3 at n=5 suggested) are live with mean p_live >= pad_threshold and cosine >= match_threshold, and (b) NO frame in the window was classified spoof (spoof veto). Expose per-frame PAD score in the context (AuthContext gets `pad_score: f32`, `pad_live_frames`, `pad_spoof_frames`). Tests: dispatcher integration test where MockPadDetector alternates spoof/live per frame must yield Deny/PadFailed; test where 3 consecutive live frames yield Allow; property test that a window containing any spoof never Allows.
- **Verifier notes**: Re-read crates/daemon/src/dispatcher.rs:627-780: `while auth_start.elapsed() < total_budget` (628), per-frame `process_frame` (668-732), `(sim, 1u8, output.pad_result.is_live)` (683), PadFailed arm only `debug!` + `(0.0, 1u8, false)` (685-692), `engine.evaluate(&ctx)` (743), `if verdict == Verdict::Allow { ... return }` (750-770), `sleep(25ms)` (778); rate limiter records once at 757 or 786. No counter/consensus/veto exists; `grep -n 'spoof|PadFailed|altern' crates/daemon/tests/pipeline_integration_tests.rs` shows only test_15 (single spoof frame) and test_48 (no-face then allow), nothing that exercises spoof-then-live sequences. Two corrections: (a) an Allow also requires cosine >= match_threshold (decision.rs:121-125), so the per-frame success probability is P(PAD false-live AND face match), which the finding does not state; (b) the '15-40 trials' estimate is overstated: each frame runs SCRFD 640x640 + PAD + the embedding model, and the installed arcface_w600k_mbf.onnx is 136,619,444 bytes (sha256 ffe014... matches manifest), not the ~3.6 MB MobileFaceNet described in AI/ARCHITECTURE.md:189, so realistic N is roughly 5-15 within 900-2450 ms. The amplification defect itself holds.

#### PAD-03 — IR/Grey frames are fed to MiniFASNetV2 as replicated 3-channel grey; model is RGB-trained and the default sensor preference is PreferIr

GitHub issue: #169

**MAJOR** · CONFIRMED · design · effort L

- **Locations**: `crates/camera-v4l/src/config.rs:45`, `crates/vision/src/color.rs:49-63`, `crates/vision/src/pipeline.rs:282-334`, `crates/camera-v4l/src/sensor.rs:48-83`
- **Evidence**: camera-v4l/src/config.rs:45 `sensor_preference: SensorPreference::PreferIr`. color.rs:49-63 `PixelFormat::Grey => { for &grey in raw_buffer { rgb.push(grey); rgb.push(grey); rgb.push(grey); } }`. pipeline.rs then crops and calls `self.pad.evaluate_liveness(...)` with no format awareness; pad.rs has no notion of the source format. Upstream README (fetched) states all test images must come from a (colour) camera; anti_spoof_predict.py uses cv2 BGR frames. `grep -rn -i emitter crates Docs AI scripts` -> no hits (no IR emitter handling/documentation). Walkthrough 66 observed a live face scored `[0.0003, 0.0054, 0.9943]` (class 2) and walkthrough 72 observed screen replays scored class 2: mutually contradictory readings are consistent with out-of-distribution input.
- **Impact**: On every dual-sensor laptop (the user's Asus G14 per walkthrough 72) the daemon auto-selects the IR node, so PAD runs on data the model was never trained on: scores are essentially uncalibrated, which explains both false-live on screens and false-spoof on real faces. No test or doc acknowledges this.
- **Recommendation**: Make the pipeline format-aware: pass `frame.format` (or `is_monochrome`) down to PAD. Short term: when the frame is Grey, apply an IR-specific liveness policy instead of blind MiniFASNet scoring (NIR physics: LCD/OLED screens emit almost no 850/940 nm light so a replay on IR appears as a dark/flat rectangle; prints lack specular skin response), e.g. contrast/texture gate + MiniFASNet with an IR-calibrated threshold learned from captures. Medium term: run PAD on the RGB sibling sensor while matching on IR, or ship an IR-trained PAD model. Document IR-emitter requirements. Tests: fixture Grey frame through `process_frame` must not silently take the RGB path; calibration fixtures per format (PAD-06).
- **Verifier notes**: crates/camera-v4l/src/config.rs:45 `sensor_preference: SensorPreference::PreferIr`; crates/vision/src/color.rs:49-63 replicates each grey byte into R,G,B; crates/vision/src/pipeline.rs:282-334 uses `frame.format` only inside `convert_to_rgb` and passes the crop to `evaluate_liveness` with no format flag; `grep -rn -i emitter crates Docs AI scripts` -> no hits; crates/camera-v4l/src/sensor.rs:48-83 classifies Grey-only or 'IR'-named nodes as Infrared. The design fact (RGB-trained MiniFASNet fed replicated-grey NIR frames with no acknowledgement, calibration or emitter handling) is verified. The causal claim that this 'explains both false-live on screens and false-spoof on real faces' is speculation, not evidence; walkthrough 72:43 reports the IR path working on live faces. Keep MAJOR as an unmitigated domain-shift risk on the default sensor, not as a demonstrated misbehavior.

#### PAD-04 — pad_threshold from daemon.toml bypasses validation: pad_threshold = 0.0 silently disables anti-spoofing

GitHub issue: #170

**MAJOR** · CONFIRMED · security · effort S

- **Locations**: `crates/daemon/src/config.rs:502-511`, `crates/policy/src/threshold.rs:207-214`, `crates/inference-ort/src/pad.rs:148`
- **Evidence**: config.rs:507-510: `config.pipeline.thresholds = soos_policy::ThresholdConfig::new_raw(match_thresh, pad_thresh); ... config.pipeline.vision.pad_threshold = pad_thresh;` — `new_raw` is documented 'without validation (internal/testing)'. The validating `ThresholdConfigBuilder::build` (threshold.rs:287-294) is never used by the daemon. pad.rs:148 `if p_live >= liveness_threshold { Ok(PadResult::live(p_live)) }` so with 0.0 (or any negative) every frame is `is_live = true` and `VisionPipeline::process_frame` (pipeline.rs:336) also passes because `score < 0.0` is never true.
- **Impact**: A typo or a 'make it work' edit in /etc/soos/daemon.toml (e.g. `pad_threshold = 0`) turns PAD off with no warning; the same applies to `match_threshold`. Fail-open by configuration.
- **Recommendation**: Route TOML thresholds through `ThresholdConfigBuilder::build()` and enforce a security floor (refuse `pad_threshold < 0.5` and `match_threshold < 0.4`, or require an explicit `unsafe_thresholds = true` flag) with a startup error. Tests in crates/daemon/tests/config_tests.rs: `pad_threshold = 0.0`, `-1.0`, `NaN`, `1.5` must return `DaemonError::Config`.
- **Verifier notes**: All cited line numbers are wrong (config.rs is 347 lines, threshold.rs is 139 lines). Actual: crates/daemon/src/config.rs:291-300 (`ThresholdConfig::new_raw(match_thresh, pad_thresh)` at 298, `vision.pad_threshold = pad_thresh` at 300); crates/policy/src/threshold.rs:26-33 `new_raw` documented 'without validation (internal/testing)', `build` at 106-113, `validate_threshold` at 116-138 which only rejects NaN/inf/outside [0,1]. `grep -rn new_raw crates` outside policy -> only config.rs:298; the builder is never used by the daemon. pad.rs:148 `if p_live >= liveness_threshold` and pipeline.rs:336 `pad_result.score < self.config.pad_threshold` confirm 0.0 (and negative values) accept every frame. Important correction to the recommendation: routing through `ThresholdConfigBuilder::build` alone does NOT fix the issue because 0.0 is inside [0,1]; a daemon-level floor is the actual fix. Existing crates/daemon/tests/config_tests.rs:54-55 uses 0.75/0.90, compatible with the proposed floors.

#### PAD-05 — Class-order contract is stated four different ways across ADRs, docs, matrix, walkthroughs and code

GitHub issue: #171

**MAJOR** · CONFIRMED · doc-drift · effort S

- **Locations**: `AI/DECISIONS.md:20`, `AI/ARCHITECTURE.md:188`, `Docs/INFERENCE_ORT_CRATE.md:12`, `Docs/INFERENCE_ORT_CRATE.md:114`, `AI/VERIFICATION_MATRIX.md:187`, `AI/VERIFICATION_MATRIX.md:318`, `AI/VERIFICATION_MATRIX.md:393`, `AI/walkthroughs/66_fix_windowed_layout_and_pad_class_index.md`, `AI/walkthroughs/72_anti_spoof_ir_stability_gdm.md`
- **Evidence**: DECISIONS.md:20 and ARCHITECTURE.md:188: `[Live, Print, Replay]`, index 0 = Live. Docs/INFERENCE_ORT_CRATE.md:12/114 and VERIFICATION_MATRIX PAD1 (l.187) / NGM9 (l.318): default 0. VERIFICATION_MATRIX ASG1 (l.393): index 1, citing tests `pad_tests::test_minifasnet_live_class_index_is_1` and `test_minifasnet_rejects_screen_replay_spoof` which do not exist (actual names: `test_pad_default_live_class_index_is_one`, `test_screen_replay_detected_as_spoof_with_default_index`, pad_tests.rs:298-345). Walkthrough 66: index 2 = Live. Code default: 1. Daemon: 2. Docs/VISION_CRATE.md:107-112 still describes `expand_bbox_for_pad` as 'clamps coordinates' although crop.rs:46-65 implements the Minivision shifting algorithm.
- **Impact**: Each successive agent 'fixed' the class index against a different document, which is exactly how PAD-01 was introduced and never caught. Any future reviewer cannot tell which value is correct.
- **Recommendation**: Write one superseding ADR in AI/DECISIONS.md ('MiniFASNetV2 class order = [Print, Live, Replay], live index 1, verified with real captures on <date>') and update ARCHITECTURE.md:188, Docs/INFERENCE_ORT_CRATE.md, VERIFICATION_MATRIX PAD1/NGM9/ASG1 (fix test names), Docs/VISION_CRATE.md §2.6. Add an invariant test that greps these files for `live class index` and asserts agreement with `DEFAULT_MINIFASNET_LIVE_CLASS_INDEX`.
- **Verifier notes**: Verified every citation: AI/DECISIONS.md:20 and AI/ARCHITECTURE.md:188 say index 0 = Live; Docs/INFERENCE_ORT_CRATE.md:12, :114 and additionally :147 say default 0; AI/VERIFICATION_MATRIX.md:187 (PAD1) and :318 (NGM9) say 0; :393 (ASG1) says 1 and cites `pad_tests::test_minifasnet_live_class_index_is_1` / `test_minifasnet_rejects_screen_replay_spoof` which do not exist (`grep -rn` -> only the matrix line); actual tests are crates/inference-ort/tests/pad_tests.rs:298 `test_pad_default_live_class_index_is_one` and :306 `test_screen_replay_detected_as_spoof_with_default_index`. Matrix :429 (GARP3) also says 1. Code default is 1 (pad.rs:82), daemon/CLI use 2. Docs/VISION_CRATE.md:107-109 still says 'Clamps the resulting coordinates' while crop.rs:46-65 implements the inward-shift algorithm. MAJOR is justified because the matrix presents non-existent tests as verification evidence for a security criterion.

#### PAD-06 — All PAD 'FAR/FRR' and 'APCER/BPCER' evidence is tautological or fabricated; the real ONNX model is never exercised by any test

GitHub issue: #172

**MAJOR** · CONFIRMED · test-gap · effort M

- **Locations**: `crates/vision/tests/pad_tests.rs:184-228`, `tests/physical/adversarial_test.sh:186-208`, `AI/VERIFICATION_MATRIX.md:191`, `AI/VERIFICATION_MATRIX.md:249`, `AI/BACKLOG.md:1297`
- **Evidence**: pad_tests.rs:186/197/207/216: `MockPadDetector::new_live()` then `pad.set_result(PadResult::live(0.95))` / `PadResult::spoof(0.04, ...)` before counting FAR/FRR -> the metric measures the mock. adversarial_test.sh:186-208 in `--mock` mode: `BONA_FIDE_TESTED=10; BONA_FIDE_ACCEPTED=10; ... ATTACKS_REJECTED=$(( ATTACKS_REJECTED + 5 ))` hardcoded, then prints 'PAD SECURITY AUDIT PASSED: 100% of presentation attacks successfully rejected!'. VERIFICATION_MATRIX PAD5 and PH5 marked '✅ Verified' on that basis, while BACKLOG PHY2 'PAD rejects printed photos and screen replays on real hardware' is '⬜ Pending'. `grep -rln OrtPadDetector crates/*/tests` shows no test running the real session; models exist on this host at /var/lib/soos/models (ls verified) but nothing consumes them in CI or locally.
- **Impact**: The project claims 0% FAR/APCER with no real measurement; regressions like PAD-01 (which flips the meaning of the output) are invisible to the entire test suite.
- **Recommendation**: (1) Add a real-model test target gated on model presence (`SOOS_MODELS_DIR` or /var/lib/soos/models, `#[ignore]` otherwise) with a small committed corpus of 80x80 crops captured on the target laptop: >=20 bona fide (RGB and IR), >=20 print, >=20 screen replay; assert argmax class per sample and compute APCER/BPCER with a hard ceiling (e.g. APCER <= 5%, BPCER <= 10% at the shipped threshold). (2) Store the model's raw logits for those crops as golden fixtures so preprocessing changes are caught. (3) Make `adversarial_test.sh --mock` print 'SIMULATION – no security metrics' and never claim PASSED; make the matrix rows cite the real test. (4) Rename `test_pad_far_frr_benchmark` to reflect it tests pipeline plumbing, not the model.
- **Verifier notes**: crates/vision/tests/pad_tests.rs:184-228: `MockPadDetector::new_live()` then `pad.set_result(PadResult::live(0.95))` / `spoof(0.04, PrintPhoto)` / `spoof(0.11, ScreenReplay)` before counting; FAR/FRR are tautological. tests/physical/adversarial_test.sh:170-190 in mock mode runs `cargo test -p soos-vision --test pad_tests` then hardcodes `BONA_FIDE_TESTED=10; BONA_FIDE_ACCEPTED=10` and `ATTACKS_REJECTED=$(( ATTACKS_REJECTED + 5 ))` x3, and lines 321-327 print 'PAD SECURITY AUDIT PASSED' whenever ATTACKS_ACCEPTED==0, i.e. always in mock mode. AI/VERIFICATION_MATRIX.md:191 (PAD5) and :249 (PH5) marked Verified on that basis; AI/BACKLOG.md:1297 PHY2 is 'Pending'. `grep -rln OrtPadDetector crates/*/tests tests` -> only inference-ort pad_tests.rs and zeroize_tests.rs, and those only call the static `softmax`/`prepare_input` helpers (pad_tests.rs:118-156), never a real session. Models present at /var/lib/soos/models with checksums matching manifest (sha256sum verified), and nothing consumes them in tests. Correction to the recommendation: committing real face crops of the user to git is biometric data at rest and conflicts with the project's privacy posture; keep the corpus out of the repository (SOOS_PAD_CORPUS_DIR) and commit only the golden logits/argmax.

#### PAD-07 — Only the 2.7-scale MiniFASNetV2 is used; upstream accuracy figures assume fusion with the 4.0-scale MiniFASNetV1SE

GitHub issue: #212

**MINOR** · PARTIAL · design · effort M · (reviewer severity: MAJOR)

- **Locations**: `models/manifest.toml:38-46`, `crates/vision/src/pipeline.rs:313-341`, `crates/daemon/src/pipeline.rs:181`
- **Evidence**: manifest.toml has a single PAD entry `minifasnet_v2_pad` (2.7_80x80_MiniFASNetV2). Upstream test.py (fetched): `for model_name in os.listdir(model_dir): ... prediction += model_test.predict(img, ...)`, `label = np.argmax(prediction)`, `value = prediction[0][label]/2` — the decision is the mean of two softmaxes over two crops (scale 2.7 for V2 and 4.0 for V1SE via `parse_model_name`). Upstream README's 97.8% TPR @ 1e-5 FPR is for the shipped APK pipeline, not one model.
- **Impact**: Single-model, single-scale scoring is measurably weaker (the two models are complementary: 2.7 sees skin texture, 4.0 sees device/paper borders and moire context). Threshold 0.85 taken from a single model's softmax is not the upstream operating point.
- **Recommendation**: Add `minifasnet_v1se_pad` (4_0_0_80x80_MiniFASNetV1SE.onnx, same license/source) to manifest.toml with SHA-256; extend `VisionPipeline` to build a second 4.0x crop (`expand_bbox_for_pad(scale 4.0)`), run both sessions, average the softmax vectors, and threshold the averaged live probability. Latency: +~8-15 ms CPU (model is ~1.8 MB, 80x80). Tests: manifest test for 4 models, pipeline test asserting two PAD crops with the right scales, fusion unit test (mean of probability vectors).
- **Verifier notes**: Code fact verified: models/manifest.toml:38-46 has a single PAD entry (`minifasnet_v2_pad`, 2.7_80x80_MiniFASNetV2.onnx from the QingHeYang fork); crates/vision/src/pipeline.rs:313-341 runs one crop at `pad_bbox_scale` and one detector; daemon pipeline.rs:181 loads one PAD session. The upstream description (test.py sums the softmax of both 2.7 V2 and 4.0 V1SE models and argmaxes) is consistent with my knowledge of minivision-ai/Silent-Face-Anti-Spoofing. However: (a) 'measurably weaker' is asserted without any measurement on this hardware; (b) the availability of a 4.0 V1SE ONNX export in the QingHeYang fork (the manifest's source) is not verified by the reviewer or by me; (c) upstream's headline accuracy figures are for its dataset/APK, not a claim that single-model inference is broken. This is an improvement opportunity, not a defect; downgrade to MINOR. Also note crates/inference-ort/tests/manifest_tests.rs:118 `test_manifest_v2_model_count_and_checksum_attestation` asserts the model count and would need a coordinated (not weakened) update.

#### PAD-08 — Crop geometry deviates from the upstream reference (detector box source, no half-pixel centre, inclusive bounds)

GitHub issue: #213

**MINOR** · CONFIRMED · bug · effort S

- **Locations**: `crates/vision/src/crop.rs:29-34`, `crates/vision/src/crop.rs:58-65`, `crates/vision/src/crop.rs:135-144`, `crates/inference-ort/src/pad.rs:244-250`
- **Evidence**: crop.rs:29 `let max_x = img_w as f32;` and 58 `if x2 > max_x` whereas upstream `_get_new_box` uses `src_w-1` and `scale = min((src_h-1)/box_h, ...)`. crop.rs:135-144 samples `xs = bbox.x1 + uf * sx` (top-left aligned) while cv2.resize INTER_LINEAR samples at `(u+0.5)*sx-0.5`. pad.rs:244-250 does nearest-neighbour when the buffer is not 80x80 (only relevant if `pad_target_*` config changes). The 2.7 scale was calibrated against the upstream RetinaFace (caffe) boxes; SCRFD boxes are tighter, so the effective context differs.
- **Impact**: Systematic half-pixel/one-pixel shift and slightly different context ratio versus the training distribution; small but it lowers separation margin on a model already operating without fusion.
- **Recommendation**: Add a golden-fixture test: run the upstream Python crop on one sample image+bbox, commit the 80x80 BGR result, assert the Rust crop matches within +/-1 LSB; use `(u+0.5)*sx-0.5` centre sampling and `img_w-1` bounds. Optionally re-fit the scale factor for SCRFD boxes using the calibration corpus from PAD-06.
- **Verifier notes**: crates/vision/src/crop.rs:29-30 `max_x = img_w as f32` and :34 `effective_scale = ... .min(max_x / orig_w)` versus upstream `_get_new_box` using `src_w - 1`; :58-65 inward shift to `max_x` rather than `src_w-1`; :135-144 `sx = bw / target_w`, `xs = bbox.x1 + uf * sx` (top-left aligned) versus cv2 INTER_LINEAR centre convention `(u+0.5)*sx-0.5`. crates/inference-ort/src/pad.rs:243-250 nearest-neighbour fallback `src_x = (x * scale_x) as usize` when the buffer is not 80x80 (unreachable with default config since pad_target_* = 80). Sub-pixel deviations are real but small; MINOR is right. The SCRFD-vs-RetinaFace box-tightness remark is plausible but unmeasured.

#### PAD-09 — No startup validation of PAD output shape or live_class_index; out-of-range index silently yields 100% spoof

GitHub issue: #214

**MINOR** · CONFIRMED · reliability · effort S

- **Locations**: `crates/inference-ort/src/pad.rs:146`, `crates/inference-ort/src/pad.rs:308-319`, `crates/inference-ort/src/registry.rs:83`
- **Evidence**: pad.rs:146 `let p_live = probs.get(live_class_index).copied().unwrap_or(0.0);` — an index >= probs.len() is not an error. evaluate_liveness (308-319) takes the first output tensor and softmaxes whatever length it has. registry.rs `verify_integrity` (83) is checksum-only; manifest `output_shapes = [[1, 3]]` is never compared to the loaded session's metadata.
- **Impact**: A wrong model file with a valid checksum entry, or a mis-set index, produces a silent permanent Deny (or, with a 2-class head and index 1, a wrong mapping) with no log line pointing at PAD.
- **Recommendation**: At `initialize_pipeline`, run a self-test inference on a fixed 80x80 fixture, assert output length == 3 (from manifest `output_shapes`) and `live_class_index < len`, log the class index and threshold at info, fail closed otherwise. Make `interpret_probabilities` return `InferenceError::PadFailed` for out-of-range index. Unit test for the error path.
- **Verifier notes**: crates/inference-ort/src/pad.rs:146 `probs.get(live_class_index).copied().unwrap_or(0.0)` -> out-of-range index silently yields p_live = 0.0 -> spoof on every frame; same at :162 for the 2-class branch. evaluate_liveness (pad.rs:296-322) takes the first output tensor and softmaxes any length. crates/inference-ort/src/registry.rs:82-85 `verify_integrity` only delegates to `manifest.verify_directory` (checksums); manifest `output_shapes = [[1, 3]]` (manifest.toml:46) is never compared to session metadata. Confirmed as stated.

#### PAD-10 — Three different PAD thresholds (0.80 detector, 0.85 pipeline, 0.80 GUI display) and a dead pad_threshold in the policy crate

GitHub issue: #215

**MINOR** · CONFIRMED · design · effort S

- **Locations**: `crates/gui/src/main.rs:288`, `crates/gui/src/app.rs:307`, `crates/gui/src/app.rs:462`, `crates/gui/src/worker.rs:78-84`, `crates/enrollment-cli/src/service.rs:773`, `crates/vision/src/pipeline.rs:44`, `crates/vision/src/pipeline.rs:336`, `crates/policy/src/decision.rs:135-153`
- **Evidence**: GUI main.rs:288 `OrtPadDetector::new(pad_session, 0.80)` but `VisionPipelineConfig::default()` pad_threshold = 0.85 (pipeline.rs:44); worker.rs:82 uses `pipeline.config().pad_threshold` (0.85) for enrollment gating, app.rs:307 and 462 hardcode `p.score >= 0.80` for the on-screen LIVE/SPOOF label. enrollment-cli service.rs:773 uses 0.80 while its pipeline config uses 0.85. `grep -rn pad_threshold crates/policy/src/decision.rs` -> no hits: `AuthorizationEngine::evaluate` never reads `ThresholdConfig::pad_threshold`; `AuthContext` carries only `pad_passed: bool`.
- **Impact**: The GUI can display 'LIVE ✓ (0.82)' for a frame the daemon rejects, confusing calibration; the threshold is applied twice with different values (the lower one is dead); policy cannot enforce or audit PAD scores.
