#### ONB-01 — Package builders bake a shared /var/lib/soos/master.key into every .deb and Arch package

GitHub issue: #144

**CRITICAL** · CONFIRMED · security · effort S

- **Locations**: `scripts/install.sh:234-250`, `scripts/build_deb.sh:100-104`, `scripts/build_arch.sh:97-101`, `packaging/debian/rules:14-19`, `packaging/debian/postinst:40-51`
- **Evidence**: install.sh step 3 generates the key whenever `${TARGET_STATE_DIR}/master.key` is absent, with no `DESTDIR` guard (lines 235-247). build_deb.sh, build_arch.sh and debian/rules all stage the package tree by calling `install.sh --destdir <stage> --skip-models --skip-systemd`. Repro (scratchpad): `bash scripts/install.sh --destdir $SP/stage --skip-models --skip-systemd` → `ls -la $SP/stage/var/lib/soos` shows `-rw------- 32 master.key`; `find $SP/stage -type f` lists `./var/lib/soos/master.key` next to the binaries. dpkg/pacman unpack that file to /var/lib/soos/master.key, then postinst line 41 (`if [ ! -f "$MASTER_KEY" ]`) and soos.install line 33 skip generation because the file already exists. The RPM path is unaffected (spec uses %ghost and generates in %post).
- **Impact**: Every machine installed from the same artifact shares the AES-256-GCM master key that protects all biometric templates; the key is also shipped inside a distributable/publishable package, so anyone who obtains the package can decrypt any `<uid>.cbor.enc` copied from any host. This breaks the ARCHITECTURE §9 'encrypted at rest, root-only key' guarantee.
- **Recommendation**: In install.sh, skip key generation entirely when `DESTDIR` is non-empty (packaging/staging mode) and let postinst/%post/post_install generate it on the target host (they already do). Add hard assertions in build_deb.sh/build_arch.sh (`[[ ! -e "$STAGE_DIR/var/lib/soos/master.key" ]]`) and an invariant test that runs `install.sh --destdir` and asserts master.key is absent (the current `test_install_script_creates_required_directories` asserts the opposite at tests/invariants/src/lib.rs:~720 and must be corrected as part of the spec change, with a backlog acceptance line). Also add a Docker package test asserting the installed key differs across two fresh installs.
- **Verifier notes**: Re-read scripts/install.sh:234-250: key generation is gated only on `[[ ! -f "${MASTER_KEY_FILE}" ]]` with no DESTDIR guard; build_deb.sh:100-104, build_arch.sh:97-101 and packaging/debian/rules:14-19 all stage via `install.sh --destdir`. Repro: `bash scripts/install.sh --destdir <scratch>/stage --skip-models --skip-systemd` -> exit 0, `find stage -type f` lists `./var/lib/soos/master.key` (32 bytes, 0600) next to the binaries. packaging/debian/postinst:41 and packaging/arch/soos.install:33 skip generation when the file exists, so the shipped key is the live key. RPM (soos.spec:110 %ghost, %post generates) and the hand-written PKGBUILD package() are unaffected; build_arch.sh is affected. Two additional consequences the finding omitted: the key is a normal package-owned file (not a conffile), so dpkg/pacman OVERWRITE it on every package upgrade (all previously enrolled `<uid>.cbor.enc` become undecryptable) and `dpkg -r` deletes it, contradicting packaging/debian/postrm:11 ('Retain biometric vector store'). tests/invariants/src/lib.rs:720-754 asserts master.key IS created under --destdir, so the fix is a spec change requiring a backlog line, as the finding says.

#### ONB-02 — Fedora authselect profile cannot be activated as documented; when activated it wipes nsswitch.conf and silently drops pam_faillock

GitHub issue: #145

**CRITICAL** · PARTIAL · bug · effort M · (reviewer severity: MAJOR)

- **Locations**: `packaging/pam/fedora/soos/README:1-9`, `packaging/pam/fedora/soos/system-auth:7,16`, `Docs/DISTRIBUTION_DEPLOYMENT.md:138`, `scripts/install.sh:337-343`, `tests/distro/fedora_rhel_test.sh:254`
- **Evidence**: In `fedora:40` with `authselect` installed and the profile copied to /etc/authselect/custom/soos: `authselect select custom/soos with-faillock --force` → `[error] Unknown profile feature [with-faillock]` / `Unable to activate profile [custom/soos] [22]: Invalid argument` (rc=1). The README does not declare any feature, so the `{?with-faillock:...}` lines (system-auth:7,16; password-auth:7,16) are unreachable. `authselect select custom/soos --force` succeeds, but the profile ships no nsswitch.conf/fingerprint-auth/smartcard-auth/postlogin templates: `grep -v '^#' /etc/nsswitch.conf` is EMPTY afterwards (default `local` profile yields passwd/group/shadow/hosts/... lines), and the generated system-auth contains no pam_faillock line. fedora_rhel_test.sh line 254 runs `authselect check || true`, which cannot catch this; the test only greps the template text for 'pam_faillock' (lines 236-249).
- **Impact**: Documented Fedora/RHEL activation fails; if an operator drops `with-faillock`, the brute-force lockout policy is silently removed and /etc/nsswitch.conf loses every explicit source (sssd, systemd, resolve, myhostname, mdns), which can break domain users and hostname resolution on the host. Auditor checklist item 8 ('PAM stack edits keep pam_faillock preauth ordering') is violated.
- **Recommendation**: Ship a complete profile: generate it with `authselect create-profile soos --base-on local --symlink-meta --symlink-nsswitch --symlink-pam` style layout (or copy all templates from the `local` profile of the target release) and insert only the two pam_soos lines; declare `with-faillock` (and the other inherited features) in README under the feature list; have install/bootstrap save `authselect current --raw` before switching and restore it on uninstall. Test: Docker Fedora job asserting `authselect select custom/soos with-faillock --force` rc=0, `authselect check` rc=0, generated system-auth contains `pam_faillock.so preauth` before `pam_soos.so` and `pam_faillock.so authfail` after `pam_unix.so`, and `/etc/nsswitch.conf` has non-comment lines equal to the base profile.
- **Verifier notes**: Core claims hold and the evidence is UNDERSTATED. Reproduced in fedora:40 (authselect installed, profile copied to /etc/authselect/custom/soos): `authselect select custom/soos with-faillock --force` -> `[error] Unknown profile feature [with-faillock]` rc=1; `authselect select custom/soos --force` rc=0, `authselect check` says 'Current configuration is valid', and `grep -v '^#' /etc/nsswitch.conf` is empty afterwards (16 non-comment lines before). CORRECTION to the evidence: the generated /etc/pam.d/system-auth does NOT 'contain no pam_faillock line' — the `{?with-faillock:...}` lines (system-auth:7,16,24; password-auth same) are emitted LITERALLY because `{?feature:...}` is not authselect syntax (the base profile at /usr/share/authselect/default/local/system-auth:3,9,14 uses a trailing `{include if "with-faillock"}`). Linux-PAM treats `{?with-faillock:auth` as an illegal module type and installs a must-fail handler under `required`, so ALL password authentication through system-auth fails: with a test user, `echo password123 | pamtester system-auth testuser authenticate` -> 'successfully authenticated' on the stock `local` profile, but 'Permission denied' rc=1 after selecting custom/soos (correct password). Isolation: deleting the three `{?with-faillock:` lines and re-selecting -> authentication succeeds, wrong password -> 'Authentication failure'; rewriting them as `... {include if "with-faillock"}` and appending `with-faillock::` to the profile README -> `authselect select custom/soos with-faillock --force` rc=0, 3 pam_faillock lines generated, correct password succeeds. So the documented Fedora activation (Docs/PACKAGING_AND_PROVISIONING.md:84 uses the plain `authselect select custom/soos`) locks every account out of sudo/login/gdm on Fedora/RHEL. tests/distro/fedora_rhel_test.sh:236-249 only greps the template text and :254 runs `authselect check || true`, so nothing catches it. Severity raised to CRITICAL (denial of authentication after following the docs). Recommendation must add: replace the `{?feature:...}` syntax with `{include if "with-faillock"}` and declare the feature in README; the rest of the recommendation (complete profile with nsswitch.conf etc., save/restore `authselect current`) stands.

#### ONB-03 — Debian pam-auth-update profile places the password-failed hook after pam_deny, so the intrusion event can never fire

GitHub issue: #161

**MAJOR** · CONFIRMED · bug · effort S

- **Locations**: `packaging/pam/debian/soos-notify:5-9`, `packaging/debian/postinst:54-56`, `Docs/PACKAGING_AND_PROVISIONING.md:78`, `Dockerfile:98-102`
- **Evidence**: `soos-notify` is declared `Auth-Type: Additional` (line 5). In `ubuntu:24.04`, after `pam-auth-update --package --enable soos soos-notify` (rc=0) the generated /etc/pam.d/common-auth is: `auth [success=done default=ignore] pam_soos.so timeout_ms=250` / `auth [success=1 default=ignore] pam_unix.so nullok try_first_pass` / `auth requisite pam_deny.so` / `auth required pam_permit.so` / `auth optional pam_soos.so event=password-failed timeout_ms=20`. Because `requisite pam_deny.so` terminates the stack on password failure, the Additional block is only reached on success. The project's own Dockerfile common-auth (lines 98-102) omits the notify line entirely, so no test covers it.
- **Impact**: The ARCHITECTURE §5 'reached only if pam_unix fails' event (evidence snapshot on wrong password) never happens on Debian/Ubuntu; the feature is dead on the most common target while docs claim it works.
- **Recommendation**: Make `soos-notify` a `Primary` profile with `Priority: 250` (below unix=256) and control `[default=ignore]` so it sits between pam_unix and pam_deny; keep `success=done` on the primary soos line. Add a Docker test that runs pam-auth-update and asserts the notify line index is smaller than the pam_deny index, and a mock-daemon test asserting a PasswordFailed event is received after a wrong password.
- **Verifier notes**: packaging/pam/debian/soos-notify:5 is `Auth-Type: Additional`. Reproduced in ubuntu:24.04: copied both profiles to /usr/share/pam-configs, `pam-auth-update --package --enable soos soos-notify` rc=0; non-comment /etc/pam.d/common-auth is exactly: (2) `[success=done default=ignore] pam_soos.so timeout_ms=250`, (3) `[success=1 default=ignore] pam_unix.so nullok try_first_pass`, (4) `requisite pam_deny.so`, (5) `required pam_permit.so`, (6) `optional pam_soos.so event=password-failed timeout_ms=20`. On password failure pam_unix falls through to `requisite pam_deny.so`, which terminates the stack before line 6, so the event is only reached on success. Dockerfile:98-102 and tests/docker/Dockerfile.ubuntu:44-48 omit the notify line, and tests/invariants/src/lib.rs:593-607 (`test_pam_config_ordering_matches_spec`) only asserts the template text contains the event line, not its generated position.

#### ONB-04 — Fedora and Arch sandbox images write single-line PAM files (literal \n): the multi-distro PAM matrix cannot pass and is not run in CI

GitHub issue: #162

**MAJOR** · CONFIRMED · test-gap · effort S

- **Locations**: `tests/docker/Dockerfile.fedora:37-49`, `tests/docker/Dockerfile.arch:32-43`, `.github/workflows/ci.yml:183-211`, `tests/docker/run_matrix.sh:51-58`
- **Evidence**: Both Dockerfiles use `RUN echo "...\n..." > /etc/pam.d/test-soos`. Docker runs `RUN` through `/bin/sh -c`; on fedora:40 and archlinux:latest /bin/sh is bash, whose builtin echo does not interpret `\n`. Verified: `docker run --rm fedora:40 sh -c 'echo "line1\nline2" > /tmp/x; wc -l /tmp/x; cat /tmp/x'` → `1 /tmp/x` / `line1\nline2` (same on archlinux:latest). The resulting test-soos/system-auth files are one comment line, so pam_test_runner T1 would fail with no modules. ci.yml builds only the root Dockerfile (ubuntu) and runs test_suite.sh once; run_matrix.sh fedora/arch is never invoked by CI.
- **Impact**: Fedora/RHEL and Arch PAM integration (T1-T9) is unverified despite VERIFICATION_MATRIX rows marking them verified; regressions in the RPM/Arch PAM paths would ship silently.
- **Recommendation**: Replace `echo` with heredocs (`COPY <<EOF /etc/pam.d/test-soos` with BuildKit, or `printf '%s\n'`), and add fedora/arch to the CI matrix (at least on push to main / nightly to limit cost). Test: run_matrix.sh all must pass; add a Dockerfile lint step (`grep -n 'RUN echo ".*\\n' tests/docker/Dockerfile.*` must be empty).
- **Verifier notes**: tests/docker/Dockerfile.fedora:37-49 and Dockerfile.arch:32-43 use `RUN echo "...\n..." > /etc/pam.d/...`. Verified: `docker run --rm fedora:40 sh -c 'ls -l /bin/sh; echo "line1\nline2" > /tmp/x; wc -l < /tmp/x'` -> `/bin/sh -> bash`, `1`, content `line1\nline2`; identical on archlinux:latest. ubuntu:24.04 has `/bin/sh -> dash` and produces 2 lines, so the root Dockerfile and Dockerfile.ubuntu are fine. .github/workflows/ci.yml:183-211 builds only the root (Ubuntu) Dockerfile and runs test_suite.sh once; grep for run_matrix/fedora/arch in ci.yml returns nothing, so the fedora/arch images are never exercised in CI.

#### ONB-05 — run_distro_validation.sh invokes non-existent scripts in Docker and runs live root tests as its 'dry-run' fallback

GitHub issue: #163

**MAJOR** · CONFIRMED · bug · effort S

- **Locations**: `tests/distro/run_distro_validation.sh:186-190`, `tests/distro/run_distro_validation.sh:204`, `tests/distro/debian_ubuntu_test.sh:137-142,159-177,310-320`
- **Evidence**: Line 204: `bash "/workspace/tests/distro/${distro}_test.sh"` → `ubuntu_test.sh`, `fedora_test.sh`, `arch_test.sh`; `ls tests/distro` shows only `debian_ubuntu_test.sh`, `fedora_rhel_test.sh`, `arch_linux_test.sh`. Lines 186-189: when docker is missing it prints 'falling back to dry-run verification' but calls `run_local_test` WITHOUT `--dry-run`, which (as root) executes dpkg -i / install.sh / pam-auth-update / useradd on the host. debian_ubuntu_test.sh installs via dpkg when INSTALL_MODE=auto (159-166) but the rollback branch tests `INSTALL_MODE = "deb"` (310), so auto mode removes files with uninstall.sh and leaves dpkg's database believing soos is installed; the EXIT trap (140) deletes /run/soos/daemon.sock on the host.
- **Impact**: The Dockerized distro validation (DV4) is unrunnable; the fallback can modify a developer's real PAM stack; VERIFICATION_MATRIX DV1-DV4 are not backed by an executable path.
- **Recommendation**: Map distro→script name explicitly; in the no-docker branch append `--dry-run` or abort; set INSTALL_MODE to the mode actually used; guard host mutations behind an explicit `--i-know-this-modifies-the-host` flag. Test: `bash tests/distro/run_distro_validation.sh all` inside CI (Docker) must reach the end; a unit test in tests/invariants asserting each `${distro}_test.sh` path referenced by the runner exists.
- **Verifier notes**: tests/distro/run_distro_validation.sh:204 runs `bash "/workspace/tests/distro/${distro}_test.sh"` with distro in {ubuntu,fedora,arch}; `ls tests/distro` shows only arch_linux_test.sh, debian_ubuntu_test.sh, fedora_rhel_test.sh, run_distro_validation.sh. Lines 186-189: when docker is absent it prints 'falling back to dry-run verification' and calls `run_local_test`, which (lines 158-166) only appends --dry-run if the global DRY_RUN was set, so a live root run happens. debian_ubuntu_test.sh:132-135 requires root, :137-142 EXIT trap does `rm -f /run/soos/daemon.sock` on the host, :159-166 installs via `dpkg -i` in auto mode without setting INSTALL_MODE=deb, and :310 only takes the `dpkg -r` path when INSTALL_MODE = "deb", so auto mode removes files via uninstall.sh while dpkg's database still lists soos.

#### ONB-06 — install.sh exits 0 with missing artifacts, silently installs debug builds, never builds, and has no root check for live installs

GitHub issue: #164

**MAJOR** · CONFIRMED · reliability · effort S

- **Locations**: `scripts/install.sh:254-308`, `scripts/install.sh:186-231`, `scripts/build_deb.sh:88-104`, `scripts/build_arch.sh:85-101`
- **Evidence**: find_artifact (254-267) searches target/release then target/debug; each missing artifact only `warn`s ('not built yet. Skipped.', lines 275,283,291,299,307) and the script still prints 'completed successfully' and exits 0 (370). Repro: staging into the scratchpad produced a tree with soos-daemon/soos-admin/soos-enroll/soos-gui (found in target/debug of this worktree) and NO pam_soos.so, exit code 0. build_deb.sh --skip-build (used by test_packages.sh:90 and debian_ubuntu_test.sh:162) would therefore package a .deb without the PAM module or with unoptimized debug binaries. There is no `id -u` check when DESTDIR is empty: a non-root run fails part-way at `mkdir -p /var/lib/soos/...` under `set -e` with no cleanup, and `chmod 0755 /usr/bin` / `chmod 0755 <pam dir>` are applied to system directories on every run.
- **Impact**: Users can end up with a half-installed system (unit enabled, PAM profiles installed, module missing) or a production PAM module compiled with debug profile; packages built from stale trees are silently incomplete.
- **Recommendation**: Fail closed: require every artifact unless `--allow-missing` is passed; add `--artifact-dir` (default target/release only, never debug unless `--profile debug`); optionally `--build` that runs `cargo build --release --locked --workspace`; require root when DESTDIR is empty; drop chmod on pre-existing system dirs. Tests: invariant test that `install.sh --destdir` with an empty artifact dir exits non-zero; build_deb.sh test asserting `dpkg-deb -c` lists `pam_soos.so`.
- **Verifier notes**: scripts/install.sh:254-267 find_artifact searches target/release then target/debug; :270-308 each miss only `warn`s; :361-370 unconditionally prints 'completed successfully' and `exit 0`. Repro in scratchpad: this worktree has no target/release, target/debug has soos-{admin,daemon,enroll,gui} and no libpam_soos.so; install.sh --destdir staged the four DEBUG binaries, logged `[WARN] libpam_soos.so artifact not built yet. Skipped.` and exited 0. build_deb.sh:88-104 / build_arch.sh:85-101 with --skip-build (used by tests/docker/test_packages.sh:90,115,136 and tests/distro/debian_ubuntu_test.sh:162) would package that tree; test_packages.sh:55 checks pam_soos.so only after installation and is not run in CI. No `id -u` check exists for DESTDIR="" (only chown is root-gated at :225,:244); `chmod 0755` is applied to TARGET_BIN_DIR/TARGET_PAM_DIR at :222-223 on every run.

#### ONB-07 — Build/runtime dependency lists are incomplete: OpenSSL headers, libclang, python3>=3.11, curl and GUI runtime libs are undeclared; ort downloads binaries at build time

GitHub issue: #165

**MAJOR** · CONFIRMED · doc-drift · effort M

- **Locations**: `Cargo.toml:45`, `crates/gui/Cargo.toml:26`, `packaging/debian/control:5,12`, `packaging/rpm/soos.spec:10-20`, `packaging/arch/PKGBUILD:13-14`, `tests/docker/Dockerfile.ubuntu:13-23`, `Dockerfile:41-51`, `scripts/build_deb.sh:119`
- **Evidence**: `cargo tree --locked -i openssl-sys` → openssl-sys 0.9.117 ← native-tls ← ureq (build-dependency of ort-sys 2.0.0-rc.13 ← ort ← soos-inference-ort ← daemon/enrollment-cli/gui/vision); `grep -c 'name = "openssl-src"' Cargo.lock` = 0, so openssl-sys is NOT vendored and needs libssl-dev/openssl-devel + pkg-config at build time. `cargo tree -i bindgen` → bindgen 0.65.1 is a build-dependency of v4l2-sys-mit (needs libclang at build time). ort-sys' build script (ureq) fetches ONNX Runtime binaries from the network during `cargo build` (no offline path documented). GUI: glutin enables `wayland-sys dlopen/egl`, `x11-dl`, glutin_egl_sys/glx_sys → libwayland-client, libwayland-egl, libxkbcommon, libEGL/libGL, libX11/libXcursor/libXi/libXrandr are dlopen'ed at runtime and are invisible to dpkg-shlibdeps. None of libssl-dev/openssl-devel/openssl appear in Dockerfile, Dockerfile.ubuntu/fedora, debian/control Build-Depends, soos.spec BuildRequires or PKGBUILD makedepends; runtime `Depends` in control:12 and build_deb.sh:119 omit python3 and curl although install.sh/postinst-era docs rely on download_models.sh (python3 tomllib, curl). ARCHITECTURE/Docs contain no dependency list at all; README.md has no installation section.
- **Impact**: A fresh Ubuntu/Debian/Fedora machine following the docs cannot build the workspace (openssl-sys build failure), `cargo build --release --workspace` inside the project's own test containers is expected to fail for the same reason, and soos-gui may fail to start on minimal systems. This is the core blocker for a one-command bootstrap.
- **Recommendation**: Document and encode per-distro package lists (see improvement plan), add libssl-dev/openssl-devel/openssl + pkg-config to all Dockerfiles and package build-deps, add python3/curl (or remove the python dependency, see ONB-09) and GUI runtime libs (`Recommends:` for the GUI) to runtime deps, and document ORT binary download (or pin `ORT_LIB_LOCATION`/vendored path for offline builds). Test: a Docker job that builds the full workspace from a bare `ubuntu:24.04`, `debian:12`, `fedora:40`, `archlinux` image using only the documented package list.
- **Verifier notes**: `cargo tree --locked -i openssl-sys` -> openssl-sys 0.9.117 <- native-tls 0.2.18 <- ureq 3.4.2 [build-dependencies] <- ort-sys 2.0.0-rc.13 <- ort <- soos-inference-ort <- daemon/enrollment-cli/gui/vision; `grep -c 'name = "openssl-src"' Cargo.lock` = 0 (not vendored). ~/.cargo/registry/.../ort-2.0.0-rc.13/Cargo.toml:147-155 default features include `download-binaries` and `tls-native`, and ort-sys build/main.rs:153 carries the network-failure message, confirming a build-time download. `cargo tree -i bindgen` -> bindgen 0.65.1 build-dep of v4l2-sys-mit. Verified in ubuntu:24.04 after installing exactly Dockerfile.ubuntu:13-23's package list: `pkg-config --exists openssl` rc=1, /usr/include/openssl absent, libssl-dev not installed. Caveat: CI's pam-integration job builds only `cargo build --release -p soos-pam` (tests/docker/test_suite.sh:64), which does not pull ort, so CI itself is green; the `--workspace` builds in tests/distro/debian_ubuntu_test.sh:152 and tests/docker/test_packages.sh:44 inside these images would fail. packaging/debian/control:5, soos.spec:10-16 and PKGBUILD:14 lack any OpenSSL dev package; x11-dl and glutin 'egl' features confirmed via cargo tree (exact runtime package names not verified). An option the finding misses: enabling ort's `tls-rustls` instead of `tls-native` removes the OpenSSL build dependency entirely.

#### ONB-08 — Uninstall cannot roll back PAM activation: no backup is ever created, Fedora profile is deleted while selected, and soos-admin gdm edits are never reverted

GitHub issue: #166

**MAJOR** · CONFIRMED · reliability · effort M

- **Locations**: `scripts/uninstall.sh:192-225`, `scripts/install.sh:324-351`, `crates/admin-cli/src/gdm.rs:82-115`, `Docs/DISTRIBUTION_DEPLOYMENT.md:205,238`, `Docs/PACKAGING_AND_PROVISIONING.md:98`
- **Evidence**: `grep -rn soos-backup` finds only uninstall.sh (restore loop, lines 193-201) and the invariant test; nothing in install.sh, packaging or admin-cli creates `*.soos-backup`, yet Docs promise `cp /etc/pam.d/system-auth.soos-backup /etc/pam.d/system-auth` as the rescue path. uninstall.sh line 217 `rm -rf /etc/authselect/custom/soos` without `authselect select <previous>` (authselect keeps pointing at a deleted profile; `authselect check`/`apply-changes` then fail). gdm.rs `GdmAction::Enable` inserts `auth sufficient pam_soos.so timeout_ms=2500` into /etc/pam.d/gdm-password via non-atomic `fs::write` (line 109) with no backup, and uninstall.sh never removes that line, leaving a dangling reference to a deleted module (harmless for `sufficient` but logged as a faulty module on every GDM login). The control keyword also differs from ARCHITECTURE §5 (`[success=done default=ignore]`).
- **Impact**: 'Clean removal without breaking authentication' (#26.3 acceptance) is not delivered; Fedora hosts are left with an invalid authselect state and Arch/GDM hosts with dangling PAM lines; the documented emergency rescue procedure references files that do not exist.
- **Recommendation**: Record pre-change state in /var/lib/soos/state/ (authselect `current --raw`, sha256 + copies of every /etc/pam.d file touched, pam-auth-update profile list) before any PAM edit; write PAM files atomically (temp + fsync + rename) with a `.soos-backup`; on uninstall: `pam-auth-update --package --remove`, `authselect select <saved profile+features> --force`, strip pam_soos lines from gdm-password, then delete the profile. Tests: Docker uninstall test asserting sha256 of /etc/pam.d/* and `authselect current` are byte-identical before install and after uninstall; unit test for gdm.rs backup+atomic write.
- **Verifier notes**: `grep -rn soos-backup` (excluding target/.git) hits only scripts/uninstall.sh:193-195 (restore loop), tests/invariants/src/lib.rs:785 (test fabricates the backup), the two Docs lines and a walkthrough; nothing creates a backup. uninstall.sh:216-218 `rm -rf` the authselect profile with no `authselect select` (post-deletion state not executed, but authselect.conf keeps pointing at custom/soos). `grep -n gdm scripts/uninstall.sh` returns nothing. crates/admin-cli/src/gdm.rs:95-109 inserts `auth  sufficient  pam_soos.so timeout_ms=2500` and writes with non-atomic `fs::write`, no backup; ARCHITECTURE.md:141 specifies `[success=done default=ignore]`. Docs/DISTRIBUTION_DEPLOYMENT.md:205,238 and PACKAGING_AND_PROVISIONING.md:98 promise `*.soos-backup` rescue files that never exist.

#### ONB-09 — download_models.sh requires python3>=3.11 (tomllib) with a nonexistent fallback module and no preflight; the last install step crashes on Arch-minimal/Ubuntu 22.04

GitHub issue: #167

**MAJOR** · CONFIRMED · reliability · effort S

- **Locations**: `scripts/download_models.sh:131-151`, `scripts/download_models.sh:265`, `scripts/install.sh:353-359`
- **Evidence**: parse_manifest tries `import tomllib`, then `tomli`, then `import tomllib_fallback` (line 140); `grep -rn tomllib_fallback` in the repo finds no such module, so on Python <3.11 the script dies with a ModuleNotFoundError traceback. Arch 'base' does not include python; nothing checks `command -v python3`/`curl` before use (curl at line 265). install.sh runs the model step last (353-359) after binaries, unit enable and PAM templates are already in place, so a failure leaves an enabled `soos-daemon.service` that will crash-loop (Restart=on-failure, RestartSec=2, no ConditionPathExists) because models are missing.
- **Impact**: One-command install fails at the final step on common hosts; daemon restart loop on every boot until models are fetched; the unit file has no guard for missing models.
- **Recommendation**: Remove the Python dependency (parse the flat manifest with awk/sed: `id/filename/sha256/source_url` keys only, or add `soos-admin models {fetch,verify}` in Rust reusing ModelRegistry), add a `require_cmd curl sha256sum` preflight, run the model step BEFORE enabling the unit, and add `ConditionPathExists=/var/lib/soos/models/manifest.toml` plus `StartLimitIntervalSec/StartLimitBurst` to soos-daemon.service. Test: T9 in test_suite.sh extended to run `--check-only` on fixture models without python3 in PATH; a unit test for the awk parser against models/manifest.toml.
- **Verifier notes**: scripts/download_models.sh:131-151 imports tomllib -> tomli -> `tomllib_fallback`; `grep -rn tomllib_fallback` finds nothing outside that line. curl at :265 with no preflight for python3/curl. `docker run --rm archlinux:latest sh -c 'command -v python3'` -> 'no python3 in archlinux base image'. install.sh order confirmed: `systemctl enable` at :319 (step 5) precedes the model step at :353-359 (step 7); packaging/soos-daemon.service has Restart=on-failure/RestartSec=2 and no ConditionPathExists; crates/daemon/src/main.rs:61-71 returns Err when initialize_pipeline fails. Note ubuntu:24.04 ships Python 3.12.3 so the tomllib gap only bites Ubuntu 22.04 (not a documented target) and python-less hosts such as Arch base.

#### ONB-10 — Deployment tests do not exercise real enrollment, violate the 0750 socket-dir invariant, and packaging tests never run in CI

GitHub issue: #168

**MAJOR** · CONFIRMED · test-gap · effort M

- **Locations**: `tests/distro/debian_ubuntu_test.sh:253-258,271-272`, `tests/distro/fedora_rhel_test.sh:315-327`, `tests/distro/arch_linux_test.sh:266-278`, `tests/docker/test_suite.sh:86-87`, `tests/invariants/src/lib.rs:679-760,1253-1330`, `.github/workflows/ci.yml:183-211`, `AI/VERIFICATION_MATRIX.md:235-237,258-261`
- **Evidence**: All three distro tests fabricate a template with `head -c 512 /dev/urandom > /var/lib/soos/biometrics/${TEST_USER}.bin`, whereas the store writes `<uid>.cbor.enc` (crates/biometric-store/src/store.rs:16,59); soos-enroll is never executed (`--mock` exists in crates/enrollment-cli/src/args.rs:45 and could be used). `chmod 777 /run/soos` appears in test_suite.sh:87 and all distro tests, contradicting the `0750 root:soos` invariant the daemon enforces. tests/invariants `test_distro_validation_suite_spec` only checks file existence and `contains(needle)` on script text; `test_install_script_creates_required_directories` only checks directory modes. ci.yml has no job invoking tests/docker/test_packages.sh, tests/distro/*, build_deb.sh, build_rpm.sh or build_arch.sh, while VERIFICATION_MATRIX rows PK5-PK7 and DV1-DV4 are marked '✅ Verified'.
- **Impact**: Packaging and deployment regressions (including ONB-01/02/03/05) are invisible to CI; the matrix overstates verification.
- **Recommendation**: Add a CI job (Docker, ubuntu always; fedora/arch on main) running test_packages.sh and the fixed distro tests; use `soos-enroll --mock enroll --username testuser` and assert `<uid>.cbor.enc` exists with mode 0600; replace `chmod 777` with `install -d -m 0750 -o root -g soos /run/soos` and run mock_daemon.py as root or with the socket group set; update VERIFICATION_MATRIX rows to cite the CI job. Test evidence: job name + run log referenced from the matrix.
- **Verifier notes**: debian_ubuntu_test.sh:254-255, fedora_rhel_test.sh:315-316, arch_linux_test.sh:266-267 write `head -c 512 /dev/urandom > /var/lib/soos/biometrics/${TEST_USER}.bin`; crates/biometric-store/src/store.rs:16 `TEMPLATE_EXTENSION = ".cbor.enc"`; enrollment-cli args.rs:43-45 has a global `--mock` flag that is never used by these tests. `grep -rn 'chmod 777' tests/ scripts/` -> test_suite.sh:87, the three distro tests, tests/physical/pam_integration_test.sh:208 (contradicts the 0750 invariant in install.sh:24). tests/invariants/src/lib.rs:1253+ only asserts file existence/contents. ci.yml contains no job for test_packages.sh, tests/distro or build_*.sh, while VERIFICATION_MATRIX.md rows PK5-PK7 and DV1-DV4 are marked Verified citing those scripts.

#### ONB-11 — Onboarding docs give wrong commands and file names; README has no install section

GitHub issue: #207

**MINOR** · PARTIAL · doc-drift · effort S

- **Locations**: `scripts/install.sh:365-369`, `Docs/DISTRIBUTION_DEPLOYMENT.md:71-78,84-89`, `README.md:1-30`, `Docs/PACKAGING_AND_PROVISIONING.md:37,79`, `AI/BACKLOG.md:1131`, `models/README.md:16,37`
- **Evidence**: install.sh 'Next steps' prints `sudo soos-enroll <username>` and `soos-admin test-pam` before any PAM activation; the actual CLI is `soos-enroll enroll --username <user>` (subcommand `Enroll(EnrollArgs)` with `-u/--username`, crates/enrollment-cli/src/args.rs:54-78). DISTRIBUTION_DEPLOYMENT.md:74-78 says `sudo soos-enroll alice` and `/var/lib/soos/biometrics/alice.bin` (real: `<uid>.cbor.enc`). README.md links only to protocol/CI/workflow docs; no prerequisites, build, install, enroll or uninstall steps. PACKAGING doc §3 says install.sh 'enables the service unit' but never starts it and never activates a PAM profile (only postinst does). models/README.md states ArcFace input RGB and MiniFASNet class 0 = Live, contradicting project-facts §4 (BGR/NHWC, live class 1) — outside my area but it is the document a new user reads.
- **Impact**: New users following the docs run commands that fail; the 'sub-250ms'/`timeout_ms=250` narrative in packaging text also conflicts with the enforced 1000 ms default in crates/pam/src/config.rs (project-facts §2).
- **Recommendation**: Add a README 'Quick start' (one-command bootstrap, verification, enrollment, rescue) and fix the commands/paths; regenerate the install.sh epilogue from the real CLI; add a doc-consistency invariant test that greps docs for `soos-enroll <` without a subcommand and for `.bin` template names.
- **Verifier notes**: Confirmed: install.sh:367 prints `sudo soos-enroll <username>` but enrollment-cli/src/args.rs:55-56,71-78 defines subcommand `Enroll(EnrollArgs)` with `--username`; Docs/DISTRIBUTION_DEPLOYMENT.md:74,77 use `soos-enroll alice` and `alice.bin` (real: `<uid>.cbor.enc`); README.md has no install/quick-start section; models/README.md:37 says Class 0 = Live while crates/inference-ort/src/pad.rs:82 has `DEFAULT_MINIFASNET_LIVE_CLASS_INDEX = 1` (project-facts.md:52-57 already records this drift). Corrections: (a) `soos-admin test-pam` is a real subcommand (admin-cli/src/args.rs:48), so that epilogue line is not wrong; (b) Docs/PACKAGING_AND_PROVISIONING.md:37 says install.sh 'enables the service unit', and install.sh:319 does `systemctl enable` — the doc does not claim it starts the daemon or activates PAM, so that sub-claim is not drift; (c) the `timeout_ms=250` vs DEFAULT_TIMEOUT_MS=1000 point is not a conflict: every template passes an explicit `timeout_ms=250` (config.rs:114-116 clamps 10..5000), which overrides the default.

#### ONB-12 — download_models.sh carries unreachable legacy URL table and unhardened curl invocation

GitHub issue: #208

**MINOR** · CONFIRMED · dead-code · effort S

- **Locations**: `scripts/download_models.sh:153-193`, `scripts/download_models.sh:265`, `models/manifest.toml:13,33,43`
- **Evidence**: resolve_download_url returns early when `source_url` ends with `.onnx` (line 160); all three manifest entries do (manifest lines 13, 33, 43), so the `case` table for scrfd/arcface/minifasnet and the four v1.0.0 models (ultraface_slim_320, landmark_5point, mobilefacenet_arcface, minifasnet_pad) is dead. curl (line 265) has no `--proto '=https' --tlsv1.2`, no `--max-time`, no `--max-filesize`; the temp file name `${dest_path}.tmp.$$` is predictable in a 0755 root-owned dir (fine as root, but no `umask`).
- **Impact**: Maintenance noise and a misleading suggestion that legacy models are still supported; slightly weaker download hardening than the Dockerfiles' rustup fetch.
- **Recommendation**: Delete the legacy table (manifest is the single source of truth), add `--proto '=https' --tlsv1.2 --max-time 600 --max-filesize 50M`, and verify size before hashing. Test: dry-run output must contain only manifest URLs.
- **Verifier notes**: scripts/download_models.sh:160 returns early when source_url ends with `.onnx` or starts with `file://`; models/manifest.toml:13,33,43 all end with `.onnx`, so the `case` table at :166-192 (including four v1.0.0 models absent from the manifest) is unreachable. curl at :265 is `curl -fSL --retry 3 --connect-timeout 15 -o ...` with no --proto/--tlsv1.2/--max-time/--max-filesize; temp path `${dest_path}.tmp.$$` at :254 with no umask.

#### ONB-13 — install.sh installs every distribution's PAM template on every distro, creating a bogus /etc/pam.d/soos.snippet service and cluttering packages

GitHub issue: #209

**MINOR** · CONFIRMED · bug · effort S

- **Locations**: `scripts/install.sh:324-351`, `packaging/arch/PKGBUILD:35`, `scripts/build_deb.sh:100-104`
- **Evidence**: Steps 6a-6c run unconditionally: staged tree from the scratchpad repro contains `./etc/authselect/custom/soos/*`, `./etc/pam.d/soos.snippet` and `./usr/share/pam-configs/*` together, so a Debian .deb ships a Fedora authselect profile and an Arch snippet, and a file placed in /etc/pam.d is interpreted by Linux-PAM as a service named `soos.snippet` (the snippet's `auth` lines make it a valid but unintended service).
- **Impact**: Confusing artifacts on every distro; a stray PAM service name; dpkg conffile handling of /etc/pam.d/soos.snippet on upgrades.
- **Recommendation**: Detect the distro (`/etc/os-release` ID/ID_LIKE, or `--distro`), install only the matching template, and move the Arch snippet to /usr/share/soos/pam/system-auth.snippet (not /etc/pam.d). Test: package content assertions per distro in test_packages.sh.
- **Verifier notes**: install.sh:329-351 installs the Debian profiles, Fedora authselect profile and Arch snippet unconditionally (only guarded by source existence). Scratchpad repro staged `etc/authselect/custom/soos/*`, `etc/pam.d/soos.snippet` and `usr/share/pam-configs/{soos,soos-notify}` together in one tree; PKGBUILD:35 also installs the snippet to /etc/pam.d/soos.snippet. Any file under /etc/pam.d is a PAM service name, so `soos.snippet` becomes an (unintended) service.

#### ONB-14 — Packaging metadata is inconsistent: wrong license, hardcoded versions, missing soos-gui in rpm/PKGBUILD, unlocked cargo builds

GitHub issue: #210

**MINOR** · CONFIRMED · doc-drift · effort S

- **Locations**: `packaging/rpm/soos.spec:6,99-104`, `packaging/arch/PKGBUILD:12,16-20`, `scripts/build_deb.sh:65,119`, `scripts/build_rpm.sh:65`, `scripts/build_arch.sh:65,117-118`, `Cargo.toml:21-23`, `packaging/debian/rules:12`
- **Evidence**: Cargo.toml:23 declares `license = "AGPL-3.0-or-later"`, but soos.spec:6 says `Apache-2.0 OR MIT`, PKGBUILD:12 `('Apache-2.0' 'MIT')`, build_arch.sh:117-118 `license = Apache-2.0 / MIT`. VERSION is hardcoded to 0.1.0 in build_deb.sh:65, build_rpm.sh:65, build_arch.sh:65 instead of `cargo metadata`. soos.spec %files (99-104) and PKGBUILD package() (24-35) omit soos-gui while install.sh/.deb include it (BACKLOG:1970-1972 acceptance). `cargo build --release --workspace` in rules:12, spec:31, PKGBUILD:19 runs without `--locked`, contrary to project-facts §5. PKGBUILD has `source=()` so `makepkg` outside the repo cannot build.
- **Impact**: Legally incorrect package metadata; version drift on release; GUI silently absent from RPM/Arch installs; non-reproducible package builds.
- **Recommendation**: Derive version/license from `cargo metadata --format-version 1` in one shared `scripts/lib/pkg_meta.sh`; add soos-gui (as optional subpackage) to spec/PKGBUILD; use `--locked --release`. Test: invariant test comparing license strings across Cargo.toml/spec/PKGBUILD/build scripts.
- **Verifier notes**: Cargo.toml:23 `license = "AGPL-3.0-or-later"`; packaging/rpm/soos.spec:6 `Apache-2.0 OR MIT`; PKGBUILD:12 `('Apache-2.0' 'MIT')`; build_arch.sh:117-118 `license = Apache-2.0` / `MIT`. VERSION="0.1.0" hardcoded at build_deb.sh:65, build_rpm.sh:65, build_arch.sh:65. soos.spec %files:99-104 and PKGBUILD package():24-35 have no soos-gui while install.sh:294-300 installs it and BACKLOG #50.4 (AI/BACKLOG.md:1970-1972) requires it. `grep -n locked packaging/debian/rules packaging/rpm/soos.spec packaging/arch/PKGBUILD scripts/build_*.sh` -> no matches. PKGBUILD:16 `source=()`.

#### ONB-15 — systemd unit lacks start guards and pre-conditions for a bootstrap-driven first start

GitHub issue: #211

**MINOR** · CONFIRMED · reliability · effort S

- **Locations**: `packaging/soos-daemon.service:4-14`, `scripts/install.sh:316-321`, `crates/daemon/src/main.rs:63-71`
- **Evidence**: Unit has `Restart=on-failure`, `RestartSec=2`, no `StartLimitIntervalSec`/`StartLimitBurst`, no `ConditionPathExists=` on the models manifest or master.key; main.rs 63-71 exits with error when pipeline init fails (missing models, no camera unless mock), so a fresh install with models not yet downloaded or no camera crash-loops until systemd's default rate limit trips. install.sh runs `systemctl enable` (319) but not `start`, and the README/docs never verify readiness (`/run/soos/daemon.sock` presence, `soos-admin status`). `Group=soos` requires the group before start; install.sh downgrades a missing group to a warning (line 199).
- **Impact**: Noisy journal, delayed first-run diagnosis for new users; bootstrap has no readiness signal to wait on.
- **Recommendation**: Add `ConditionPathExists=/var/lib/soos/models/manifest.toml`, `StartLimitIntervalSec=60`/`StartLimitBurst=5`, and a bootstrap readiness loop (poll socket up to 30 s, then `soos-admin status --format json`). Test: Docker test that enables the unit without models and asserts the unit is 'condition failed' rather than restart-looping.
- **Verifier notes**: packaging/soos-daemon.service:12-13 Restart=on-failure, RestartSec=2, no StartLimit* and no ConditionPathExists (systemd's default 5 starts/10 s limit eventually stops it). crates/daemon/src/main.rs:67-70 returns Err on pipeline init failure. install.sh:316-321 enables but never starts; :199 downgrades a missing group to a warning while the unit has `Group=soos` (:10). Recommendation is sound; `soos-admin status` with `--format` exists (admin-cli/src/args.rs:45,33-35) so a readiness probe can use it.

#### ONB-16 — rustup is fetched with unpinned curl|sh and the toolchain channel floats; the bootstrap needs a verified, pinned policy

GitHub issue: #260

**SUGGESTION** · CONFIRMED · security · effort S

- **Locations**: `Dockerfile:60-63`, `tests/docker/Dockerfile.ubuntu:26-28`, `tests/docker/Dockerfile.fedora:28-30`, `tests/docker/Dockerfile.arch:23-25`, `rust-toolchain.toml:1-3`
- **Evidence**: All images run `curl ... https://sh.rustup.rs | sh -s -- -y --default-toolchain stable`; rust-toolchain.toml pins only `channel = "stable"`, so every fresh machine builds with whatever stable is current (clippy `-D warnings` can break on new lints).
- **Impact**: Non-reproducible bootstrap and a supply-chain weak spot for the one-liner the user asked for.
- **Recommendation**: Bootstrap downloads `rustup-init` for the host triple from static.rust-lang.org together with its `.sha256`, verifies, installs with `--default-toolchain none --profile minimal`, then `rustup toolchain install` from a pinned `rust-toolchain.toml` (e.g. `channel = "1.xx.y"`), with `--use-system-rust` allowed when `rustc --version` >= MSRV. Publish a SHA-256 for bootstrap.sh in releases and document `sha256sum -c` instead of `curl | sudo bash`.
- **Verifier notes**: rust-toolchain.toml pins only `channel = "stable"`; no `rust-version` in Cargo.toml or any crate manifest (grep empty). Dockerfile:60-61, Dockerfile.ubuntu:26-27, Dockerfile.fedora:28-29, Dockerfile.arch:23-24 all run `curl ... https://sh.rustup.rs | sh -s -- -y --default-toolchain stable`. Accurate as a hardening suggestion.

### 4.4 PAM Module & IPC Protocol

*Coverage*: Read in full: crates/pam/src/{lib.rs,ipc.rs,config.rs,syslog.rs}, crates/pam/build.rs, crates/pam/Cargo.toml, crates/pam/tests/{ipc_tests.rs,config_tests.rs,pam_bindings_tests.rs,uid_resolution_tests.rs}, crates/protocol/src/{lib.rs,codec.rs,types.rs}, crates/protocol/tests/{property_tests.rs,preview_tests.rs}, crates/protocol/fuzz/{Cargo.toml,README.md,fuzz_targets/*.rs}, Docs/PAM_MODULE.md, Docs/IPC_PROTOCOL.md, tests/docker/{test_suite.sh,mock_daemon.py,pam_test_runner.c,Dockerfile.ubuntu}, root Cargo.toml [workspace.lints]/[profile.*], AGENTS.md, .agents/skills/dev-workflow/references/project-facts.md, .agents/skills/auditor-agent/SKILL.md, pam-bindings 0.3.0 sources (conv.rs, module.rs, items.rs from ~/.cargo/registry). Targeted reads (cross-references only): crates/daemon/src/dispatcher.rs lines 175-330, 400-500, 780-870; crates/daemon/src/peercred.rs; crates/daemon/src/pipeline.rs:73-85; crates/admin-cli/src/gdm.rs 40-115 and args.rs grep; crates/gui/src/ipc_camera.rs grep; AI/DECISIONS.md, AI/ARCHITECTURE.md, AI/VERIFICATION_MATRIX.md (PA rows), AI/walkthroughs/73 (grep); Docs/SECURITY_AND_QUALITY_GUIDELINES.md lines 32-63; packaging/scripts build commands (grep). Commands run: `cargo test --locked -p soos-pam -p soos-protocol --all-features` (all green: 5+16+23+8+4 pam tests, 16+2+10 protocol tests); three scratch repros under the scratchpad (panic=abort vs catch_unwind; drip-fed response body vs deadline; postcard trailing-byte leniency and varint widths). NOT covered: daemon internals beyond the cited lines (session validator, rate limiter, preview streaming server side), GUI/admin-cli client code beyond the preview/status bound grep, camera/vision/inference crates, Dockerfile.fedora/arch, run_matrix.sh, test_packages.sh, CI workflow content beyond grep for fuzz/docker. <details><summary>Reviewer improvement notes</summary>

Ordered plan for the fix pass (each step is one topic branch per AGENTS.md, TDD cycle, candid review):

1. fix/pam-panic-strategy (PAM-01, CRITICAL). Architect: ADR "PAM artifact must be built with panic=unwind; release profile abort is for daemon/CLIs only" and choose between a workspace-wide `panic = "unwind"` or a `[profile.release-pam]` used by every packaging script, install.sh and tests/docker/test_suite.sh. Tester: invariant test parsing Cargo.toml + Docker T10 fault-injection case running the release .so. Fix Docs/SECURITY_AND_QUALITY_GUIDELINES.md §compiler profiles.

2. fix/pam-cumulative-read-deadline (PAM-02, PAM-10). Refresh `set_read_timeout` inside `read_exact_counted` on every iteration; compute `deadline_monotonic_ns` once at `start_time`; ceil the poll budget. Contract tests: drip-fed body < 250 ms; deadline ≤ start + timeout.

3. fix/pam-service-item-and-disable-flags (PAM-05, PAM-17). Read `PAM_SERVICE` via `items::Service` when `service=` is absent; generic `/etc/soos/<service>.disable`; warn on rejected args. Docker test with gdm.disable; update Docs/PAM_MODULE.md §5 and admin-cli gdm docs.

4. fix/daemon-event-peer-check (PAM-04, cross-area with the daemon reviewer). `handle_event` must apply `verify_peer_credentials` semantics to `event.uid` for non-root peers and rate-limit events; integration test with non-root peer.

5. fix/pam-feedback-hardening (PAM-03, PAM-07, PAM-08, PAM-09). Introduce a `PamFeedback` trait (real handle + test recorder), remove the 0x10000 heuristic, honor PAM_SILENT, suppress messages on connect failure, collapse Deny messages to one neutral text (ADR if product disagrees), scope the panic hook with take_hook/restore. Fix walkthrough 73 references.

6. chore/protocol-spec-and-codec-strictness (PAM-06, PAM-12, PAM-13, PAM-14, PAM-16). Decide expiry enforcement (implement client check with real monotonic fixtures, or drop the claim); make `decode_with_limit` strict on trailing bytes; zeroize the encode intermediate; have the client use `Response::is_allow`/`matches_request`; delete the tautological check; rewrite Docs/IPC_PROTOCOL.md and protocol lib.rs frame layout from code; fix mock_daemon.py reason index; retire the "200–250 ms" prose with the clamp ADR from project-facts §2. Plan a v2 tagged `Message` enum as a separate backlog issue.

7. test/pam-protocol-coverage (PAM-15, PAM-11 documentation). Preview proptests + fuzz target, nightly fuzz CI job, Docker deny/PAM_SILENT cases; document the NSS resolution latency risk in Docs/PAM_MODULE.md and open a backlog item for username-based events.

Verification after each step: `cargo test --locked -p soos-pam -p soos-protocol --all-features`, `cargo clippy --locked --workspace --all-targets --all-features -- -D warnings`, `./scripts/candid_review.sh`, and `./run_tests.sh` for steps 1, 3 and 5 (Docker matrix).

#### PAM-01 — Release profile `panic = "abort"` makes every `catch_unwind` in the shipped pam_soos.so a no-op: any panic aborts the host login process instead of degrading to PAM_IGNORE

GitHub issue: #148

**CRITICAL** · CONFIRMED · security · effort M

- **Locations**: `Cargo.toml:126`, `crates/pam/src/lib.rs:288-309`, `crates/pam/src/lib.rs:84-159`, `Docs/SECURITY_AND_QUALITY_GUIDELINES.md:60-63`, `packaging/arch/PKGBUILD:19-29`, `scripts/install.sh:302-304`, `tests/docker/test_suite.sh:62-65`, `AI/VERIFICATION_MATRIX.md:51`
- **Evidence**: Root Cargo.toml `[profile.release]` sets `panic = "abort"` (line 126). Every production artifact is built with that profile: `cargo build --release --workspace` in packaging/arch/PKGBUILD:19, scripts/build_{deb,rpm,arch,packages}.sh, packaging/debian/rules:12; the Docker matrix deploys `target/release/libpam_soos.so` (test_suite.sh:62-65). Scratch repro (scratchpad/pam-ipc/abort_repro.rs): `rustc -C panic=unwind` prints `caught -> PAM_IGNORE`, exit 0; `rustc -C panic=abort` on the identical `catch_unwind(AssertUnwindSafe(|| panic!()))` aborts with exit 134 (SIGABRT). Docs/SECURITY_AND_QUALITY_GUIDELINES.md:60 claims overflow panics are "caught by catch_unwind" and line 63 says abort is only for panics that "escape catch_unwind" — both statements are false under panic=abort: no panic is ever caught. PA3 in the matrix is marked Verified on the basis of `cargo test` (test profile = unwind), which never exercises the release artifact. `overflow-checks = true` in release (Cargo.toml:127) increases the panic surface that now aborts.
- **Impact**: ARCHITECTURE.md Mandatory Security Invariant 5 ("any panic ... degrades silently to password fallback") and ADR [2026-09-12] Panic Safety are not satisfied by the installed module. A panic inside pam_soos.so kills gdm-session-worker, sudo, login or the screen locker (no password fallback, login denied/greeter restart). Not an authentication bypass (fail-closed), but a login-availability defect in the exact component whose contract is 'never crash the host'.
- **Recommendation**: Decide explicitly and record an ADR. Preferred: build pam_soos.so with unwinding, e.g. add `[profile.release-pam]` (`inherits = "release"`, `panic = "unwind"`) and use `cargo build --profile release-pam -p soos-pam` in every packaging script and in tests/docker/test_suite.sh, or set `panic = "unwind"` workspace-wide (rustc cannot set panic strategy per package). Fix Docs/SECURITY_AND_QUALITY_GUIDELINES.md:60-63. Add an invariant test in tests/invariants that parses Cargo.toml and asserts the profile used for the PAM artifact has `panic = "unwind"`, and add a Docker matrix case that loads the release .so with a `cfg(feature = "fault-injection")` argument that panics and asserts PAM_IGNORE + password fallback (must run against the release artifact, not `cargo test`).
- **Verifier notes**: Re-read Cargo.toml:121-127: `[profile.release]` has `panic = "abort"` and `overflow-checks = true`; no `.cargo/config.toml` exists and no `prefer-dynamic`/per-package override (grep, exit 1; Cargo forbids `panic` in `[profile.*.package.*]` anyway). All packaging paths build with that profile: packaging/arch/PKGBUILD:19, scripts/build_{arch,deb,rpm,packages}.sh (:87/:90/:90/:112), packaging/debian/rules:12 (`cargo build --release --workspace`), tests/docker/test_suite.sh:64 (`cargo build --release -p soos-pam`), and scripts/install.sh:302-304 installs whatever `find_artifact` finds. Re-ran the prior reviewer's binaries from the scratchpad: `./abort_bin` exits 134 (SIGABRT, nothing printed), `./unwind_bin` prints `caught -> PAM_IGNORE` exit 0 (rustc 1.96.0). Additional aggravation the finding missed: crates/pam/src/syslog.rs:22-31 the panic hook only records the location in a thread-local; `syslog::log_panic` is called solely from the `Err` branch of `catch_unwind` (lib.rs:143-153, 295-305), so under abort a production panic is both fatal and completely absent from the auth log. Docs/SECURITY_AND_QUALITY_GUIDELINES.md:60 ("caught by catch_unwind") and :63 ("if any unexpected panic escapes") are contradicted. AI/ARCHITECTURE.md:43 invariant 5 and AI/DECISIONS.md:9 are not met by the shipped artifact. Fail-closed (no bypass), but a documented mandatory security invariant is broken in the exact component whose contract is never to crash the host, so CRITICAL per the rubric stands.

#### PAM-02 — Read deadline is applied per `read()` syscall, not cumulatively: a slowly-written response body extends pam_sm_authenticate far beyond timeout_ms

GitHub issue: #173

**MAJOR** · CONFIRMED · reliability · effort S

- **Locations**: `crates/pam/src/ipc.rs:107-131`, `crates/pam/src/ipc.rs:312-319`, `crates/pam/src/ipc.rs:352-358`
- **Evidence**: `read_exact_counted` loops on `stream.read()` without touching the socket timeout; the only budget refreshes are `set_read_timeout` before the header (ipc.rs:313-315) and once before the body (353-355). `SO_RCVTIMEO` bounds each syscall, so each partial read gets the full remaining budget again. Scratch repro (scratchpad/pam-ipc/drip): server announces a 40-byte body and writes 1 byte every 70 ms; `pam_soos::ipc::authenticate` with `timeout_ms: 100` returned after **2.80 s** (`result=Err(RequestIdMismatch) elapsed=2.803611053s`). The existing contract test `test_ipc_slow_daemon_body_timeout` (ipc_tests.rs:441-475) only covers header-then-silence, not intra-body pacing.
- **Impact**: Violates the enforced invariant recorded in project-facts §2 ("every blocking PAM operation has an explicit deadline derived from the clamped timeout_ms; nothing is ever unbounded"). Worst case is bounded only by bytes×timeout (up to 4100 × 5 s). Requires a slow or compromised daemon (root), so not attacker-reachable from an unprivileged user, but a daemon under heavy load that writes in fragments will blow the GDM/sudo latency budget.
- **Recommendation**: Inside `read_exact_counted`, recompute `remaining_budget(start_time, total_timeout)?` and call `set_read_timeout` before every `read()` (pass `start`/`total` or a closure), or replace the blocking read with `poll(POLLIN, remaining_ms)` + non-blocking read. Add contract test `test_ipc_drip_fed_body_respects_cumulative_deadline` (server drips 1 byte per 70 ms with timeout_ms=100; assert PAM_IGNORE and elapsed < 250 ms).
- **Verifier notes**: Re-read ipc.rs:107-131 (`read_exact_counted` loops on `stream.read` without touching the timeout) and :312-315/:352-355 (only two `set_read_timeout` refreshes). The prior reviewer's cited scratch (scratchpad/pam-ipc/drip/src/main.rs) is actually the trailing-bytes repro, not a drip-feed test, so I wrote my own (scratchpad/pam-protocol-verify/drip2): server sends the 4-byte header immediately then 1 body byte every 70 ms; `pam_soos::ipc::authenticate` with `timeout_ms: 100` returned `result=Ok(Allow) elapsed=2.594062958s`. This is worse than reported: not only is the deadline unbounded (≈bytes×timeout), the verdict is still honored ~2.5 s after the budget expired — there is no post-read `remaining_budget` check before `Ok((resp.verdict, ..))` at ipc.rs:384. Existing test `test_ipc_slow_daemon_body_timeout` (ipc_tests.rs:441-475) only covers header-then-silence. Recommendation should add: reject the response if `remaining_budget` is exhausted after the body read, in addition to refreshing `SO_RCVTIMEO` per `read()`.

#### PAM-03 — `ReasonClass::PadFailed` is surfaced verbatim to the unauthenticated user ("[soos] Biometric spoof detected.") although the protocol declares reason_class must never be exposed

GitHub issue: #174

**MAJOR** · CONFIRMED · security · effort S

- **Locations**: `crates/pam/src/lib.rs:118-121`, `crates/pam/src/lib.rs:126-129`, `crates/protocol/src/types.rs:157-158`, `Docs/IPC_PROTOCOL.md:64`, `AI/walkthroughs/73_gdm_lockscreen_feedback_multi_frame.md:27`
- **Evidence**: types.rs:157-158 doc on `ReasonClass`: "Must NEVER be exposed to unprivileged users or leaked into user logs." lib.rs:118-121 sends `PAM_TEXT_INFO` "[soos] Biometric spoof detected." for `(Deny, PadFailed)` and a different text "[soos] Face not recognized." for other Deny reasons, plus "Camera unavailable" for `(Unavailable, CameraUnavailable)`. No ADR in AI/DECISIONS.md covers this (grep for spoof/PAM_TEXT_INFO only hits walkthrough 73).
- **Impact**: The lock screen becomes an oracle for presentation attacks: an attacker holding a photo/replay learns whether the PAD model tripped versus the embedding simply not matching, and can iterate spoof material until the message changes to "Face not recognized". Also leaks camera state to whoever is at the locked machine. Contradicts a documented protocol invariant without a recorded decision.
- **Recommendation**: Collapse all `Deny` outcomes to a single neutral message ("[soos] Face not recognized.") and all non-Allow outcomes to at most two texts (denied / unavailable); keep `reason_class` for daemon logs only. If product wants the spoof message, add an ADR that explicitly revokes the types.rs invariant and updates it. Add a unit test asserting the conversation text is identical for every `ReasonClass` under `Verdict::Deny` (needs a conversation mock, see PAM-15).
- **Verifier notes**: lib.rs:118-121 emits `[soos] Biometric spoof detected.` for `(Deny, PadFailed)` vs `[soos] Face not recognized.` for other Deny (122-125) and `[soos] Camera unavailable.` for `(Unavailable, CameraUnavailable)` (126-129), all via `PAM_TEXT_INFO` to the unauthenticated conversation. types.rs:157-158 on `ReasonClass`: "Must NEVER be exposed to unprivileged users or leaked into user logs." `grep -n -i spoof|PAM_TEXT_INFO|reason_class|conversation AI/DECISIONS.md` returns only the model ADRs (lines 16, 19), no ADR revoking the invariant; walkthrough 73:27 merely lists the strings. The PAD/embedding distinction is a genuine presentation-attack oracle at the lock screen and violates a written protocol invariant without a recorded decision; MAJOR holds.

#### PAM-04 — `event=password-failed` path: daemon trusts `event.uid` from any socket peer without SO_PEERCRED cross-check, so any `soos`-group process can trigger camera evidence snapshots attributed to arbitrary UIDs

GitHub issue: #175

**MAJOR** · CONFIRMED · security · effort M

- **Locations**: `crates/daemon/src/dispatcher.rs:264-303`, `crates/daemon/src/dispatcher.rs:273`, `crates/daemon/src/dispatcher.rs:416-441`, `crates/pam/src/ipc.rs:401-408`
- **Evidence**: `handle_event` (dispatcher.rs:273): `let target_uid = event.uid.unwrap_or(peer_uid);` then `store_snapshot(target_uid, "PasswordFailed", &frame.data, ...)` whenever evidence is enabled. Unlike `handle_request`, which calls `verify_peer_credentials(&cred_struct, req.uid_hint)` (dispatcher.rs:422), no peer/uid check, no rate limit and no request_id binding exist on the event path. The PAM client sends `uid: Some(uid)` with `request_id: None` (ipc.rs:401-408), and the socket is 0660 root:soos, so every member of the `soos` group (the GUI user) can send this frame at will.
- **Impact**: An unprivileged local user in the `soos` group can (a) take camera snapshots on demand through the root daemon and (b) attribute them to any UID in the evidence store, polluting intrusion evidence and defeating the privacy/opt-in intent of the evidence feature. The PAM crate is the only legitimate emitter, so this is a missing authorization check in the daemon on a PAM-originated path (cross-area: daemon).
- **Recommendation**: In `handle_event`, apply the same rule as requests: if `peer_uid != 0` then require `event.uid.is_none() || event.uid == Some(peer_uid)`, otherwise drop the event with a warn log; add a per-peer token-bucket for events. Daemon integration test: non-root peer sending `Event{uid: Some(other)}` stores nothing; root peer stores. Consider also carrying the failed auth `request_id` from the preceding pam_soos.so call (currently always `None`).
- **Verifier notes**: dispatcher.rs:264-303 `handle_event`: `let target_uid = event.uid.unwrap_or(peer_uid);` then `store_snapshot(target_uid, "PasswordFailed", &frame.data, ..)` when `evidence_store.config().enabled`; no call to `verify_peer_credentials`, no rate limiter (the only rate-limit is inside `handle_request` at :506-513), only the global `Semaphore(max_concurrent_connections=8)` (config.rs:53). `handle_request` does verify at :417-422. peercred.rs:51-63 confirms the request rule (root or peer==requested). PAM client sends `uid: Some(uid), request_id: None` (ipc.rs:401-408). Socket is 0660 root:soos (daemon config.rs:30, socket.rs:259-281) and Docs/PACKAGING_AND_PROVISIONING.md:127 documents `usermod -aG soos <username>` for the GUI user, so the desktop user can emit forged events. Caveat: evidence is opt-in (evidence-store/src/config.rs:36 `enabled: false`), so the snapshot side effect only exists when an admin enabled it; the missing authorization check itself is unconditional. MAJOR stands (cross-area: daemon).

#### PAM-05 — `/etc/soos/gdm.disable` (written by `soos-admin gdm disable`) never disables the GDM PAM line because the module never reads PAM_SERVICE and the installed line passes no `service=`

GitHub issue: #176

**MAJOR** · CONFIRMED · bug · effort S

- **Locations**: `crates/pam/src/config.rs:71-76`, `crates/pam/src/config.rs:18`, `crates/pam/src/config.rs:135-142`, `crates/pam/src/lib.rs:46`, `crates/admin-cli/src/gdm.rs:51-68`, `crates/admin-cli/src/gdm.rs:97-105`, `crates/daemon/src/dispatcher.rs:800-804`
- **Evidence**: `is_disabled()` checks `/etc/soos/gdm.disable` only when `self.service == "gdm-password" || self.service.contains("gdm")` (config.rs:71-76). `service` is populated solely from the `service=` PAM argument (config.rs:135-142) and defaults to "pam_soos" (config.rs:18); the only `get_item` call in the PAM crate is for `Conv` (lib.rs:46), never `items::Service` although pam-bindings exposes it (items.rs:81). `soos-admin gdm disable` only writes the flag file (gdm.rs:51-68) and the line it installs is `auth  sufficient  pam_soos.so timeout_ms=2500` with no `service=` (gdm.rs:97,105). Every packaging PAM snippet also omits `service=`. The unit test `test_gdm_disable_file_triggers_disabled_state` passes only because it sets `service: "gdm-password"` and `disable_file` by hand (config_tests.rs:198-213). Side effect: `req.service.contains("gdm")` in the daemon (dispatcher.rs:800-804) never matches real GDM traffic either, so `camera.notify_activity()` is never called for the lock screen.
- **Impact**: An administrator who runs `soos-admin gdm disable` (or creates the documented flag) believes facial login is off while pam_soos.so keeps authenticating at the GDM greeter; `soos-admin gdm status` reports 'disabled' (gdm.rs:33) — a misleading security control. Daemon per-service behaviour keyed on the service name is silently dead for the primary use case.
- **Recommendation**: In `authenticate_with_config`, when no `service=` argument is present, read `pamh.get_item::<pam_bindings::items::Service>()` and use it (bounded to MAX_SERVICE_LEN) as `config.service`. Additionally make `is_disabled()` honor `/etc/soos/<service>.disable` generically. Docker matrix test: create `/etc/soos/gdm.disable`, register a service file named `gdm-password`, run pam_test_runner without password and assert the password prompt is reached (PAM_IGNORE). Update `soos-admin gdm` to add `service=gdm-password` only if the item-based fix is not adopted.
- **Verifier notes**: config.rs:71-76 gates `/etc/soos/gdm.disable` on `self.service` containing "gdm"; `service` is set only by `service=` (config.rs:135-142) and defaults to "pam_soos" (:18). `grep -rn get_item crates/pam/src` → only lib.rs:46 (`Conv`), never `items::Service`, although pam-bindings 0.3.0 exposes it (items.rs:5,81; module.rs:145 `get_item`). Every installed PAM line omits `service=`: gdm.rs:97/105 (`auth sufficient pam_soos.so timeout_ms=2500`), packaging/pam/{arch,fedora}/*, Dockerfile.*, Docs/PAM_MODULE.md:86/92. `soos-admin gdm disable` only writes the flag (gdm.rs:51-68) and `status` reports `disabled` purely from file existence (gdm.rs:33-34). The unit test config_tests.rs:199-213 hand-sets `service: "gdm-password"` so it cannot detect this. Side effect also verified: dispatcher.rs:800-804 keys `notify_activity()` on `req.service.contains("gdm")`, which never matches. Misleading security control → MAJOR.

#### PAM-06 — `expires_monotonic_ns` / `issued_monotonic_ns` are documented as replay protection but never validated by the PAM client

GitHub issue: #219

**MINOR** · CONFIRMED · doc-drift · effort S

- **Locations**: `crates/pam/src/ipc.rs:369-384`, `crates/protocol/src/types.rs:190-193`, `Docs/IPC_PROTOCOL.md:65-66`, `crates/daemon/src/dispatcher.rs:844-845`, `crates/pam/tests/ipc_tests.rs:130-131`, `tests/docker/mock_daemon.py:44-47`
- **Evidence**: After decode the client checks only `resp.version` and `resp.request_id` (ipc.rs:371-379); `grep -n expires crates/pam/src/ipc.rs` returns nothing. The daemon sets `expires = now + 2 s` (dispatcher.rs:845) on CLOCK_MONOTONIC (pipeline.rs:73-74), the same clock the client uses (ipc.rs:427). Docs/IPC_PROTOCOL.md:66 and types.rs:191-193 describe the field as "short expiration timestamp preventing replay". Test fixtures send `expires_monotonic_ns: 2000` (ipc_tests.rs:131) and the mock daemon sends 0 (mock_daemon.py:46-47), i.e. values in the past.
- **Impact**: No replay is currently possible because the nonce is fresh per connection, so this is defense-in-depth drift, not a bypass. But the documented guarantee is unimplemented and the fixtures would break the day it is implemented.
- **Recommendation**: Either enforce on the client (`now_ns < resp.expires_monotonic_ns && resp.issued_monotonic_ns <= now_ns + skew`, treating `expires == 0` as invalid) and update fixtures/mock_daemon to emit real CLOCK_MONOTONIC values, or delete the replay claim from Docs/IPC_PROTOCOL.md and types.rs. Property test: any response with `expires <= now` is rejected.
- **Verifier notes**: `grep -n expires\|issued crates/pam/src/*.rs` returns nothing; ipc.rs:369-379 validates only `version` and `request_id`. dispatcher.rs:844-845 sets `expires = now + 2_000_000_000` on CLOCK_MONOTONIC (pipeline.rs:73-74), same clock as the client (ipc.rs:427). Docs/IPC_PROTOCOL.md:66 and types.rs:191-193 claim replay protection. Fixtures: ipc_tests.rs:130-131 (`issued 1000 / expires 2000`), mock_daemon.py:44-47 (both 0). No bypass since the nonce is single-use; doc/code drift, MINOR is right.

#### PAM-07 — `send_pam_info` guards the PAM handle with an `addr >= 0x10000` heuristic that exists only to tolerate fake handles in tests; walkthrough cites tests that do not exist

GitHub issue: #220

**MINOR** · CONFIRMED · design · effort M

- **Locations**: `crates/pam/src/lib.rs:42-51`, `crates/pam/src/lib.rs:93-100`, `crates/pam/tests/pam_bindings_tests.rs:25-27`, `crates/pam/tests/pam_bindings_tests.rs:54-56`, `AI/walkthroughs/73_gdm_lockscreen_feedback_multi_frame.md:23`, `AI/walkthroughs/73_gdm_lockscreen_feedback_multi_frame.md:52`
- **Evidence**: lib.rs:44-45 casts the `&mut PamHandle` to usize and skips the conversation when `< 0x10000`. Tests build handles as `0x1000 as *mut PamHandle` (pam_bindings_tests.rs:25,54,76) and pass `uid=1000` so that `h.get_user(None)` at lib.rs:95 (which has no such guard and would dereference 0x1000) is never reached. Walkthrough 73 lines 23/52 cite `config_tests.rs::test_send_pam_info_low_address_guard` and `test_send_pam_info_null_safe`; `grep -rn` over crates/ and tests/ finds neither (exit 1).
- **Impact**: Production code carries a meaningless pointer heuristic (a real handle is never below 0x10000, a bogus one above it is still dereferenced); tests are fragile (omitting `uid=` segfaults) and the traceability record references non-existent evidence.
- **Recommendation**: Remove the address heuristic. Introduce a small `trait PamFeedback { fn info(&mut self, msg: &str); fn user(&mut self) -> Option<String>; }` implemented for `&mut PamHandle` and for a test recorder; make `authenticate_with_config` generic over it. Rewrite the three tests to use the recorder, and correct walkthrough 73 to cite the real tests.
- **Verifier notes**: lib.rs:44-45 `addr >= 0x10000` guard confirmed; `h.get_user(None)` at lib.rs:95 has no such guard. pam_bindings_tests.rs:25,54,76 build `0x1000 as *mut PamHandle` (PamHandle is a ZST `_data: [u8;0]`, module.rs:15-16, so the reference itself is legal) and every authenticate test passes `uid=1000`, which is the only reason `get_user` (→ libpam `pam_get_user` on a bogus pointer) is never reached. `grep -rn test_send_pam_info_low_address_guard\|test_send_pam_info_null_safe crates/ tests/` → exit 1; walkthrough 73:52 cites both. All claims hold.

#### PAM-08 — PAM_SILENT flag is ignored and informational messages are emitted even when the daemon is not installed/running

GitHub issue: #221

**MINOR** · CONFIRMED · bug · effort S

- **Locations**: `crates/pam/src/lib.rs:111`, `crates/pam/src/lib.rs:134-137`, `crates/pam/src/lib.rs:164`, `crates/pam/src/lib.rs:323-328`
- **Evidence**: Both entry points discard the flags (`_flags: PamFlag` lib.rs:164, `_flags: i32` lib.rs:325) while `send_pam_info` always emits `PAM_TEXT_INFO` (lib.rs:111 before the IPC call, 115-136 after). pam-bindings defines `PAM_SILENT = 0x8000` (constants.rs:11). On `Err(IpcError::Connect(ENOENT/ECONNREFUSED))` the module still prints "[soos] Looking for face..." then "[soos] Face verification unavailable." (lib.rs:134-137).
- **Impact**: Applications that call `pam_authenticate(pamh, PAM_SILENT)` (cron, some sshd/su paths, scripted sudo) receive unexpected conversation calls, which the Linux-PAM module writer's guide forbids; every `sudo` on a machine where the daemon is stopped or not installed prints two extra lines.
- **Recommendation**: Thread `flags` into `authenticate_with_config`; skip all `send_pam_info` when `flags & PAM_SILENT != 0`. Do not print the pre-IPC message until the socket connects, and suppress the post-message on `IpcError::Connect`. Test via Docker `pam_test_runner` (count PAM_TEXT_INFO messages with `PAM_SILENT`, and with the socket absent).
- **Verifier notes**: lib.rs:164 `_flags: PamFlag` and :325 `_flags: i32` are discarded; `send_pam_info(&pamh, "[soos] Looking for face...")` at :111 runs before `ipc::authenticate`, and the `Err(_)` arm at :134-137 emits "[soos] Face verification unavailable." for any `IpcError` including `Connect`. pam-bindings constants.rs:11 `PAM_SILENT = 0x8000`; `grep -rn PAM_SILENT crates Docs tests` → nothing. Behaviour as described; MINOR.

#### PAM-10 — Deadline sent to the daemon is computed after connect, so the daemon may keep working (and hold the camera) after the client has already given up; sub-millisecond poll budget truncates to 0

GitHub issue: #222

**MINOR** · CONFIRMED · improvement · effort S

- **Locations**: `crates/pam/src/ipc.rs:277-281`, `crates/pam/src/ipc.rs:293-295`, `crates/pam/src/ipc.rs:207-208`
- **Evidence**: `start_time` is taken at ipc.rs:277 and connect may consume part of the budget (280-281), yet `deadline_monotonic_ns = monotonic_nanos() + timeout_ms` is taken fresh at 293-295, i.e. the daemon's deadline is later than the client's by the connect duration. At 207-208 `remaining.as_millis()` truncates (e.g. 0.9 ms → 0 → `poll(...,0)` returns immediately → `IpcError::Timeout`).
- **Impact**: Daemon pipeline runs up to a full connect-latency longer than anyone is listening and writes to a closed socket (wasted camera/ONNX time, log noise); sub-ms rounding produces a spurious early timeout instead of using the remaining budget.
- **Recommendation**: Capture `let deadline_ns = monotonic_nanos().saturating_add(timeout_ns)` once next to `start_time` and reuse it; use `remaining.as_millis().max(1)` (or ceil) for `poll`. Unit test: assert `deadline - now <= timeout` measured before connect.
- **Verifier notes**: ipc.rs:277 `start_time`, :280-281 connect consumes budget, :293-295 `deadline_monotonic_ns = monotonic_nanos() + timeout_ms*1e6` taken after connect → daemon deadline later than client's by the connect latency. ipc.rs:207-208: `remaining_budget` filters `Duration::ZERO` but `remaining.as_millis()` truncates sub-ms to 0 → `poll(.., 0)` returns 0 → `IpcError::Timeout` (line 218-219). Both correct; MINOR.

#### PAM-11 — Username→UID resolution (`pam_get_user` + `getpwnam_r`) runs outside every deadline, including the 20 ms `event=password-failed` path

GitHub issue: #223

**MINOR** · CONFIRMED · reliability · effort M

- **Locations**: `crates/pam/src/lib.rs:93-103`, `crates/pam/src/lib.rs:105-109`, `crates/pam/src/lib.rs:225-279`
- **Evidence**: `config.uid.unwrap_or_else(...)` (lib.rs:93-103) calls `h.get_user(None)` and `resolve_username_to_uid` before `ipc::notify_event`/`ipc::authenticate`; `getpwnam_r` (lib.rs:243-251) has no timeout and NSS backends (sssd/LDAP/NIS) can block for seconds when the network is down. `EVENT_TIMEOUT_MS = 20` (ipc.rs:28) only bounds the socket part.
- **Impact**: On LDAP/AD-joined machines with the directory unreachable, the 'best-effort 20 ms telemetry' line can stall the password-failure path for the NSS timeout; the auth path similarly exceeds timeout_ms. The stack usually has a warm nsswitch cache (pam_unix already resolved the user), which limits but does not eliminate the risk.
- **Recommendation**: For the event path, send the username (bounded) instead of resolving it in the PAM process and let the daemon resolve it off the latency path; for the auth path, document the dependency and prefer `uid=`-less deployments to rely on `PAM_USER` only when `sss_cache` is warm. At minimum log (LOG_INFO) when resolution exceeds the budget. Test: inject a slow resolver via a function pointer and assert notify_event is skipped/bounded.
- **Verifier notes**: lib.rs:93-103 resolves the UID (`h.get_user` + `resolve_username_to_uid` → `getpwnam_r` at :243-251, no timeout) before both `ipc::notify_event` (:107) and `ipc::authenticate` (:113); `EVENT_TIMEOUT_MS = 20` (ipc.rs:28) starts only inside `notify_event` (:389). NSS latency is outside every deadline as claimed. The recommendation's 'prefer uid=-less deployments ... when sss_cache is warm' is confusing (uid= actually avoids the lookup entirely); a clearer recommendation is to accept `uid=` from the stack where available and to log when resolution exceeds the budget. MINOR.

#### PAM-12 — Codec accepts trailing bytes inside the declared payload on the client while the daemon is strict, and the wire format has no message-type discriminator (daemon guesses Request vs Event by heuristic)

GitHub issue: #224

**MINOR** · CONFIRMED · design · effort M

- **Locations**: `crates/protocol/src/codec.rs:178-179`, `crates/daemon/src/dispatcher.rs:214-245`, `crates/protocol/src/types.rs:62-94`
- **Evidence**: `decode_with_limit` ends with `postcard::from_bytes(payload_slice)` (codec.rs:179), which ignores unconsumed bytes; scratch repro: a valid Allow response with 3 garbage bytes appended inside the declared length decodes as `Ok(Allow)`. The daemon instead uses `take_from_bytes` + `rest.is_empty()` (dispatcher.rs:216-222) and, because `Request` and `Event` share the framing with no tag, decodes the body as both and disambiguates with `req.uid_hint == peer.uid` (dispatcher.rs:225-245). For root peers (gdm/sudo/login, uid_hint ≠ 0) that tie-break routes an ambiguous payload to the Event handler, i.e. no response → client timeout. The probability that a random 32-byte nonce makes a genuine Request also parse as a valid Event is negligible (needs Option tags in {0,1}, valid UTF-8 string and exact length), so this is a design fragility rather than an observed bug.
- **Impact**: Two decoders with different strictness for the same v1 protocol; schema evolution (adding a Request field) can make the heuristic mis-route; fuzz/property tests cannot express 'exactly one interpretation'.
- **Recommendation**: Protocol v2 (or v1 minor with bump of CURRENT_VERSION): wrap payloads in `enum Message { Request(Request), Event(Event), Status(...), Preview(...) }` so the first varint is a discriminator; make `decode_with_limit` strict (`take_from_bytes` and reject non-empty rest) on both sides. Add codec unit test `decode_rejects_trailing_bytes` and a property test that a `Message` roundtrips and never decodes as another variant.
- **Verifier notes**: codec.rs:178-179 ends with `postcard::from_bytes(payload_slice)` (lenient). Re-ran the prior scratch (scratchpad/pam-ipc/drip): `decode with trailing bytes inside payload -> Ok(Allow)`. dispatcher.rs:216-222 uses `take_from_bytes` + `rest.is_empty()` (strict) and :225-245 disambiguates Request/Event by `req.uid_hint == peer.uid`, so for a root peer with `uid_hint != 0` a payload that parses as both is routed to `handle_event` (no response). No message-type tag in types.rs. Design fragility as described; MINOR.

#### PAM-13 — Intermediate `postcard::to_allocvec` buffer holding the request nonce is freed without zeroization despite the documented 'all buffers zeroized' guarantee

GitHub issue: #225

**MINOR** · CONFIRMED · security · effort S

- **Locations**: `crates/protocol/src/codec.rs:88-116`, `crates/pam/src/ipc.rs:306`, `Docs/PAM_MODULE.md:100`, `Docs/IPC_PROTOCOL.md:155`
- **Evidence**: `encode_with_limit` serializes into `payload: Vec<u8>` (codec.rs:89), copies it into `buf` (113-114) and drops `payload` un-zeroized; the caller wraps only the returned `buf` in `Zeroizing` (ipc.rs:306). Docs claim `encoded`, `len_buf`, `full_buf` and nonces are all scrubbed (PAM_MODULE.md:100, IPC_PROTOCOL.md:155).
- **Impact**: The 256-bit request_id (and service string) persists in freed heap of the login process until reuse. Low practical risk (nonce is single-use and the response binding is the real control), but it is a documented invariant (PA16) that is not fully met.
- **Recommendation**: In `encode_with_limit`, serialize directly into the pre-sized output (`postcard::to_extend` into a `Vec` pre-filled with 4 placeholder bytes, then patch the prefix) or wrap `payload` in `zeroize::Zeroizing`. Unit test cannot observe freed memory; cover by code review + a `cargo geiger`-style grep in tests/invariants for `to_allocvec` in codec.rs.
- **Verifier notes**: codec.rs:89 `let payload = postcard::to_allocvec(msg)`, :112-114 copies into `buf`, `payload` dropped without zeroization; ipc.rs:306 wraps only the returned `buf`. Docs/PAM_MODULE.md:100 and Docs/IPC_PROTOCOL.md:155 claim all intermediate buffers are scrubbed. Correct; low practical risk; MINOR.

#### PAM-14 — Docs/IPC_PROTOCOL.md, Docs/PAM_MODULE.md, protocol lib.rs and mock_daemon.py describe identifiers, wire widths, latency budgets and enum indices that do not match the code

GitHub issue: #226

**MINOR** · CONFIRMED · doc-drift · effort S

- **Locations**: `Docs/IPC_PROTOCOL.md:17`, `Docs/IPC_PROTOCOL.md:41-42`, `Docs/IPC_PROTOCOL.md:50`, `Docs/IPC_PROTOCOL.md:78`, `crates/protocol/src/lib.rs:15-20`, `Docs/PAM_MODULE.md:20`, `crates/pam/src/config.rs:9-15`, `crates/pam/src/lib.rs:10`, `crates/pam/src/ipc.rs:7`, `tests/docker/mock_daemon.py:30`, `crates/protocol/src/types.rs:161-169`, `AI/VERIFICATION_MATRIX.md:50`
- **Evidence**: IPC_PROTOCOL.md:41 references `CodecError::PayloadCorrupted` and :42 `PROTOCOL_VERSION` — neither exists (actual: `ValidationError::ServiceTooLong`, `CURRENT_VERSION`); :50 lists `RequestKind::Authenticate` vs actual `Auth | Status | PreviewFrame` (types.rs:40-47); `StatusResponse`, `PreviewResponse`, `MAX_PREVIEW_MESSAGE_SIZE`, `encode_preview/decode_preview` are absent from the spec; :78 says `ZeroizeOnDrop` while the code implements `Drop` manually (types.rs:227-232). protocol lib.rs:15-20 documents `uid_hint:u32`, `service_len:u8`, `verdict:u8`, `*_ns:u64` as fixed widths, but postcard uses varints — repro: `uid_hint=1000` encodes to 43 bytes, `uid_hint=1` to 42. Latency: IPC_PROTOCOL.md:17, PAM_MODULE.md:20, pam lib.rs:10, ipc.rs:7 and matrix PA2 say 200–250 ms, while `DEFAULT_TIMEOUT_MS = 1000`, clamp 10–5000 (config.rs:9-15) and the GDM line uses 2500 (gdm.rs:97) — drift acknowledged in project-facts §2 but still unfixed in the crate docs. mock_daemon.py:30 `REASON_SCORE_BELOW_THRESHOLD = 1` decodes as `ReasonClass::NoFace` (index 1; ScoreBelowThreshold is 3, types.rs:165-169).
- **Impact**: Future agents (per project-facts precedence rule) will re-derive wrong constants/types from prose; the Docker deny mode asserts the wrong reason class if ever inspected.
