- **Recommendation**: Rewrite Docs/IPC_PROTOCOL.md §2–3 from the code (postcard varint widths, real enum names, preview/status types, both size limits, clamp range), fix the frame diagram in protocol lib.rs, set `REASON_SCORE_BELOW_THRESHOLD = 3` in mock_daemon.py, and add the ADR proposed by project-facts §2 for the 10–5000 ms clamp so the '250 ms' prose can be retired everywhere (including PA2 wording).
- **Verifier notes**: Docs/IPC_PROTOCOL.md:41 `CodecError::PayloadCorrupted`, :42 `PROTOCOL_VERSION`: `grep -rn PayloadCorrupted\|PROTOCOL_VERSION crates/protocol/src/` → exit 1. :50 `RequestKind` shows `Authenticate` vs code `Auth|Status|PreviewFrame`. :77-78 says `ZeroizeOnDrop`, code is manual `impl Drop for Response` (types.rs:227-232). protocol lib.rs:15-20 draws fixed widths; repro prints request wire len 43 for uid_hint=1000 vs 42 for uid_hint=1 (varint). Latency prose 200–250 ms at IPC_PROTOCOL.md:17, PAM_MODULE.md:20, pam lib.rs:10, ipc.rs:7 and VERIFICATION_MATRIX PA2 ('> 250ms') vs `DEFAULT_TIMEOUT_MS = 1000`, clamp 10–5000 (config.rs:9-15), gdm.rs:97 uses 2500. mock_daemon.py:30 `REASON_SCORE_BELOW_THRESHOLD = 1` while types.rs:165 `NoFace = 1`, :169 `ScoreBelowThreshold = 3`. All drift confirmed.

#### PAM-15 — No test exercises the PAM conversation, the disable-flag path through the real entry point, drip-fed reads, PreviewFrame/PreviewResponse properties, or fuzzing in CI

GitHub issue: #227

**MINOR** · CONFIRMED · test-gap · effort M

- **Locations**: `crates/pam/tests/pam_bindings_tests.rs:22-92`, `crates/pam/tests/config_tests.rs:198-213`, `crates/protocol/tests/property_tests.rs:44-46`, `crates/protocol/fuzz/README.md:1-40`, `.github/workflows/ci.yml:194-211`, `tests/docker/test_suite.sh:99-262`
- **Evidence**: All PAM tests pass a null `pamh` or a fake `0x1000` pointer with `uid=`; `send_pam_info` and `get_user` are never executed under test (no conversation mock exists; pam-bindings' `Inner` is only usable inside that crate). `test_gdm_disable_file_triggers_disabled_state` bypasses `pam_sm_authenticate` and hand-sets `service` (would not catch PAM-05). No drip-feed read test (PAM-02). `arb_request_kind` (property_tests.rs:44-46) omits `PreviewFrame`; no strategy or never-panics property for `PreviewResponse`/`decode_preview`, and no fuzz target for it; `grep fuzz .github/workflows` finds nothing, so libFuzzer targets never run. Docker matrix has no case for a panicking release module (PAM-01), for PAM_SILENT, or for a Deny verdict (mock has a `deny` mode that test_suite.sh never uses).
- **Impact**: The highest-value invariants (panic safety of the shipped artifact, conversation safety, disable flags, cumulative deadline) are asserted by prose rather than tests; regressions in those areas would pass CI.
- **Recommendation**: (1) Add a Docker T10 that runs the release .so with fault injection (see PAM-01) and T11 with `--mode deny` asserting password fallback and message count; (2) introduce the feedback trait from PAM-07 and unit-test message selection and PAM_SILENT; (3) add the drip-feed test from PAM-02; (4) extend proptest with `PreviewFrame`, an `arb_preview_response` roundtrip and `prop_decode_preview_never_panics` (0..=2 MiB+4 bytes, bounded cases); add `fuzz_targets/decode_preview.rs`; (5) add a nightly CI job running each fuzz target for a fixed `-max_total_time` and uploading crashes.
- **Verifier notes**: pam_bindings_tests.rs:22-92: all handles are null or `0x1000` with `uid=`; no conversation mock. config_tests.rs:199-213 bypasses the entry point. No drip-feed test (`grep -n sleep crates/pam/tests/ipc_tests.rs` shows only 300/350/250 ms single sleeps at :83/:459/:488). property_tests.rs:44-46 `arb_request_kind` is `Auth|Status` only. `grep -rn fuzz .github/workflows/` → exit 1. tests/docker/test_suite.sh uses mock modes allow/timeout/crash-immediate/crash-partial only (lines 107-232) although mock_daemon.py:7,135 implements `deny`; ci.yml:206-211 runs T1–T9 only. All gaps real.

#### PAM-09 — Process-global panic hook is installed inside the host process and never restored, dangling after Linux-PAM dlcloses the module

GitHub issue: #263

**SUGGESTION** · PARTIAL · reliability · effort S · (reviewer severity: MINOR)

- **Locations**: `crates/pam/src/syslog.rs:14-32`, `crates/pam/src/lib.rs:82`, `crates/pam/src/lib.rs:289`
- **Evidence**: `init_panic_hook` calls `std::panic::set_hook` once per loaded image (syslog.rs:22-31) from every entry point. The hook closure and the `LAST_PANIC_LOC` thread-local live in pam_soos.so's mapping. There is no `take_hook`/restore. Linux-PAM unloads modules at `pam_end` (pam_test_runner.c:96 calls `pam_end`), after which the registered hook pointer refers to unmapped code.
- **Impact**: Replaces the host's own Rust panic hook (Rust display managers, other Rust PAM modules) and suppresses their stderr panic output; if any Rust code in the host panics after dlclose and before a new pam_soos load re-registers, the process jumps into unmapped memory (SIGSEGV). Low probability today, but the module must not leave process-wide state behind.
- **Recommendation**: In `catch_c_entry`: `let prev = std::panic::take_hook(); set_hook(...); let r = catch_unwind(..); drop(take_hook()); set_hook(prev);` (PAM entry points are synchronous so the swap is not racy in practice), or drop the hook entirely and log only the payload string (location is a nicety). Unit test: after `pam_sm_authenticate` returns, a test panic still reaches the default hook (capture via a custom prior hook).
- **Verifier notes**: Code fact holds: syslog.rs:22-31 registers a hook via `Once` and nothing ever calls `take_hook`; pam_test_runner.c:98 calls `pam_end` (Linux-PAM dlcloses modules there). The impact analysis is wrong, however: pam_soos.so is a `cdylib` (crates/pam/Cargo.toml:13) that statically links its own copy of libstd (no `prefer-dynamic` anywhere, grep exit 1), so the `HOOK` static it writes lives inside pam_soos.so's own image. It cannot replace the panic hook of a Rust host process or of another Rust PAM module (each has its own std copy), and after dlclose nothing outside the unloaded image references the hook, so the SIGSEGV scenario does not exist. Under the current `panic = "abort"` (PAM-01) the hook is also the only code that runs on a panic and it does not even syslog. Downgrade to SUGGESTION: simplify/remove the hook or make it log directly, but no process-wide contamination bug.

#### PAM-16 — Tautological truncation check, unused protocol helpers, unwired `PamHooks` impl and unreachable null check

GitHub issue: #264

**SUGGESTION** · CONFIRMED · dead-code · effort S

- **Locations**: `crates/pam/src/ipc.rs:360-367`, `crates/protocol/src/types.rs:328-352`, `crates/pam/src/ipc.rs:371-379`, `crates/pam/src/lib.rs:163-193`, `crates/pam/src/config.rs:170-174`, `crates/pam/build.rs:6`
- **Evidence**: ipc.rs:361 `total_received = 4 + declared_size` is compared with `total_capacity = declared_size + 4` (338-343) — always equal, the branch at 362-367 can never execute (real truncation is already reported by `read_exact_counted`). `Response::is_allow`, `matches_request`, `Verdict::should_ignore` (types.rs:328-352) are only used by tests/fuzz; the client re-implements the same checks inline (ipc.rs:371-379). `impl PamHooks for SoosPam` (lib.rs:163-193) is not connected to the exported symbols (`pam_hooks!` macro unused) and exists for PA11 tests only. config.rs:172-174 checks `argv.add(i).is_null()` on a pointer derived from a non-null base (unreachable). build.rs:6 only re-runs on build.rs changes, so a symlink to `libpam.so.0` created in OUT_DIR persists after `libpam0g-dev` is installed.
- **Impact**: Maintenance noise; two sources of truth for the Allow decision (client inline vs `Response::is_allow`) can diverge silently.
- **Recommendation**: Delete the dead branch; make the client call `resp.is_allow()`/`resp.matches_request(&req.request_id)` so the protocol crate owns the acceptance predicate (and PA rows can cite one function); either wire `PamHooks` through `pam_hooks!` or drop the impl and retarget PA11 tests at `authenticate_with_config`; remove the unreachable null check; add `cargo:rerun-if-env-changed`/`rerun-if-changed` for the candidate lib paths in build.rs.
- **Verifier notes**: ipc.rs:361 `total_received = 4 + declared_size` vs `total_capacity = declared_size + 4` (:338-343): identical by construction, branch :362-367 unreachable. `Response::is_allow/matches_request` and `Verdict::should_ignore` (types.rs:328-352) are referenced only in codec.rs unit tests, property_tests.rs:257, the fuzz target and policy tests, never by the PAM client (grep). `grep -rn 'pam_hooks!' crates tests` → none, so `impl PamHooks for SoosPam` (lib.rs:163-193) is not what the exported symbols call. config.rs:171-174 null-checks `argv.add(i)` derived from a non-null base. build.rs:6 only `rerun-if-changed=build.rs`. Correct; SUGGESTION.

#### PAM-17 — Silently ignored configuration errors: invalid timeout_ms, >256-byte arguments, and service names cut on a non-UTF-8 boundary fall back to defaults without any syslog line

GitHub issue: #265

**SUGGESTION** · CONFIRMED · improvement · effort S

- **Locations**: `crates/pam/src/config.rs:114-117`, `crates/pam/src/config.rs:135-142`, `crates/pam/src/config.rs:199-212`
- **Evidence**: `timeout_ms=abc` keeps 1000 ms (config.rs:114-117); an argument of ≥256 bytes (e.g. a long `socket_path=`) is dropped (config.rs:209-212); `service=` longer than 64 bytes where byte 64 is inside a multibyte char yields `None` from `s.get(..64)` and keeps "pam_soos" (138-141). None of these paths call `syslog::log_info`.
- **Impact**: Operator typos degrade to defaults (a wrong socket path means facial login never works with no diagnostic in the auth log).
- **Recommendation**: Emit one `LOG_AUTHPRIV|LOG_WARNING` line per rejected argument (never echo the value of unknown keys beyond the key name), and use `floor_char_boundary`-style truncation for `service=`. Unit tests asserting the returned config plus a recorded warning via a test hook.
- **Verifier notes**: config.rs:114-117 silently keeps the default on unparsable `timeout_ms`; :209-212 drops any argument ≥256 bytes; :138-141 `s.get(..bounded_len)` returns `None` when byte 64 is not a char boundary and leaves "pam_soos". No `syslog::log_info` in any of those paths (only call sites are lib.rs:86). Confirmed as described.

### 4.5 Privileged Daemon

*Coverage*: Read in full: crates/daemon/src/{main,lib,socket,peercred,session,dispatcher,pipeline,config,health,logging,mlock,error}.rs, packaging/soos-daemon.service, crates/daemon/Cargo.toml. Read targeted sections of: crates/daemon/tests/{config_tests,dispatcher_tests (518-612),hardening_tests (87-146),systemd_test,logging_audit_test}.rs and the test-function index of all 12 daemon test files; crates/inference-ort/src/pad.rs (78-200); crates/policy/src/{decision.rs 88-148, rate_limit.rs 195-275, threshold.rs new_raw}; crates/camera-v4l/src/{v4l_impl.rs 28-181, frame.rs 40-110, config.rs defaults}; crates/evidence-store/src/{store.rs 55-135, config.rs}; crates/protocol/src/types.rs (36-100, 305-345); crates/pam/src/ipc.rs (280-425); crates/gui/src/ipc_camera.rs (100-140); crates/admin-cli/src/status.rs (185-200); AI/ARCHITECTURE.md (§2-§4, §10-§12), AI/DECISIONS.md (grep), .agents/skills/dev-workflow/references/project-facts.md, auditor-agent and candid-reviewer skills, walkthroughs 66/72/74/75/77 (grep), Docs/IPC_PROTOCOL.md, Docs/MEMORY_PROTECTION_AND_SWAP.md, Docs/CAMERA_V4L_CRATE.md (grep), packaging PAM stacks and group-creation scripts (grep), git history of pipeline.rs (git log -L189,193). NOT covered: no cargo build/test was executed (shared target dir); camera-v4l streaming internals beyond spawn/is_ready/supervisor; evidence-store encryption and rotation internals; vision pipeline internals; PAM module beyond request/response construction; GUI beyond the preview client loop; tests/invariants.

<details><summary>Reviewer improvement notes</summary>

Ordered plan for the fix pass (each step is a separate topic branch following the Architect -> Tester -> Auditor -> Developer cycle; tests are written first and never weakened):

1. fix/pad-live-class-index (DMN-01, CRITICAL, S): make crates/daemon/src/pipeline.rs and crates/enrollment-cli/src/service.rs use `OrtPadDetector::new` (crate default). Tester: add `test_pipeline_pad_detector_uses_default_live_class_index` in crates/daemon/tests/pipeline_init_tests.rs and an equivalent in enrollment-cli tests. Before merging, run a manual validation against the real minifasnet_v2_80x80.onnx (live capture vs phone replay) and record the observed softmax vectors in a new ADR in AI/DECISIONS.md (fixing the stale "class 0" entry), since walkthroughs 66 and 72 contradict each other.

2. fix/daemon-authorization-gaps (DMN-02, DMN-03, DMN-09, MAJOR, M): in dispatcher.rs move PreviewFrame after peer verification and require root or matching UID plus an active local session; in handle_event require `peer_uid == 0 || Some(peer_uid) == event.uid` and rate-limit events per peer UID; in session.rs require `REMOTE=0`, `CLASS=user`, non-empty `SEAT=`; carry `peer.pid` through and add a loginuid check (`/proc/<pid>/loginuid`) behind a test hook. Evidence-store: global daily cap and pruning of `daily_counts`. Tests: mismatched-UID preview -> ProtocolError; spoofed event uid -> no snapshot; REMOTE=1 session -> rejected; loginuid mismatch -> rejected. Add ADR for preview-over-IPC and update ARCHITECTURE.md:303.

3. fix/daemon-budget-and-inference (DMN-05, DMN-06, DMN-10, MAJOR, M): introduce a single hard deadline computed at the start of `read_and_process`; run the vision stage in `spawn_blocking` behind a `Semaphore::new(1)`; check remaining time against an EMA of inference duration before each iteration; reserve/record the rate-limit attempt under the write lock before camera work and honor its result. Tests: slow mock vision always yields a wire response (never EOF) within connection_timeout; concurrent Status stays responsive during two auths; `max_attempts=1` with two concurrent auths yields at most one non-RateLimited verdict. Update ARCHITECTURE.md:117 to match.

4. fix/daemon-connection-caps (DMN-04, MAJOR, M): per-peer-UID permits, reserved root permits, max requests/lifetime per connection, peer_uid in limit logs. Test: 8 non-root polling connections cannot starve a root Auth.

5. fix/daemon-config-validation (DMN-08, DMN-16, MAJOR/MINOR, S): `DaemonConfig::validate()` with the ranges listed in DMN-08; unify warmup_frames default; negative tests for each rule; socket test refusing 0o666.

6. fix/daemon-health-status (DMN-07, MAJOR, S): Status derives camera_ready from `camera.is_ready()`; add last-frame-age to a v2 StatusResponse if protocol change is acceptable; mock-camera tests for both states. This is the piece most relevant to the user's "camera badly detected" report, alongside the camera-area findings.

7. chore/daemon-hardening (DMN-13, DMN-14, DMN-11, DMN-12, MINOR, M): systemd directives (CapabilityBoundingSet, SystemCallFilter, Protect*, PrivateNetwork, SupplementaryGroups=video, Before=display-manager.service) validated with `systemd-analyze security` on a real host and mirrored in systemd_test.rs; remove runtime groupadd; fd-based chmod fallback; truncated request_id logging with an audit-test pattern; either wire or remove LockedBuffer and fix Docs/MEMORY_PROTECTION_AND_SWAP.md; warn on mlockall failure.

8. chore/daemon-docs (DMN-17, DMN-18, DMN-19, S): add Docs/DAEMON.md (config reference, connection model, per-kind authorization), update Docs/IPC_PROTOCOL.md (Status/PreviewFrame, persistent loop, message disambiguation), define the response expiry contract with the PAM area, and add graceful drain + panic hook.

9. feat/protocol-message-tag (DMN-15, later, M): protocol v2 top-level message enum with fuzz coverage; coordinate with the protocol and PAM areas.

</details>

#### DMN-01 — Daemon (and enrollment CLI) hardcode MiniFASNet live class index 2 (ScreenReplay) instead of the verified default 1

GitHub issue: #146

**CRITICAL** · CONFIRMED · security · effort S

- **Locations**: `crates/daemon/src/pipeline.rs:189-193`, `crates/enrollment-cli/src/service.rs:773`, `crates/inference-ort/src/pad.rs:81-82`, `crates/gui/src/main.rs:92`
- **Evidence**: pipeline.rs:189-193: `OrtPadDetector::new_with_class_index(pad_session, config.vision.pad_threshold, 2)`. enrollment-cli/src/service.rs:773: `OrtPadDetector::new_with_class_index(pad_session, 0.80, 2)`. pad.rs:81-82: `/// Default live class index for MiniFASNetV2 models (Class 0: PrintPhoto, Class 1: Genuine Live, Class 2: ScreenReplay). pub const DEFAULT_MINIFASNET_LIVE_CLASS_INDEX: usize = 1;`. gui/src/main.rs:92 uses `OrtPadDetector::new(pad_session, 0.80)` (default 1). `git log -L189,193:crates/daemon/src/pipeline.rs`: 5659151 (#124) set `1`; 084ce5c (#127) changed `-1 / +2`; 72469ba (#135, walkthrough 72) changed only the crate constant back to 1. AI/walkthroughs/72_anti_spoof_ir_stability_gdm.md:20: "Because index 2 was treated as genuine live, the system assigned high live confidence to screen replays, enabling attacks." project-facts.md §2/§4: live class index **1**. interpret_probabilities (pad.rs:146) reads `probs.get(live_class_index)`, so the daemon's `p_live` is the ScreenReplay probability. No daemon test asserts the index used by `initialize_pipeline` (pipeline_init_tests.rs only checks startup/mock/missing-models). Walkthrough 66:20 reports the opposite empirical ordering, so the two walkthroughs contradict each other.
- **Impact**: With index 2, a phone/monitor replay yields high p_live and passes PAD, while a genuine face gets p_live from the replay column and is denied (PadFailed). The daemon and the enrollment CLI disagree with the GUI and the crate contract. This directly matches the user's reported spoofing weakness.
- **Recommendation**: Use `OrtPadDetector::new(pad_session, config.vision.pad_threshold)` (crate default) in pipeline.rs and enrollment-cli/service.rs; never pass a literal. Add `test_pipeline_pad_detector_uses_default_live_class_index` in crates/daemon/tests/pipeline_init_tests.rs asserting `pad.live_class_index() == DEFAULT_MINIFASNET_LIVE_CLASS_INDEX` (expose the detector or assert via a constructor helper). Because walkthroughs 66 and 72 contradict each other, validate the ordering against the real minifasnet_v2_80x80.onnx with a live capture and a screen replay (project-facts §4) and record the result in an ADR in AI/DECISIONS.md (which still says class 0).
- **Verifier notes**: Re-opened crates/daemon/src/pipeline.rs:189-193: `OrtPadDetector::new_with_class_index(pad_session, config.vision.pad_threshold, 2)`. crates/enrollment-cli/src/service.rs:773: `new_with_class_index(pad_session, 0.80, 2)`. crates/gui/src/main.rs:92: `OrtPadDetector::new(pad_session, 0.80)` (default). crates/inference-ort/src/pad.rs:81-82: `DEFAULT_MINIFASNET_LIVE_CLASS_INDEX: usize = 1` with doc 'Class 1: Genuine Live'; pad.rs:146 reads `probs.get(live_class_index)`. `git log -L189,193:crates/daemon/src/pipeline.rs` confirms 5659151 (#124) set 1, 084ce5c (#127) changed to 2. `git show 72469ba --stat` + diff of daemon files: #135 touched pipeline.rs only for DECISION_BUDGET_MS (220->900) and dispatcher.rs for the wake timeout; it changed pad.rs to 1 and rewrote pad_tests.rs (`test_pad_default_live_class_index_is_one`, pad_tests.rs:298) but left the daemon/enrollment literals at 2, so production disagrees with the crate contract and the GUI. `grep -rn live_class_index crates` shows no daemon test asserting the index. Walkthrough 66:16-20 and 72:15-20 do contradict each other; AI/DECISIONS.md:20 still says class 0; project-facts.md:52,76 say 1. CAVEAT for the fix pass (not verifiable statically in this session, no onnxruntime available): the empirical ordering for the shipped file (/var/lib/soos/models/minifasnet_v2_80x80.onnx, manifest source QingHeYang fork) was not re-measured. Upstream Silent-Face-Anti-Spoofing uses label 1 = real, which supports index 1, but walkthrough 66's measurement may have been taken on the IR sensor (CameraConfig::default() is `SensorPreference::PreferIr`, camera-v4l/src/config.rs:46; Grey frames are replicated to RGB in vision/src/color.rs:49) where the RGB-trained model may score differently. Whichever index is correct, the daemon/enrollment/GUI divergence on the liveness class is a security defect; severity stands.

#### DMN-02 — PreviewFrame streams raw camera frames to any socket peer without UID, root, session or rate-limit checks

GitHub issue: #143

**MAJOR** · CONFIRMED · security · effort M

- **Locations**: `crates/daemon/src/dispatcher.rs:347-413`, `crates/daemon/src/dispatcher.rs:415-461`, `AI/ARCHITECTURE.md:303`
- **Evidence**: dispatcher.rs:347 `if req.kind == RequestKind::PreviewFrame {` returns `frame.data.clone()` (line 375) and `encode_preview` (391) BEFORE Step 6 peer verification (`verify_peer_credentials`, line 422) and Step 6b session validation (line 445, which is additionally gated on `req.kind == RequestKind::Auth`). No rate limit applies (the `check_allowed` call is at line 509, inside the Auth path). Line 349 `pipe.camera.notify_activity()` is called unconditionally, defeating auto-standby. Neither `peer_uid` nor `uid_hint` is logged for preview. `PreviewResponse.data` (Vec<u8>) and the encoded buffer are never zeroized. ARCHITECTURE.md:303 lists "Transmitting camera frames or biometric embeddings across the IPC socket" as a pitfall to avoid; DECISIONS.md has no ADR for the preview feature (grep 'preview' returns nothing). GUI client (gui/src/ipc_camera.rs:118-125) sends `uid_hint: uid` but the daemon ignores it.
- **Impact**: Any account in the `soos` group (socket is 0660 root:soos), including one logged in over SSH, can continuously pull live webcam frames of whoever is in front of the machine (lock screen included) at ~30 FPS, keep the camera LED/stream on indefinitely, and load the daemon. Frames are biometric data per the threat model (ARCHITECTURE.md:31).
- **Recommendation**: Move the PreviewFrame branch after peer verification; require `peer.uid == 0 || peer.uid == req.uid_hint` AND an active local (non-remote, seated) session for that UID; apply a per-UID token bucket to preview requests; log `peer_uid` at info; zeroize the cloned data/encoded buffer (`Zeroizing<Vec<u8>>`). Add dispatcher test: peer UID != uid_hint (use a uid different from `getuid()`) on PreviewFrame must receive `Verdict::ProtocolError/UidMismatch` and no frame; add an ADR documenting the preview exception to ARCHITECTURE.md:303.
- **Verifier notes**: dispatcher.rs:347-413: PreviewFrame branch runs before Step 6 `verify_peer_credentials` (line 422) and Step 6b session check (line 445, gated on `req.kind == RequestKind::Auth`). Line 349 `pipe.camera.notify_activity()` unconditional; line 375 `frame.data.clone()` into `PreviewResponse.data` (plain Vec<u8>; only `Request` implements Zeroize in protocol/src/types.rs:96, `Frame` zeroizes on Drop in camera-v4l/src/frame.rs:101 but the clone does not). `Request::validate()` (protocol/src/types.rs:312-327) checks only version and service length. Rate limiting only in the Auth path (`check_allowed`, dispatcher.rs:509). GUI sends `uid_hint: getuid()` (gui/src/ipc_camera.rs:101,122) so a `peer.uid == uid_hint || peer.uid == 0` rule is compatible. Existing tests (dispatcher_tests.rs:518-612) only exercise the current uid; no authorization test. ARCHITECTURE.md:303 lists frames over IPC as a pitfall; no ADR (grep 'preview' AI/DECISIONS.md returns nothing). Socket is 0660 root:soos (config.rs:30-32; unit RuntimeDirectoryMode=0750), so any soos-group member, local or SSH, can stream frames.

#### DMN-03 — PasswordFailed events trust the payload UID and are unauthenticated; evidence cap is per attacker-chosen UID with an unbounded counter map

GitHub issue: #175

**MAJOR** · CONFIRMED · security · effort M

- **Locations**: `crates/daemon/src/dispatcher.rs:265-304`, `crates/evidence-store/src/store.rs:29`, `crates/evidence-store/src/store.rs:105-118`
- **Evidence**: dispatcher.rs:272 `let target_uid = event.uid.unwrap_or(peer_uid);` then `store_snapshot(target_uid, "PasswordFailed", &frame.data, None, None)` (281-287). No call to `verify_peer_credentials` and no session check in `handle_event`; events are not rate limited (policy `check_allowed` is only in the Auth path). evidence-store/src/store.rs:29 `daily_counts: Mutex<HashMap<(u32, String), u32>>`; lines 105-118 enforce `daily_cap_per_uid` (default 10, config.rs:9) keyed by `(uid, date)` only; there is no global cap. PAM sends `uid: Some(uid)` (pam/src/ipc.rs:404-405) from a root peer, so a strict check would not break the legitimate path.
- **Impact**: When evidence capture is enabled (opt-in), any soos-group member can (a) photograph whoever is in front of the camera at will, (b) attribute the snapshot to an arbitrary UID (false intrusion evidence against another user), and (c) bypass the daily cap by iterating UIDs (2^31 valid values), filling /var/lib/soos/evidence with ~W*H*3-byte encrypted blobs and growing the in-memory HashMap without bound until restart.
- **Recommendation**: In `handle_event`: reject unless `peer_uid == 0 || Some(peer_uid) == event.uid`; use `peer_uid` when the event uid is None; add a per-peer-UID rate limit for events (reuse RateLimiter); in evidence-store add a global daily cap and prune `daily_counts` for past dates (bound the map). Tests: non-root peer sending `uid: Some(other)` must not create a snapshot; N events with distinct uids must stop at the global cap; the counter map size must not exceed a bound.
- **Verifier notes**: dispatcher.rs:265-304: `handle_event` has no peer verification; line 272 `event.uid.unwrap_or(peer_uid)`; `store_snapshot(target_uid, ...)` at 281-287. evidence-store/src/store.rs:29 `daily_counts: Mutex<HashMap<(u32, String), u32>>`; cap enforced per `(uid, date)` at 107-118 (entry incremented before the write); the only other access is the read at store.rs:224 (`daily_count`), no pruning anywhere; `MAX_VALID_UID = 2_147_483_647` (store.rs:23,74) bounds uid but leaves 2^31 keys. PAM sends `uid: Some(uid)` from a root peer (pam/src/ipc.rs:400-405), so the recommended `peer_uid == 0 || Some(peer_uid) == event.uid` rule keeps the legitimate path. Impact is gated on `evidence.enabled` (opt-in, default false per config_tests.rs:170 assertion), which is why MAJOR rather than CRITICAL is right; disk fill via distinct UIDs is the realistic DoS, the HashMap growth is secondary.

#### DMN-04 — No per-UID connection cap or per-connection request limit: one unprivileged user can hold all 8 permits and disable face login for everyone

GitHub issue: #157

**MAJOR** · CONFIRMED · reliability · effort M

- **Locations**: `crates/daemon/src/dispatcher.rs:121-127`, `crates/daemon/src/dispatcher.rs:130-176`, `crates/daemon/src/config.rs:53-54`, `AI/ARCHITECTURE.md:100`
- **Evidence**: dispatcher.rs:121 `self.semaphore.clone().try_acquire_owned()` is a single global semaphore (`max_concurrent_connections: 8`, config.rs:53). The persistent loop (130-176) has no maximum request count or connection lifetime; a client that sends any request (Status/PreviewFrame, which need no authorization) at least every `connection_timeout` (1000ms) keeps the permit forever (line 136 `timeout(self.config.connection_timeout, ...)` restarts per request). Requests from a root PAM peer arriving while permits are exhausted get `ConcurrencyLimitReached` (line 125) and the stream is dropped. ARCHITECTURE.md:100 promises "Strict per-UID rate limits and global concurrent connection caps"; only Auth is rate limited (dispatcher.rs:509).
- **Impact**: A single soos-group account (e.g. over SSH) opens 8 connections and sends Status every 900ms: every gdm/sudo/login face attempt then gets EOF -> PAM_IGNORE. Fail-closed, but a trivial local denial of the feature for all users, indefinitely and without any log identifying the peer.
- **Recommendation**: Add per-peer-UID connection permits (e.g. 2) keyed on `SO_PEERCRED` uid, reserve at least 2 permits for root peers (PAM), cap requests per connection (e.g. 1 for Auth, N for preview) and total connection lifetime, and log `peer_uid` on limit rejections. Test: 8 non-root connections idle-polling Status must not prevent a 9th root-peer Auth from being served (simulate root reservation via a config knob for the peer uid used in tests).
- **Verifier notes**: dispatcher.rs:121-127: single global `Semaphore` (`max_concurrent_connections: 8`, config.rs:53) acquired with `try_acquire_owned` before peer credentials are even read (line 186), so a root PAM peer is rejected identically; line 124 warn has no peer identity. Loop 130-176: `timeout(connection_timeout, read_and_process)` restarts per request (line 136), no request count or lifetime cap; Status/PreviewFrame need no authorization (see DMN-02), so a Status every <1000ms holds a permit indefinitely. ARCHITECTURE.md:100 promises per-UID limits and global caps; only Auth is rate limited (509). No test covers starvation (policy_concurrency_tests.rs tests listed at 69-217 do not).

#### DMN-05 — CPU inference runs synchronously on Tokio worker threads with no spawn_blocking and no inference semaphore

GitHub issue: #158

**MAJOR** · CONFIRMED · performance · effort M

- **Locations**: `crates/daemon/src/dispatcher.rs:668-671`, `crates/vision/src/pipeline.rs:281`, `crates/inference-ort/src/pad.rs:85`, `AI/ARCHITECTURE.md:117`, `crates/daemon/src/main.rs:36`
- **Evidence**: dispatcher.rs:668-671 `match pipe.vision.process_frame(&frame)` is called directly inside `async fn handle_request`; `VisionPipeline::process_frame` (vision/pipeline.rs:281) is a plain synchronous fn running SCRFD + PAD + ArcFace on CPU. The ORT session is `Arc<Mutex<Session>>` (pad.rs:85), so concurrent auths block additional worker threads on a std Mutex. main.rs:36 `#[tokio::main]` (multi-thread runtime, workers = CPU count). ARCHITECTURE.md:117: "CPU-heavy capture and inference run on dedicated worker threads with semaphore limit 1 to avoid thread exhaustion." `timeout(...)` at line 136 cannot cancel a future whose worker is blocked in inference.
- **Impact**: On a 2-4 vCPU machine, two or more concurrent Auth requests (e.g. gdm + a lock-screen retry, or an attacker's connections) stall the whole runtime: accept loop, other connections, Tokio timers and the preview stream all freeze until inference completes; deadlines documented as strict are not enforced during that window.
- **Recommendation**: Wrap the per-frame vision stage in `tokio::task::spawn_blocking` guarded by a `Semaphore::new(1)` (inference gate) held in `PipelineComponents`; return `Unavailable/InternalError` if the gate cannot be acquired before the remaining budget. Test in policy_concurrency_tests.rs: with a mock vision that sleeps 300ms, two concurrent Auths plus a concurrent Status request; the Status response must arrive in < 100ms.
- **Verifier notes**: dispatcher.rs:668-671 calls `pipe.vision.process_frame(&frame)` inline in `async fn handle_request`; vision/src/pipeline.rs:281 `pub fn process_frame(&self, frame: &Frame) -> Result<...>` is synchronous; `grep -rn 'spawn_blocking|block_in_place' crates/daemon/src` returns nothing; pad.rs:85 `session: Arc<Mutex<Session>>`; main.rs:36 `#[tokio::main]` (multi-thread). ARCHITECTURE.md:117 describes 'dedicated worker threads with semaphore limit 1' that do not exist. A blocked worker cannot be cancelled by `timeout` at line 136 (the future is not polled during inference). Confirmed as described.

#### DMN-06 — Multi-frame loop budget ignores time already spent under the outer timeout and starts inference with no remaining-time margin, so slow attempts are cancelled before any response is written

GitHub issue: #159

**MAJOR** · PARTIAL · bug · effort M

- **Locations**: `crates/daemon/src/dispatcher.rs:136-140`, `crates/daemon/src/dispatcher.rs:504`, `crates/daemon/src/dispatcher.rs:607-628`, `crates/daemon/src/dispatcher.rs:646-648`, `crates/daemon/src/dispatcher.rs:783-787`, `crates/daemon/src/pipeline.rs:19`
- **Evidence**: Outer bound: line 136 `timeout(self.config.connection_timeout, self.read_and_process(&mut stream))` starts before peer-cred, reads, decode, session-dir scan. Inner budget: line 504 `let auth_start = Instant::now();` (after all of that) and 616-620 `max_allowed_budget = connection_timeout - 50ms; total_budget = client_budget.min(max_allowed_budget)`. Loop 628 `while auth_start.elapsed() < total_budget` and 646 only checks `cur_ns >= req.deadline_monotonic_ns` BEFORE an iteration; an iteration started at 940ms runs the full synchronous pipeline (100-400ms on CPU) and overruns the 1000ms outer timeout. When the outer timeout fires, `handle_connection` gets `Err(_)` (line 154) and returns `DaemonError::Timeout` with no response written; the post-loop `evaluate_with_rate_limit` (786) is never reached so the attempt is not recorded. `client_budget = deadline - now` (608-615) reserves no margin for write + PAM read; with the PAM default `timeout_ms=1000` the client deadline and the daemon budget coincide. DECISION_BUDGET_MS = 900 (pipeline.rs:19) is only used when the client sends no deadline.
- **Impact**: Any attempt that does not match early (wrong face, no face, PAD fail) runs to the end of the budget and is likely cancelled: PAM receives EOF (PAM_IGNORE) instead of a Deny with a ReasonClass, which breaks the lock-screen feedback introduced in #137 and leaves the rate limiter unrecorded (each cancelled attempt is free). With the 1000ms PAM default the response, even when written, arrives after the PAM deadline.
- **Recommendation**: Compute a single `hard_deadline = min(client_deadline - write_margin(~50ms), outer_timeout_start + connection_timeout - write_margin)` captured at the start of `read_and_process`; before each inference check `remaining >= last_inference_duration (EMA) + margin`, otherwise finalize with the last verdict; record the attempt in the rate limiter before the loop (or in a `Drop` guard) so cancellation cannot skip it. Test: mock vision sleeping 300ms with `connection_timeout = 1000ms` and `deadline = now + 1s` must always produce a decoded Deny response on the wire (never EOF), and the rate limiter must count the attempt.
- **Verifier notes**: Core confirmed: dispatcher.rs:608-620 budget = `min(client_deadline - now, connection_timeout - 50ms)` with no write/PAM-read margin; loop 628-648 checks time only before an iteration; inference (synchronous) can start at budget-1ms and overrun. PAM: default `timeout_ms = 1000` (pam/src/config.rs:9), but every packaged stack passes `timeout_ms=250` (packaging/pam/arch/system-auth:5, fedora/soos/system-auth:12, debian/soos:7-9), so in deployed configs the daemon budget (~250ms) equals the PAM total budget and a single 100-400ms inference already overruns. Mechanism is misdescribed though: the outer `timeout` cannot cancel a worker blocked in sync inference; after the overrunning iteration `auth_start.elapsed() >= total_budget` so the loop exits, `evaluate_with_rate_limit` (786) IS called and the response IS built and written (tokio `Timeout` polls the inner future first and returns its Ready value). Cancellation at the sleep (778) is practically unreachable because the loop exits at <=950ms of the 1000ms outer window. Net effect for PAM is the same (response arrives after the PAM deadline -> read timeout -> PAM_IGNORE, lock-screen feedback lost), but the 'attempt not recorded' and 'never reaches evaluate_with_rate_limit' claims are wrong. Also note walkthrough 72 §4 ties DECISION_BUDGET_MS=900 to a '1000ms PAM deadline' while packaging still says 250ms: this drift is the bigger contributor and is missing from the finding.

#### DMN-07 — Health/Status reports camera_ready=true unconditionally; camera absence or standby is never reflected

GitHub issue: #153

**MAJOR** · CONFIRMED · bug · effort S

- **Locations**: `crates/daemon/src/main.rs:61-66`, `crates/daemon/src/dispatcher.rs:327-344`, `crates/camera-v4l/src/v4l_impl.rs:31-68`, `crates/camera-v4l/src/v4l_impl.rs:132-181`
- **Evidence**: main.rs:63 `health.set_camera_ready(true);` runs after `initialize_pipeline` returns Ok. `V4lCameraManager::spawn` (v4l_impl.rs:31-68) only spawns the supervisor thread and cannot fail on a missing/busy device; the supervisor (132-181) retries `open_and_stream` with backoff up to `max_backoff` (5s) forever. Nothing in the daemon calls `health.set_camera_ready(false)` afterwards, and the Status handler (dispatcher.rs:327-344) copies `status.camera_ready` from `HealthState` rather than querying `pipe.camera.is_ready()`.
- **Impact**: `soos-admin status` and the GUI show camera_ready=true / is_healthy=true while the camera is unplugged, held by another process, or failing to negotiate a format. This misleads exactly the diagnostics the user relies on when "the camera is badly detected"; auth requests then return CameraUnavailable with a healthy status.
- **Recommendation**: In the Status handler compute `camera_ready = pipe.camera.is_ready()` (and expose a `camera_streaming`/`last_frame_age_ms` field in a v2 StatusResponse); alternatively a lightweight periodic task updating HealthState from `is_ready()`. Test: mock camera constructed not-ready -> Status.camera_ready == false; after readiness -> true.
- **Verifier notes**: main.rs:61-66 `health.set_camera_ready(true)` after `initialize_pipeline` Ok; `grep -rn set_camera_ready crates` shows no other production caller (only tests; pipeline_integration_tests.rs:165 even does `set_camera_ready(camera.is_ready())`). Status handler dispatcher.rs:327-344 copies `status.camera_ready` from HealthState. camera-v4l/src/v4l_impl.rs:31-68 `spawn` only spawns the supervisor thread (cannot fail on missing device); supervisor 132-181 retries forever with backoff, storing `is_ready=false`. So Status/`is_healthy` are true with no camera. One correction for the fix: `is_ready()` is also false during auto-standby (supervisor `Suspend` branch stores false), so deriving `camera_ready` directly from `is_ready()` would report an idle-but-healthy daemon as unhealthy; the fix must distinguish standby from failure.

#### DMN-09 — Active-session check accepts remote (SSH) sessions and never ties the request to the caller's own session, so a root-peer request from another local user (su/sudo) is verified against the victim at the desk

GitHub issue: #160

**MAJOR** · CONFIRMED · security · effort M

- **Locations**: `crates/daemon/src/session.rs:169-184`, `crates/daemon/src/peercred.rs:51-63`, `crates/daemon/src/dispatcher.rs:416-421`, `AI/ARCHITECTURE.md:97-98`
- **Evidence**: session.rs:175-181 only parse `UID=` and `ACTIVE=1`/`STATE=active`; `REMOTE=`, `CLASS=`, `SEAT=`, `TYPE=` are ignored, so an SSH session file (REMOTE=1) satisfies "active session". peercred.rs:55 `if peer.uid == 0 || peer.uid == requested_uid { Ok(()) }` lets any root peer target any UID. dispatcher.rs:416-421 rebuilds `PeerCredentials { uid, gid: 0, pid: None }`, discarding the kernel-provided `pid` that could identify the caller's session/loginuid. ARCHITECTURE.md:98 requires the target UID to "own the active local graphical session". Packaging places pam_soos in the shared auth stacks used by su/sudo (packaging/pam/arch/system-auth:5, packaging/pam/debian/soos:7-9, packaging/pam/fedora/soos/system-auth:12).
- **Impact**: Local user A (in scope of the threat model, ARCHITECTURE.md:36) logged in via SSH or a second TTY runs `su B` or `sudo -u B` while B is sitting in front of the camera: su is setuid root so the peer is uid 0, B has an active session, the camera sees B, and the daemon returns Allow -> A obtains B's shell without B's password. Also enables face auth for purely remote sessions, contrary to the local-only design.
- **Recommendation**: (1) Require `REMOTE=0`, `CLASS=user` and a non-empty `SEAT=` for the target UID's session. (2) Use `peer.pid` to read `/proc/<pid>/loginuid` (audit login uid) and require it to be unset (4294967295), 0, or equal to `uid_hint`; a `su`/`sudo` process started by A carries loginuid A and is rejected. (3) Record the residual risk in an ADR and Docs/PAM_MODULE.md. Tests: session file with REMOTE=1 rejected; a synthetic loginuid mismatch rejected (inject via a `PeerCredentials` with pid and a test hook for the loginuid reader).
- **Verifier notes**: session.rs:169-184 parses only `UID=` and `ACTIVE=1`/`STATE=active`; `grep -rn 'REMOTE|SEAT|CLASS' crates/daemon/src crates/daemon/tests` returns nothing. peercred.rs:55 `peer.uid == 0 || peer.uid == requested_uid`. dispatcher.rs:416-421 rebuilds `PeerCredentials { pid: None }` discarding the kernel pid from get_peer_credentials (peercred.rs:39). PAM side has no PAM_RHOST/PAM_TTY check (`grep -rn -i 'rhost|PAM_TTY|remote|loginuid' crates/pam/src` empty). pam_soos is in the shared stacks used by su/sudo: packaging/pam/arch/system-auth:5, fedora/soos/system-auth:12, and Debian via `pam-auth-update --enable soos` (packaging/debian/postinst:55 -> common-auth). logind marks seatless (SSH) sessions active, so the su/sudo -u scenario against the user at the desk holds. Corrections for the recommendation: the proposed `/proc/<pid>/loginuid` check conflicts with the `ProtectProc=invisible` proposed in DMN-14 (the daemon would no longer see other processes' /proc entries and would have to fail closed for everyone), and SO_PEERCRED's pid is subject to PID reuse; also note that at the initial GDM login the target user has no logind session yet, so requiring CLASS=user/SEAT= does not change that path.

#### DMN-08 — Configuration is accepted without validation: world-writable socket mode, zero timeouts/permits, and non-finite or zero thresholds are all possible from daemon.toml

GitHub issue: #199

**MINOR** · PARTIAL · security · effort S · (reviewer severity: MAJOR)

- **Locations**: `crates/daemon/src/config.rs:212-214`, `crates/daemon/src/config.rs:224-229`, `crates/daemon/src/config.rs:292-301`, `crates/daemon/src/config.rs:303-314`, `crates/policy/src/threshold.rs:28-33`, `crates/daemon/src/socket.rs:260`
- **Evidence**: config.rs:212-214 copies `socket_mode` verbatim; socket.rs:260 applies it via `Mode::from_bits_truncate(config.socket_mode)`; a TOML line `socket_mode = 438` (0o666) yields a world-writable socket, which AGENTS.md lists as a strict prohibition. config.rs:224-229 accept `max_concurrent_connections = 0` (every connection rejected) and `connection_timeout_ms = 0` (every request times out; wake budget `saturating_sub(100ms)` becomes 0). config.rs:292-301 build thresholds with `ThresholdConfig::new_raw` (threshold.rs:28-33, no checks) and mirror them into `vision.match_threshold/pad_threshold`; `match_threshold = 0.0` makes `evaluate` (decision.rs:126 `ctx.score < match_threshold`) Allow any live single face, `NaN`/negative values are not rejected. `rate_limit.max_attempts = 0` (303-306) makes `check_allowed` fail for every UID (rate_limit.rs:202). No `validate()` exists and config_tests.rs has no negative test beyond invalid TOML syntax (line 173).
- **Impact**: A single mistyped line silently turns the daemon into a world-writable or universally-allowing service, or into a service that rejects everything; no startup error is logged.
- **Recommendation**: Add `DaemonConfig::validate()` called from `load_or_default`: socket_mode must have no `o` bits and be one of 0o660/0o600; connection_timeout_ms in [100, 10_000]; max_concurrent_connections >= 1; thresholds finite and in (0.0, 1.0]; rate-limit max_attempts >= 1 and window >= 1s; retention_days >= 1; log_level parseable by `EnvFilter::try_new`. Tests for each rejection in config_tests.rs, plus a socket test that a 0o666 mode is refused before bind.
- **Verifier notes**: Confirmed no validation: config.rs:212-214 copies `socket_mode`; socket.rs:260 `Mode::from_bits_truncate(config.socket_mode)`; config.rs:224-229 accept 0 for permits/timeout; 292-301 use `ThresholdConfig::new_raw` (threshold.rs:28-33 unchecked) and mirror into vision; decision.rs:126 `ctx.score < match_threshold` so 0.0 or NaN thresholds allow any live single face (TOML 1.0 accepts `nan`); rate_limit.rs:202 rejects all when `max_attempts == 0`. config_tests.rs has only the syntax-error negative test (line 173) and no `validate`. Severity lowered: /etc/soos/daemon.toml is root-owned input (root attackers are out of scope per ARCHITECTURE.md:34), and a 0666 socket mode is still unreachable for 'other' because the runtime dir is 0750 root:soos (unit RuntimeDirectoryMode=0750, socket.rs:424 rejects world-writable dirs). Fail-closed startup validation is still the right hardening.

#### DMN-10 — Rate-limit check/record is not atomic and the recorded result is discarded on the Allow path

GitHub issue: #200

**MINOR** · CONFIRMED · security · effort S

- **Locations**: `crates/daemon/src/dispatcher.rs:507-526`, `crates/daemon/src/dispatcher.rs:756-758`, `crates/daemon/src/dispatcher.rs:783-798`
- **Evidence**: Line 508 `pipe.policy.read().await; engine.check_allowed(...)` (read lock) and only later line 756-757 `let mut engine_write = pipe.policy.write().await; let _ = engine_write.evaluate_with_rate_limit(&last_ctx, cur_ns);` — the `(Verdict, ReasonClass)` returned (which is `ProtocolError/RateLimited` when `check_and_record` fails, decision.rs:139-142) is ignored and the response is still built with the pre-computed `verdict == Allow` (line 760-765). Post-loop (783-798) the recorded verdict is only used when `last_verdict == Allow`, which is impossible there.
- **Impact**: N concurrent Auth requests for one UID (up to 8) all pass `check_allowed` with one remaining attempt and can all be granted; cancelled attempts (DMN-06) are never recorded. The limiter is weaker than the documented per-UID cap; concurrency is bounded so exploitation is limited to a small overshoot.
- **Recommendation**: Take the write lock once at the start of the Auth path and call `check_and_record` (or a `reserve_attempt`) before any camera/vision work; honor the returned verdict on the Allow path. Test: two simultaneous Auths with `max_attempts = 1` must yield at most one non-RateLimited verdict.
- **Verifier notes**: dispatcher.rs:507-526 `check_allowed` under `policy.read()`; on Allow, 756-757 `let _ = engine_write.evaluate_with_rate_limit(&last_ctx, cur_ns);` discards the `(Verdict, ReasonClass)` and 760-765 encode the pre-computed `verdict` (Allow). decision.rs:139-142 shows `evaluate_with_rate_limit` returns `ProtocolError/RateLimited` when `check_and_record` fails, which is thus ignored on the Allow path. Post-loop 789-798 only use the recorded verdict when `last_verdict == Allow`, unreachable there. Overshoot bounded by the 8-permit semaphore; MINOR is right.

#### DMN-12 — LockedBuffer / mlock_slice are unused in production while docs present them as an active protection layer; mlockall failure is only logged at debug

GitHub issue: #201

**MINOR** · CONFIRMED · dead-code · effort S

- **Locations**: `crates/daemon/src/mlock.rs:14-124`, `crates/daemon/src/main.rs:51-55`, `Docs/MEMORY_PROTECTION_AND_SWAP.md:37-65`
- **Evidence**: `grep -rn "LockedBuffer\|mlock_slice\|mlock::" crates/*/src` (excluding mlock.rs) returns only main.rs:51 (`mlock_process_address_space`). Docs/MEMORY_PROTECTION_AND_SWAP.md:37 "Layer 2: Granular Buffer Locking (mlock / LockedBuffer)" and :63-65 show `LockedBuffer::new(master_key_bytes)` as usage. main.rs:54 logs the mlockall failure with `tracing::debug!` claiming "continuing with granular buffer protection" that does not exist.
- **Impact**: Documentation overstates the memory protection; if `mlockall` fails (e.g. a future `CapabilityBoundingSet` without CAP_IPC_LOCK, or a container), keys/embeddings/frames are swappable and the operator gets no visible warning.
- **Recommendation**: Either wire `LockedBuffer` around the master keys (biometric/evidence) and the decrypted template, or delete it and correct the doc. Raise the mlockall failure to `warn!` and expose it in Status. Keep hardening_tests for whichever path remains.
- **Verifier notes**: `grep -rn 'LockedBuffer|mlock_slice|mlock::' crates/*/src crates/*/tests` excluding mlock.rs: only main.rs:51 (`mlock_process_address_space`) and hardening_tests.rs:15-44. Docs/MEMORY_PROTECTION_AND_SWAP.md:37-40 present 'Layer 2: Granular Buffer Locking (mlock / LockedBuffer)' and :60-65 show `LockedBuffer::new(master_key_bytes)` usage that does not exist. main.rs:54 logs mlockall failure at `tracing::debug!` claiming 'continuing with granular buffer protection'.

#### DMN-13 — Socket binding: path-based chmod fallback follows symlinks, and a runtime `groupadd` subprocess is spawned from the auth daemon

GitHub issue: #202

**MINOR** · CONFIRMED · security · effort S

- **Locations**: `crates/daemon/src/socket.rs:267-272`, `crates/daemon/src/socket.rs:103-116`, `packaging/soos-daemon.service:27`
- **Evidence**: socket.rs:267-272: on `EOPNOTSUPP` from `fchmodat(..., NoFollowSymlink)` the code falls back to `fs::set_permissions(&config.socket_path, ...)`, a path-based call that follows symlinks (glibc < 2.32 returns ENOTSUP for AT_SYMLINK_NOFOLLOW). socket.rs:103-116 runs `std::process::Command::new("groupadd").args(["--system", group_name])` when root; with `ProtectSystem=strict` (service:27) /etc is read-only so it can only fail, and all packaging paths already create the group (packaging/debian/postinst:10-14, packaging/rpm/soos.spec:65-66, packaging/arch/soos.install:7-8, scripts/install.sh:188-193).
- **Impact**: The fallback reintroduces a (mitigated by the flock + 0750 root dir) TOCTOU on old glibc; the groupadd path is dead in production, adds a privileged subprocess and PATH dependency to an authentication daemon, and can mask a packaging bug.
- **Recommendation**: Replace the fallback with `open(O_PATH|O_NOFOLLOW)` on the socket node and `chmod` via `/proc/self/fd/<n>`, or fail closed. Remove the runtime groupadd and return the `Group not found` error. Add a socket test that a missing group fails bind without spawning anything (assert no `groupadd` on PATH needed).
- **Verifier notes**: socket.rs:267-272: on `Errno::EOPNOTSUPP` from `fchmodat(..., NoFollowSymlink)` falls back to path-based `fs::set_permissions(&config.socket_path, ...)`. nix 0.29.0 (Cargo.lock) maps NoFollowSymlink to AT_SYMLINK_NOFOLLOW which glibc <2.32 and musl report as ENOTSUP, so the fallback is reachable on old libc (host here is glibc 2.44, so not locally). Mitigated by flock + 0750 root dir. socket.rs:103-116 spawns `groupadd --system` when root; unit has `ProtectSystem=strict` (soos-daemon.service:27) making /etc read-only; all packaging paths create the group (packaging/debian/postinst:13-14, rpm/soos.spec:66, arch/soos.install:8, scripts/install.sh:192-193).

#### DMN-14 — systemd unit runs as unrestricted root: no CapabilityBoundingSet, syscall filter, namespace/realtime/kernel protections, network isolation, or display-manager ordering

GitHub issue: #203

**MINOR** · CONFIRMED · security · effort M

- **Locations**: `packaging/soos-daemon.service:4-34`, `crates/daemon/tests/systemd_test.rs:37-59`, `crates/daemon/tests/hardening_tests.rs:87-146`
- **Evidence**: The unit (lines 7-34) sets `User=root` with no `CapabilityBoundingSet`, `AmbientCapabilities`, `SystemCallFilter`, `ProtectKernelTunables`, `ProtectKernelModules`, `ProtectKernelLogs`, `ProtectControlGroups`, `ProtectClock`, `ProtectHostname`, `ProtectProc`, `RestrictNamespaces`, `RestrictRealtime`, `PrivateNetwork`/`IPAddressDeny`, `SupplementaryGroups=video`, or `LimitMEMLOCK`. Ordering is only `After=local-fs.target systemd-udevd.service` (line 4) with no `Before=display-manager.service`. Actual needs observed in code: CAP_IPC_LOCK (mlock.rs:49 mlockall), CAP_CHOWN/CAP_FOWNER (socket.rs:261, 291 fchmodat/fchownat), read access to /dev/video* (root:video 0660), AF_UNIX only (RestrictAddressFamilies already), no network at all. Both hardening tests only assert presence of the current directive list.
- **Impact**: A compromise of the daemon (it parses untrusted IPC, decodes camera frames, runs a native ORT runtime) yields full root capabilities, kernel module loading, and network access despite the process needing none of them. GDM may present its first prompt before the daemon is listening (first login silently falls back to password).
- **Recommendation**: Add: `CapabilityBoundingSet=CAP_IPC_LOCK CAP_CHOWN CAP_FOWNER CAP_DAC_OVERRIDE`, `SupplementaryGroups=video`, `SystemCallFilter=@system-service`, `SystemCallErrorNumber=EPERM`, `ProtectKernelTunables=yes ProtectKernelModules=yes ProtectKernelLogs=yes ProtectControlGroups=yes ProtectClock=yes ProtectHostname=yes ProtectProc=invisible RestrictNamespaces=yes RestrictRealtime=yes PrivateNetwork=yes IPAddressDeny=any LimitMEMLOCK=infinity`, and `Before=display-manager.service` + `WantedBy=multi-user.target`. Validate on a real host with `systemd-analyze security soos-daemon` and extend systemd_test.rs with the new mandatory list.
- **Verifier notes**: packaging/soos-daemon.service:4-37 read in full: `User=root`, no CapabilityBoundingSet/SystemCallFilter/ProtectKernel*/ProtectControlGroups/ProtectClock/ProtectHostname/ProtectProc/RestrictNamespaces/RestrictRealtime/PrivateNetwork/IPAddressDeny/LimitMEMLOCK; ordering `After=local-fs.target systemd-udevd.service` only. systemd_test.rs:37-59 and hardening_tests.rs:87-146 assert only the current directive list plus a few negative patterns. Corrections to the recommendation: `SupplementaryGroups=video` is pointless while the process is root with CAP_DAC_OVERRIDE; `ProtectProc=invisible` is incompatible with the loginuid check proposed in DMN-09; `WantedBy=multi-user.target` is already present (line 37).

#### DMN-15 — Request/Event frames share one wire format with no discriminator; disambiguation relies on double-decoding and a uid heuristic

GitHub issue: #204

**MINOR** · CONFIRMED · design · effort M

- **Locations**: `crates/daemon/src/dispatcher.rs:214-243`, `crates/protocol/src/types.rs:63-94`, `crates/pam/src/ipc.rs:400-407`
- **Evidence**: dispatcher.rs:216-222 decode the same bytes as both `Request` and `Event` with `take_from_bytes` + `rest.is_empty()`; on double match line 230 `if req.uid_hint == peer.uid` picks Request else Event. The PAM event (ipc.rs:400-407) is sent by a root peer (uid 0) with `request_id: None`; if its bytes also decode exactly as a Request whose `uid_hint` varint is 0 it is processed as an Auth request for uid 0. Event and Request have no type tag (types.rs:63-94).
- **Impact**: Behavior on ambiguous frames is defined by a heuristic, not the protocol; a sender can choose which handler runs. Not an authorization bypass by itself (both handlers still enforce their own checks after DMN-02/03 are fixed), but a fragile foundation for future message kinds.
- **Recommendation**: Introduce a top-level `Message { Request, Event }` enum (protocol v2 or a leading kind byte) and reject any frame that decodes as more than one type; add a fuzz target for the disambiguation. Document in Docs/IPC_PROTOCOL.md.
- **Verifier notes**: dispatcher.rs:214-243 double-decodes with `postcard::take_from_bytes` for both `Request` and `Event` and, on double match, picks Request iff `req.uid_hint == peer.uid` (line 230). types.rs:63-94: no discriminator. PAM event (pam/src/ipc.rs:400-407) has `request_id: None`, `uid: Some(uid)`; decoding it as a Request needs >=32 bytes after the kind byte for `request_id`, which typical events (~27 bytes with a 12-byte service) do not reach, so collisions are possible but uncommon; still a heuristic, not a protocol rule. Impact correctly bounded as design fragility.

#### DMN-16 — warmup_frames default differs between file-based config (0) and no-config startup (20)

GitHub issue: #205

**MINOR** · CONFIRMED · design · effort S

- **Locations**: `crates/daemon/src/config.rs:244`, `crates/daemon/src/config.rs:84-98`, `crates/camera-v4l/src/config.rs:49`, `crates/daemon/tests/config_tests.rs:232-243`, `Docs/CAMERA_V4L_CRATE.md:54`
- **Evidence**: config.rs:244 `config.pipeline.camera.warmup_frames = pipe.warmup_frames.unwrap_or(0);` overrides the CameraConfig default (camera-v4l/src/config.rs:49 `warmup_frames: 20`) whenever a `[pipeline]` table exists, whereas `DaemonConfig::default()` (config.rs:84-98, used when /etc/soos/daemon.toml is absent, config.rs:340-345) keeps 20. config_tests.rs:232-243 makes 0 a contract "for instant wake"; Docs/CAMERA_V4L_CRATE.md:54 documents default 20.
- **Impact**: Same binary behaves differently depending on whether a config file exists; with 0 warmup frames the first post-standby frames (auto-exposure unsettled) are fed to inference, which is a plausible contributor to intermittent false rejections; with 20 the wake budget is consumed by discarded frames.
- **Recommendation**: Pick one default in `PipelineConfig::default()` (0 to honor the existing test contract) and document it; consider a time-based warmup (discard frames younger than N ms after resume) instead of a count. Test: `DaemonConfig::default().pipeline.camera.warmup_frames == DaemonConfig::from_toml_str("[pipeline]\n").pipeline.camera.warmup_frames`.
- **Verifier notes**: config.rs:244 `config.pipeline.camera.warmup_frames = pipe.warmup_frames.unwrap_or(0);` executed whenever a `[pipeline]` table exists; `DaemonConfig::default()` keeps `CameraConfig::default().warmup_frames = 20` (camera-v4l/src/config.rs:49); `load_or_default` (config.rs:340-345) uses the default when /etc/soos/daemon.toml is absent. config_tests.rs:232-243 pins 0 as a contract; Docs/CAMERA_V4L_CRATE.md:54 documents 20. No packaged daemon.toml exists (`grep -rn daemon.toml scripts packaging Docs` empty), so installs without a config get 20 and any admin-written config gets 0.

#### DMN-17 — Daemon behavior undocumented or contradicted by Docs/AI prose (daemon.toml keys, Status/Preview kinds, persistent loop, frames-over-IPC prohibition, PAD class index)

GitHub issue: #206

**MINOR** · CONFIRMED · doc-drift · effort S

- **Locations**: `Docs/IPC_PROTOCOL.md:130`, `AI/ARCHITECTURE.md:303`, `AI/DECISIONS.md:20`, `AI/ARCHITECTURE.md:117`, `crates/daemon/src/config.rs:124-191`
- **Evidence**: `grep -rn 'connection_timeout_ms|max_concurrent_connections|enforce_active_session|daemon.toml' Docs/*.md` returns nothing: none of the TOML keys in config.rs:124-191 are documented. `grep -in preview Docs/IPC_PROTOCOL.md` returns nothing: `RequestKind::Status`, `PreviewFrame`, `PreviewResponse` and `MAX_PREVIEW_MESSAGE_SIZE` are absent; line 130 still describes a single request per connection although dispatcher.rs:130-176 loops (walkthrough 74). ARCHITECTURE.md:303 forbids frames over IPC while the daemon ships them. DECISIONS.md:20 says live class 0 vs code 1/2. ARCHITECTURE.md:117 describes an inference thread + semaphore that does not exist (DMN-05).
- **Impact**: Operators cannot configure the daemon from the docs; reviewers derive invariants from stale prose (the project-facts file explicitly warns about this drift).
- **Recommendation**: Add Docs/DAEMON.md (config reference with every key, defaults and validation rules; connection model; authorization rules per RequestKind), update IPC_PROTOCOL.md for v1 kinds and the persistent loop, and add ADR entries for preview-over-IPC and the PAD class index.
- **Verifier notes**: `grep -rln 'connection_timeout_ms|max_concurrent_connections|enforce_active_session|daemon.toml' Docs README.md packaging scripts` returns no files; none of config.rs:124-191 keys are documented. `grep -in 'preview|persistent' Docs/IPC_PROTOCOL.md` empty; IPC_PROTOCOL.md:50 lists only `Authenticate` as RequestKind. ARCHITECTURE.md:303 (frames over IPC pitfall), :117 (nonexistent inference threads/semaphore) and DECISIONS.md:20 (class 0) confirmed stale. Minor correction: IPC_PROTOCOL.md:128-132 describes the timeout/close behavior rather than literally 'a single request per connection', but it predates the persistent loop (dispatcher.rs:130-176, which now only closes on timeout when `requests_processed == 0`).

#### DMN-11 — Full 32-byte request nonce is logged, contrary to the logging policy, and the audit test cannot catch it

GitHub issue: #257

**SUGGESTION** · PARTIAL · security · effort S · (reviewer severity: MINOR)

- **Locations**: `crates/daemon/src/dispatcher.rs:816-819`, `crates/daemon/src/logging.rs:11-13`, `crates/daemon/tests/logging_audit_test.rs:42-50`
- **Evidence**: dispatcher.rs:817 `warn!(request_id = ?req.request_id, "Rejecting authentication request: daemon pipeline is not initialized")` prints all 32 bytes. logging.rs:11-12 permits only an "anonymized `request_id`". logging_audit_test.rs:42-50 keyword list (`password, secret, credential, embedding, frame, image, raw_payload`) does not include `request_id`.
- **Impact**: The single-use nonce binding the Allow response lands in the journal (readable by `adm`/`systemd-journal` members). Low exploitability because the response is bound to the socket, but it violates the stated D5 invariant and sets a precedent.
- **Recommendation**: Log a truncated hex prefix (first 4 bytes) or a hash; add a `short_id()` helper and a negative pattern `request_id = ?` to the audit test.
- **Verifier notes**: dispatcher.rs:816-819 does log `request_id = ?req.request_id` (full 32-byte array) and logging.rs:11-12 permits only an anonymized id; logging_audit_test.rs:42-50 keyword list lacks `request_id`. However this warn! is on the `pipeline: None` fallback path, and main.rs:73-77 always constructs the dispatcher via `with_pipeline`, so it is unreachable in production; only tests using `ConnectionDispatcher::new` hit it. Real but low value; the audit-test gap is the useful part.

#### DMN-18 — Response issued/expires timestamps are inconsistent and never consumed

GitHub issue: #258

**SUGGESTION** · CONFIRMED · design · effort S

- **Locations**: `crates/daemon/src/dispatcher.rs:839-846`, `crates/daemon/src/dispatcher.rs:318`, `crates/daemon/src/dispatcher.rs:454`, `crates/daemon/src/dispatcher.rs:637`, `crates/pam/src/ipc.rs:370-380`
- **Evidence**: build_response sets `expires_monotonic_ns: now_ns.saturating_add(2_000_000_000)` (845) but several callers pass `now_ns = 0` (lines 318, 454, 475, 637) producing `issued=0, expires=2s-after-boot`. The PAM client (ipc.rs:370-380) checks only version and request_id; expiry is never validated (PAM area, noted for completeness).
- **Impact**: Invariant 4 ("bound to ... monotonic deadline") is not actually enforced end to end; the field is noise.
- **Recommendation**: Always pass a real clock value (fall back to `Unavailable` if the clock fails) and define the contract: PAM must reject `Allow` when `now > expires` (owned by the PAM area). Add a daemon test asserting `issued <= expires` and `issued > 0` on every verdict path.
- **Verifier notes**: dispatcher.rs:839-846 `expires_monotonic_ns: now_ns.saturating_add(2_000_000_000)`; callers pass `0` at lines 318, 437, 454, 475, 637. pam/src/ipc.rs:370-380 checks only version and request_id; no expiry validation. Confirmed.

#### DMN-19 — Shutdown drops in-flight connections abruptly and handler panics are only visible on stderr

GitHub issue: #259

**SUGGESTION** · CONFIRMED · reliability · effort S

- **Locations**: `crates/daemon/src/main.rs:89-120`, `crates/daemon/src/main.rs:95-99`
- **Evidence**: main.rs:106-113 break out of the accept loop on SIGINT/SIGTERM and immediately return; spawned handler tasks (95-99) are aborted when the runtime drops. JoinHandles are discarded so a panic inside `handle_connection` is reported only by the default panic hook (stderr), not via tracing, and no health flag changes.
- **Impact**: A PAM client mid-verification at shutdown gets EOF (fail-closed, acceptable) but with no log; a panicking handler path would be silent in journald filtered on the tracing format.
- **Recommendation**: Keep handlers in a `JoinSet`, on signal stop accepting, unlink the socket, then drain with `timeout(connection_timeout)`; install `std::panic::set_hook` that logs via `tracing::error!` without payload data. Test: shutdown during an in-flight mock auth completes within connection_timeout and logs the drain.
- **Verifier notes**: main.rs:89-120: `tokio::spawn` handlers with discarded JoinHandles (95-99); SIGINT/SIGTERM branches `break` (106-113) and main returns, dropping the runtime and in-flight tasks; no panic hook installed anywhere in crates/daemon/src. Fail-closed for PAM (EOF -> PAM_IGNORE) as stated.

### 4.6 Storage & CLIs

*Coverage*: Read in full: crates/biometric-store/src/{lib,error,crypto,store,template}.rs; crates/evidence-store/src/{lib,error,config,crypto,snapshot,store}.rs; crates/enrollment-cli/src/{lib,main,error,args,quality,shred,html_report,guided_enrollment,service}.rs; crates/admin-cli/src/{lib,main,args,error,gdm,user,test_pam,status,logs,redact}.rs; all four crates' Cargo.toml; tests: biometric-store/tests/permissions_tests.rs, crud_tests.rs (delete shred part), enrollment-cli/tests/{guided_enrollment,shred,enroll,verify(partial)}_tests.rs, admin-cli/tests/gdm_tests.rs, evidence-store/tests/retention_tests.rs (assertions), plus a listing of every test function name in the four crates; Docs/BIOMETRIC_STORE_CRATE.md, Docs/EVIDENCE_STORE_CRATE.md, Docs/ENROLLMENT_CLI.md, Docs/PACKAGING_AND_PROVISIONING.md §6; AI/ARCHITECTURE.md, AI/DECISIONS.md, project-facts.md, auditor-agent and candid-reviewer skills; root Cargo.toml workspace lints; AI/VERIFICATION_MATRIX.md rows E1-E8, GEPU1-4, ASG5, LSF1; walkthroughs 66/72/75 (class-index sections). Cross-area lines opened only as evidence: crates/daemon/src/pipeline.rs:150-200, dispatcher.rs:265-300,464-495,525-545,600-625; crates/vision/src/pipeline.rs:56-71,176-181,281,336; crates/vision/src/error.rs; crates/inference-ort/src/pad.rs:60-122; crates/camera-v4l/src/frame.rs:48-61, manager.rs, v4l_impl.rs:76-88; crates/gui/src/worker.rs:80-130, app.rs:785-905; packaging/debian/postinst:19-50, scripts/install.sh:230-250. Commands run: greps listed in findings; a scratch Cargo project under the scratchpad (path deps on soos-admin-cli, soos-biometric-store, soos-evidence-store) whose output is quoted in the evidence. NOT covered: camera device resolution (service.rs:53-161, device_resolution_tests.rs, owned by another reviewer); import_tests/list_tests/path_validation_tests/root_check_tests bodies (names only); evidence-store proptest/encryption/opt-in/daily-cap/zero-network test bodies; admin-cli status/test_pam/logs/redact test bodies; packaging beyond key generation; GUI and daemon logic beyond the cited lines; no cargo clippy/test run on the workspace target dir.

<details><summary>Reviewer improvement notes</summary>

Ordered by risk and dependency; each step names the test that proves it.

1. STO-01 (PAD class index): replace the literal `2` in crates/enrollment-cli/src/service.rs:773 and crates/daemon/src/pipeline.rs:192 with `OrtPadDetector::new(...)`/the DEFAULT constant; add a tests/invariants grep that forbids numeric literals in `new_with_class_index(`; add an enrollment-cli test through the real construction path with a 3-class MockPadDetector. Coordinate with the vision reviewer, who may already have this in their list.

2. STO-02 (debug-vision frame on disk): write with create_new(true)+0600 into a validated root-only path, remove the leftover comment block, propagate detector errors; test symlink pre-creation and file mode.

3. STO-03 + STO-20 (gdm): rewrite the insertion algorithm (after faillock preauth, never index 0, ignore comments), verify the module file exists, atomic write with backup and a `restore` action; add the six tests the matrix already names so STO-07's citations become true.

4. STO-04 + STO-14 (filesystem trust): BiometricStore::new and both MasterKey::load_or_create verify ownership/mode of pre-existing paths instead of chmod-ing them; O_NOFOLLOW in evidence key open; tighten ALLOWED_FHS_PREFIXES; tests for 1777 dir untouched, 0644 key rejected, parent dir mode preserved.

5. STO-05 + STO-11 + STO-09 + STO-21 (enrollment correctness): sequence-aware frame acquisition, PadFailed counted as invalid candidate, explicit target uid (SUDO_UID aware, refuse uid 0 by default), model metadata derived from the registry, args parsed before the root check, import overwrite confirmation. Update Docs/ENROLLMENT_CLI.md and the enroll test expectations with an ADR entry explaining the contract change.

6. STO-10 (fusion): identity-consistency gate against the frontal mean, enforce documented pose bounds, finiteness check; adversarial tests (orthogonal sample, extreme yaw, NaN).

7. STO-12 (GUI import handoff): stdin pipe or 0600 file in $XDG_RUNTIME_DIR; import caps file size and checks owner == PKEXEC_UID; add a packaging polkit .policy scoped to soos-enroll import/delete.

8. STO-08 + STO-18 + STO-19 (evidence/store hygiene): persist width/height/format, Zeroizing for plaintext, size caps on reads, on-disk daily counts, an admin export command; tests for decodability and cap-after-restart.

9. STO-06 + STO-17 (secure erasure truthfulness and dead code): shred-before-rename in enroll or drop the claim in favour of documented crypto-erasure; delete encode_bmp/build_service/shred.rs duplicates; fix AlreadyEnrolled semantics.

10. STO-13, STO-15, STO-16 (admin-cli robustness): byte-offset-safe case-insensitive redaction, CLOCK_MONOTONIC deadline with clamped timeout, serde_json for all --format json output.

11. STO-07 (traceability): correct AI/VERIFICATION_MATRIX.md (test names, `.cbor.enc`), document soos-admin gdm/add-user and soos-enroll import/debug-vision/--mock, and add an invariant test that every `test_*` reference in the matrix resolves to a real function.

12. STO-22 (optional): AAD binding of uid/date and an enrollment counter for rollback detection.
