#### STO-01 — soos-enroll (and daemon) hard-code MiniFASNetV2 live class index 2 = ScreenReplay; walkthrough-72 fix never reached the callers

GitHub issue: #146

**CRITICAL** · CONFIRMED · security · effort S

- **Locations**: `crates/enrollment-cli/src/service.rs:773`, `crates/daemon/src/pipeline.rs:189-193`, `crates/inference-ort/src/pad.rs:81-82`, `AI/walkthroughs/72_anti_spoof_ir_stability_gdm.md:18-20`, `crates/inference-ort/tests/pad_tests.rs:298-308`
- **Evidence**: service.rs:773: `let pad = Arc::new(OrtPadDetector::new_with_class_index(pad_session, 0.80, 2));` and pipeline.rs:189-193 pass literal `2`. pad.rs:81-82: `/// Default live class index ... (Class 0: PrintPhoto, Class 1: Genuine Live, Class 2: ScreenReplay). pub const DEFAULT_MINIFASNET_LIVE_CLASS_INDEX: usize = 1;`. Walkthrough 72:20: "Because index 2 was treated as genuine live, the system assigned high live confidence to screen replays, enabling attacks." `git blame -L 773,773 crates/enrollment-cli/src/service.rs` -> 084ce5c6 (2026-09-26, walkthrough 66, which set 2); `git blame -L 192,192 crates/daemon/src/pipeline.rs` -> 084ce5c6. `git log -S"pad_session, 0.80, 2)"` shows only 084ce5c: the value was never changed after walkthrough 72 realigned the constant to 1. project-facts.md §2 lists DEFAULT_MINIFASNET_LIVE_CLASS_INDEX = 1 [72]. pad_tests.rs:298 `test_pad_default_live_class_index_is_one` only asserts the constant, not the call sites. vision/pipeline.rs:336 rejects when `!pad_result.is_live || pad_result.score < pad_threshold`, so `soos-enroll enroll` returns `Err(VisionError::PadFailed)` for genuine faces (service.rs:346 aborts) while `soos-enroll verify` reports `pad_result: "PASSED"` for a phone-screen replay.
- **Impact**: Presentation-attack detection is inverted in both the enrollment CLI and (cross-area) the production daemon: screen replays are scored as live, genuine users are scored as spoof. In the daemon this is an authentication bypass; in soos-enroll it makes verify diagnostics lie and enroll fail on real faces unless --mock is used.
- **Recommendation**: Replace both literals with `OrtPadDetector::new(pad_session, threshold)` (defaults to DEFAULT_MINIFASNET_LIVE_CLASS_INDEX) or pass the constant explicitly. Add a repository invariant test (tests/invariants) that greps for `new_with_class_index(` with a numeric literal, and an enrollment-cli test that builds the real (non-mock) pipeline construction path with a MockPadDetector returning probs [0.0, 0.99, 0.0] and asserts the frame is accepted. Coordinate with the vision/daemon reviewer.
- **Verifier notes**: Re-opened crates/enrollment-cli/src/service.rs:773 `OrtPadDetector::new_with_class_index(pad_session, 0.80, 2)` and crates/daemon/src/pipeline.rs:189-193 (literal `2`). crates/inference-ort/src/pad.rs:81-82 `DEFAULT_MINIFASNET_LIVE_CLASS_INDEX: usize = 1` with doc 'Class 2: ScreenReplay'. `git show 084ce5c` (walkthrough 66, #127) changed both call sites 1->2 and the constant to 2; `git show 72469ba` (walkthrough 72, #135) changed ONLY the constant 2->1 (`-... = 2;` / `+... = 1;`), no call-site diff; `git log -S"pad_session, 0.80, 2)"` shows only 084ce5c (and 94d9ebe, which does not touch that line). Interpretation at pad.rs:145-160: `p_live = probs[live_class_index]`, so with index 2 a replay-dominant vector [0.15,0.10,0.75] is live and a genuine [0.03,0.94,0.03] is spoof. vision/pipeline.rs:336-341 returns PadFailed on !is_live, and service.rs:346 `Err(e) => return Err(e.into())` aborts enroll. GUI (crates/gui/src/main.rs:92) uses `OrtPadDetector::new(pad_session, 0.80)` i.e. index 1, so GUI and daemon/CLI disagree at runtime. Additional drift not in the finding: AI/DECISIONS.md:20 still states '[Live, Print, Replay] ordering (Class 0 = Live) ... defaulting to 0', a third contradictory value; walkthrough 66:15-18 claims Class 1 = ScreenReplay while walkthrough 72:15-20 claims Class 1 = Live. No real-model fixture test exists (models/ holds only manifest.toml; pad_tests.rs only exercises interpret_probabilities). Severity stands: the daemon call site accepts screen replays as live if index 1 is correct.

#### STO-02 — debug-vision writes a cleartext raw camera frame into soos-debug.html in CWD with default permissions via symlink-following fs::write

GitHub issue: #149

**CRITICAL** · CONFIRMED · security · effort S

- **Locations**: `crates/enrollment-cli/src/service.rs:680-686`, `crates/enrollment-cli/src/html_report.rs:553-565`, `crates/enrollment-cli/src/service.rs:659-674`, `AI/ARCHITECTURE.md:145`, `AI/ARCHITECTURE.md:370`
- **Evidence**: service.rs:680-686: `let base64_img = base64_encode(&rgb); let html = generate_html_report(...); let cwd = std::env::current_dir()...; let out_path = cwd.join("soos-debug.html"); std::fs::write(&out_path, html)`. html_report.rs:553-565 embeds the full RGB buffer (`const b64 = "{}"; ... ctx.putImageData`). `std::fs::write` uses O_CREAT|O_TRUNC without O_NOFOLLOW or an explicit mode, so the file is created with the process umask (typically 0644) and follows a pre-existing symlink. The command runs as root (main.rs:65 `check_privileges(true)`). ARCHITECTURE.md:145 lists "Storing raw frames ... on disk" as a prohibited anti-pattern and :370 says raw enrollment frames are deleted immediately. service.rs:659-674 is a block of leftover stream-of-consciousness comments ("Wait, VisionPipeline doesn't expose detector...") and :678 `.unwrap_or_default()` silently swallows detector errors.
- **Impact**: A root-run diagnostic leaves an unencrypted face image readable by every local user (0644 in e.g. /tmp or a shared home), and any unprivileged user who can pre-create `soos-debug.html` as a symlink in the admin's CWD gets root to truncate/overwrite an arbitrary file (e.g. /etc/shadow) with HTML.
- **Recommendation**: Write with OpenOptions::create_new(true).mode(0o600) (fail if the file exists), default the output to a root-only directory (e.g. /var/lib/soos/debug/ or an explicit --output under a validated FHS path), and require an explicit `--i-understand-this-writes-a-raw-frame` style flag or drop the image and keep only detection geometry. Remove the comment block at 659-674 and propagate detector errors. Test: pre-create a symlink at the output path and assert the command returns an error and the target is untouched; assert the created file mode is 0600.
- **Verifier notes**: Line numbers corrected: debug_vision is service.rs:641-690. service.rs:679-681 `let base64_img = base64_encode(&rgb); let html = generate_html_report(...)`; :683-686 `cwd.join("soos-debug.html"); std::fs::write(&out_path, html)` (O_CREAT|O_TRUNC, no O_NOFOLLOW, umask mode; no `umask` call anywhere in enrollment-cli: grep returned nothing). html_report.rs:152-164 embeds the full raw RGB buffer (`const b64 = "{}"` ... `ctx.putImageData`); :176 comment 'Actually just raw RGB base64 now'. Stream-of-consciousness comment block is at service.rs:659-674 and `.unwrap_or_default()` at :678 swallows detector errors — both as described. Root is mandatory: main.rs:32 `check_privileges(true)?` (not :65). Architecture citations corrected: AI/ARCHITECTURE.md:20 lists 'Storing raw frames or passwords on disk' as an anti-pattern; :245 says raw enrollment frames are deleted immediately (finding cited :145/:370 which are PAM stanza lines). Root-run fs::write into an attacker-controllable CWD (e.g. /tmp) following a pre-planted symlink is a real truncate-as-root primitive; the invariant violation justifies CRITICAL.

#### STO-03 — soos-admin gdm enable places pam_soos.so before pam_faillock/pam_nologin/pam_succeed_if, writes the PAM file non-atomically with no backup, and never checks the module is installed (lockout risk)

GitHub issue: #177

**MAJOR** · PARTIAL · security · effort M

- **Locations**: `crates/admin-cli/src/gdm.rs:451-475`, `crates/admin-cli/src/gdm.rs:462-467`, `AI/ARCHITECTURE.md:264-273`, `AI/ARCHITECTURE.md:426`
- **Evidence**: gdm.rs:454-461 inserts `auth  sufficient  pam_soos.so timeout_ms=2500` immediately before the first line containing `@include common-auth`; :462-467 inserts it at index 0 when no such line exists; :469 `fs::write(pam_file, new_lines.join("\n"))` (truncate+write, no temp file, no rename, no backup). Scratch probe output on a Fedora/Arch-style file: `auth  sufficient  pam_soos.so timeout_ms=2500` became line 1, above `pam_selinux_permit.so` and `substack password-auth` (where pam_faillock preauth lives). On a Debian-style file the line lands after `pam_succeed_if.so user != root` but before `@include common-auth`, i.e. before `pam_faillock preauth` inside common-auth. ARCHITECTURE.md:265 requires "Placed AFTER mandatory faillock preauth"; :426 lists this exact ordering as a pitfall; auditor checklist #8 requires keeping faillock preauth ordering. No code checks that pam_soos.so exists in the PAM module directory; Linux-PAM marks an unloadable module `must_fail`, which fails the whole stack regardless of the `sufficient` control.
- **Impact**: Facial auth can bypass account lockout counters on every distribution; on Fedora/Arch-style files it also runs before pam_nologin/pam_selinux_permit. A crash between truncate and write, or enabling before the .so is installed, leaves gdm-password unusable (graphical login lockout) with no automatic rollback.
- **Recommendation**: Insert after the faillock preauth line when present, otherwise immediately before the first `pam_unix.so`/`substack`/`@include` line following any `pam_succeed_if`/`pam_nologin` lines, never at index 0; refuse to enable unless pam_soos.so is found under the distro's security dir; write to `<file>.soos.tmp` (mode preserved) + fsync + rename and keep a `<file>.soos.bak`; add a `gdm restore` action. Tests: Fedora-style and Debian-style fixtures asserting exact line position relative to faillock/pam_unix; a fixture with `pam_faillock.so preauth` asserting insertion after it; a missing-module fixture asserting refusal; assertion that a backup file exists after enable.
- **Verifier notes**: Line numbers wrong (file is 126 lines): insertion logic is gdm.rs:91-107 (before first `@include common-auth`, else `new_lines.insert(0, ...)` at :103-106), non-atomic `fs::write(pam_file, ...)` at :109 with no temp file/rename/backup, no check that pam_soos.so exists. Scratch probe (cargo run of scratchpad crate, path dep soos-admin-cli) on a Fedora-style file `pam_selinux_permit.so / substack password-auth / pam_gnome_keyring.so` produced `auth  sufficient  pam_soos.so timeout_ms=2500` as line 1 — confirmed. AI/ARCHITECTURE.md:140 'Placed AFTER mandatory faillock preauth, BEFORE pam_unix' and :301 pitfall — confirmed (finding cited :264-273/:426, wrong). REFUTED sub-claim: 'Linux-PAM marks an unloadable module must_fail, which fails the whole stack regardless of sufficient'. Fetched linux-pam/libpam/pam_handlers.c and pam_dispatch.c: a dlopen failure sets `mod->type = PAM_MT_FAULTY_MOD` and the handler keeps its parsed actions; `_pam_set_default_control(actions, _PAM_ACTION_BAD)` + PAM_HT_MUST_FAIL is only applied for failed include/substack parsing. In `_pam_dispatch_aux`, a faulty module hits `h->func == NULL` -> `retval = PAM_MODULE_UNKNOWN` and `action = h->actions[retval]`, which for `sufficient` is `default=ignore`, so a missing .so is logged and skipped, not fatal. The lockout risk therefore reduces to a crash between truncate and write (narrow) — the 'refuse unless .so is found' step is a nicety, not a lockout fix. Ordering/atomicity/backup parts hold; MAJOR retained for the faillock-bypass ordering.

#### STO-04 — BiometricStore::new chmods any existing admin-supplied directory to exactly 0700 (drops sticky/setgid bits); FHS validation allows /tmp, /home, /etc, /usr, /dev

GitHub issue: #178

**MAJOR** · CONFIRMED · security · effort S

- **Locations**: `crates/biometric-store/src/store.rs:294-300`, `crates/enrollment-cli/src/args.rs:994-996`, `crates/enrollment-cli/src/service.rs:703-710`
- **Evidence**: store.rs:297-300: `} else { // Best-effort ensure mode 0700 ... let _ = std::fs::set_permissions(&base_dir, std::fs::Permissions::from_mode(0o700)); }`. args.rs:994-996 `ALLOWED_FHS_PREFIXES = ["/var","/run","/etc","/usr","/tmp","/dev","/opt","/home"]` and validate_fhs_path accepts the prefix itself. Scratch probe: created a directory with mode 1777, called `BiometricStore::new(&dir, key)`, observed `existing dir mode before=1777 after=700`. `sudo soos-enroll --biometrics-dir /tmp list` therefore rewrites /tmp to 0700 without sticky bit. The daemon also calls BiometricStore::new on its configured dir (daemon/pipeline.rs:155).
- **Impact**: A typo or a shared directory passed as --biometrics-dir silently breaks the host (e.g. /tmp, /home, /var unusable for all non-root users). No ownership check either: a non-root-owned directory is accepted as the template store.
- **Recommendation**: Only set 0700 on a directory this call created; for an existing directory verify `symlink_metadata`: is_dir, uid == 0 (or the daemon's uid), mode & 0o077 == 0, and fail with InvalidPath otherwise instead of chmod-ing. Tighten ALLOWED_FHS_PREFIXES to the dedicated soos paths (/var/lib/soos, /etc/soos, /run/soos, /tmp/<subdir>) or require the path to be at least two components deep. Test: pre-create a 1777 directory, call new(), assert mode unchanged and an error is returned.
- **Verifier notes**: Actual lines: crates/biometric-store/src/store.rs:43-46 `else { // Best-effort ensure mode 0700 ... let _ = std::fs::set_permissions(&base_dir, Permissions::from_mode(0o700)); }` (finding cited 294-300). args.rs:204-206 `ALLOWED_FHS_PREFIXES = ["/var","/run","/etc","/usr","/tmp","/dev","/opt","/home"]` and validate_fhs_path (:209-224) uses `starts_with(prefix)`, which accepts the prefix itself. Scratch probe: created dir with 0o1777, called `BiometricStore::new(&dir, key)`, printed `existing dir mode before=1777 after=700`. No ownership/uid check exists in new(). Daemon also calls BiometricStore::new (pipeline.rs:155 per finding; not re-checked, in another area). Impact as stated.

#### STO-06 — Secure-erasure claims are not delivered: re-enrollment leaves the previous template's blocks unshredded, and overwrite-in-place is ineffective on SSD/CoW/journaled filesystems

GitHub issue: #179

**MAJOR** · CONFIRMED · doc-drift · effort S

- **Locations**: `crates/biometric-store/src/store.rs:403-409`, `crates/biometric-store/src/store.rs:342-371`, `Docs/ENROLLMENT_CLI.md:10`, `crates/enrollment-cli/src/main.rs:128`
- **Evidence**: store.rs:403-407 doc: "ensuring the deleted biometric template cannot be recovered from disk sectors". enroll() (store.rs:353-370) writes a new temp file and `rename`s over the destination; the old inode's blocks are freed without any overwrite. Scratch probe: enroll twice for uid 1000 and compare `MetadataExt::ino()` -> `re-enroll inode changed (old blocks freed without shred): true`. Docs/ENROLLMENT_CLI.md:10 "Anti-Forensic Secure Erasure: ... file blocks on disk are overwritten"; main.rs:128 prints "securely shredded". On SSDs (wear levelling), btrfs/ZFS (copy-on-write), and ext4 data=journal, in-place overwrite does not reach the physical blocks (this is the documented limitation of GNU shred as well).
- **Impact**: Administrators and users are given a false guarantee; the most common path that discards a template (re-enroll from the GUI or `enroll --yes`) performs no overwrite at all.
- **Recommendation**: Either shred the existing file before rename in enroll() (call delete's overwrite routine when dest exists) and reword all docs/messages to "best-effort overwrite; ineffective on SSD/CoW; rely on encryption at rest and key destruction", or drop the overwrite and document key-based crypto-erasure (rotate master key = erase all). Test: enroll twice via a hard-link probe (as crud_tests.rs:117-129 does for delete) and assert the first inode's content was overwritten.
- **Verifier notes**: Actual lines: store.rs:149-153 doc 'ensuring the deleted biometric template cannot be recovered from disk sectors'; enroll() store.rs:88-118 writes a temp file and `std::fs::rename(&tmp_path, &dest_path)` at :116 with no overwrite of the previous inode. Scratch probe: enrolled uid 1000 twice, hard-linked the first file before the second enroll, observed `re-enroll inode changed=true old bytes intact via hardlink=true` (old ciphertext still fully readable through the link after re-enroll). Docs/ENROLLMENT_CLI.md:13 (not :10) 'Anti-Forensic Secure Erasure ... file blocks on disk are overwritten'; main.rs:95 (not :128) prints 'securely shredded'. SSD/CoW limitation is standard (same as GNU shred docs). Doc-drift MAJOR is fair given the explicit user-facing guarantee.

#### STO-07 — Verification matrix cites six gdm tests and a GDM_PAM_LINE constant that do not exist; soos-admin gdm, import, debug-vision, --mock are undocumented; GEPU1 names a wrong file extension

GitHub issue: #180

**MAJOR** · CONFIRMED · doc-drift · effort M

- **Locations**: `AI/VERIFICATION_MATRIX.md:397`, `AI/VERIFICATION_MATRIX.md:405`, `AI/VERIFICATION_MATRIX.md:370`, `crates/admin-cli/tests/gdm_tests.rs:15-94`, `crates/admin-cli/src/gdm.rs:457`, `crates/admin-cli/src/gdm.rs:465`, `Docs/ENROLLMENT_CLI.md:22-70`
- **Evidence**: VERIFICATION_MATRIX.md:397 lists `gdm_tests::test_gdm_status_unconfigured`, `test_gdm_status_configured_enabled`, `test_gdm_status_configured_disabled`, `test_gdm_enable_idempotent`, `test_gdm_disable_creates_flag`; :405 lists `test_gdm_pam_line_includes_timeout_ms_2500` and a `GDM_PAM_LINE` constant. `grep -rl <name> crates/ tests/` returned nothing for all seven identifiers. gdm_tests.rs contains exactly two tests (`test_gdm_subcommand_parsing`, `test_gdm_disable_and_enable_lifecycle`); the PAM line is a string literal duplicated at gdm.rs:457 and :465. VERIFICATION_MATRIX.md:370 (GEPU1) says templates are stored in `/var/lib/soos/biometrics/<uid>.bio` while store.rs:270 `TEMPLATE_EXTENSION = ".cbor.enc"`. `grep -rn "soos-admin gdm" Docs/ README.md` -> no hits; Docs/ENROLLMENT_CLI.md documents only enroll/verify/delete/list (no `import`, `debug-vision`, `--mock`).
- **Impact**: The traceability gate is satisfied by evidence that does not exist, so the GDM lockout-safety criterion (ASG5) is effectively unverified; operators have no documentation for the most dangerous privileged command.
- **Recommendation**: Either add the named tests (idempotent enable, status unconfigured/enabled/disabled, disable creates flag, line includes timeout) and a `pub const GDM_PAM_LINE`, or correct the matrix rows to the real test names. Fix GEPU1's extension. Document `soos-admin gdm|add-user`, `soos-enroll import|debug-vision`, `--mock` in Docs/. Add a repository invariant test that every backtick-quoted `<file>::test_*` reference in AI/VERIFICATION_MATRIX.md resolves to an existing `fn` in the tree.
- **Verifier notes**: AI/VERIFICATION_MATRIX.md:397 (ASG5) lists gdm_tests::test_gdm_status_unconfigured, _configured_enabled, _configured_disabled, test_gdm_enable_idempotent, test_gdm_disable_creates_flag; :405 (LSF1) lists test_gdm_pam_line_includes_timeout_ms_2500 and `GDM_PAM_LINE`. `grep -rl` for each of the seven identifiers over crates/ and tests/ returned nothing. crates/admin-cli/tests/gdm_tests.rs contains only test_gdm_subcommand_parsing (:16) and test_gdm_disable_and_enable_lifecycle (:46). The PAM line is a duplicated string literal at gdm.rs:97 and :105 (not :457/:465). :370 (GEPU1) says `<uid>.bio` while store.rs:16 `TEMPLATE_EXTENSION = ".cbor.enc"`. `grep -rln "soos-admin gdm" Docs README.md` -> no hits (only AI/BACKLOG.md, AI/VERIFICATION_MATRIX.md, walkthroughs 72/73). Docs/ENROLLMENT_CLI.md has no `import`, `debug-vision` or `--mock` entries (grep). All claims hold.

#### STO-08 — Evidence snapshots store raw frame bytes without width/height/pixel-format, named .webp.enc; the stored evidence cannot be decoded, and the CBOR plaintext is not zeroized

GitHub issue: #181

**MAJOR** · CONFIRMED · bug · effort M

- **Locations**: `crates/evidence-store/src/snapshot.rs:338-349`, `crates/evidence-store/src/store.rs:623-635`, `crates/daemon/src/dispatcher.rs:281-287`, `Docs/EVIDENCE_STORE_CRATE.md:58`, `crates/camera-v4l/src/frame.rs:48-61`
- **Evidence**: EvidenceRecord (snapshot.rs:338-349) has fields snapshot_id, uid, timestamp, reason, image_data only. The daemon passes `&frame.data` (dispatcher.rs:284), i.e. the raw V4L buffer whose interpretation requires `frame.width/height/format` (frame.rs:50-58), none of which is persisted. store.rs:634 names the file `{snapshot_id}.webp.enc` and Docs/EVIDENCE_STORE_CRATE.md:58 shows a WebP/JPEG frame, but no encoder exists in the crate. The only consumer (daemon/tests/pipeline_integration_tests.rs:388-398) checks `record.uid` only. store.rs:623-632: `record` and `serialized` (plaintext frame) are plain Vec<u8>, dropped without zeroization although ADR 2026-09-16 requires Zeroize for raw frames; no decrypt/export tool exists in admin-cli.
- **Impact**: The opt-in intrusion-evidence feature produces files nobody can render (unknown resolution/format), so it delivers no forensic value while still storing sensitive images; plaintext frames linger in freed heap.
- **Recommendation**: Add width, height, pixel_format (and frame sequence) to EvidenceRecord, encode to a real container (or at least name files `.raw.enc`), wrap `serialized`/`image_data` in Zeroizing, and add a `soos-admin evidence export` that decrypts to a root-only path. Test: store a mock frame, load it, and reconstruct an RGB image with the stored dimensions.
- **Verifier notes**: crates/evidence-store/src/snapshot.rs:9-20 EvidenceRecord = {snapshot_id, uid, timestamp, reason, image_data} only (finding cited 338-349). Daemon dispatcher.rs:281-287 passes `&frame.data` (raw V4L buffer) with no width/height/format. store.rs:154-166 builds `record` and `serialized` as plain Vec<u8>/`record.to_cbor()` with no Zeroizing (finding cited 623-635); file named `{snapshot_id}.webp.enc` at :165. evidence-store/Cargo.toml has no image/webp encoder dependency (aes-gcm, ciborium, serde, zeroize, thiserror, getrandom, nix only). Docs/EVIDENCE_STORE_CRATE.md:45-48 shows .webp.enc names and :95 'JPEG/WebP camera frame bytes'. daemon/tests/pipeline_integration_tests.rs:393-398 asserts only `record.uid`. AI/DECISIONS.md:15 requires Zeroize for raw frames. No admin-cli export subcommand exists (admin-cli/src/lib.rs modules: args, error, gdm, logs, redact, status, test_pam, user).

#### STO-09 — soos-enroll enroll records model_id=mobilefacenet/1.0.0 by default although the pipeline is ArcFace w600k 2.0.0; import and GUI use the correct ids

GitHub issue: #182

**MAJOR** · CONFIRMED · bug · effort S

- **Locations**: `crates/enrollment-cli/src/args.rs:878-884`, `crates/enrollment-cli/src/args.rs:938-944`, `crates/gui/src/app.rs:828-829`, `crates/enrollment-cli/src/service.rs:395-401`, `Docs/ENROLLMENT_CLI.md:30-31`, `crates/enrollment-cli/tests/enroll_tests.rs:81-94`
- **Evidence**: args.rs:878-884 `#[arg(long, default_value = "mobilefacenet")] pub model_id` / `default_value = "1.0.0"`; args.rs:938-944 ImportArgs default `"arcface_w600k_mbf"` / `"2.0.0"`; gui/app.rs:828-829 hard-codes `"arcface_w600k_mbf".to_string(), "2.0.0"`. service.rs:395-401 stores `args.model_id` verbatim; the actual extractor is MODEL_ID_EMBEDDING = "arcface_w600k_mbf" (service.rs:47, :770). AI/DECISIONS.md 2026-09-20 retired mobilefacenet. enroll_tests.rs:93-94 asserts the mobilefacenet default. Nothing in the daemon reads model_id (grep for model_id in crates/daemon/src returned nothing), so the B3 "model migration" metadata is inert and wrong.
- **Impact**: Every CLI-enrolled template carries false provenance; any future migration logic keyed on model_id would treat real ArcFace templates as legacy and re-enrollment would be forced or skipped incorrectly.
- **Recommendation**: Derive model_id/model_version from the loaded registry (MODEL_ID_EMBEDDING + manifest version) and drop the user-facing flags or make them override-only with a warning; update Docs/ENROLLMENT_CLI.md and the enroll test expectations (test contract change must be justified by the ADR). Add a daemon-side check that rejects templates whose model_id differs from the loaded extractor.
- **Verifier notes**: args.rs:89-94 `default_value = "mobilefacenet"` / `"1.0.0"` for EnrollArgs (finding cited 878-884); args.rs:149-154 ImportArgs defaults `arcface_w600k_mbf` / `2.0.0`; gui/src/app.rs:828-829 hard-codes `"arcface_w600k_mbf"`, `"2.0.0"`. service.rs:377-378 and :396-397 store `args.model_id`/`args.model_version` verbatim; the real extractor session is loaded by MODEL_ID_EMBEDDING at service.rs:770. AI/DECISIONS.md:16 (2026-09-20) replaced MobileFaceNet 128D with ArcFace w600k 512D. `grep -rn model_id crates/daemon/src` -> nothing. enroll_tests.rs:93-94 assert the mobilefacenet default and Docs/ENROLLMENT_CLI.md:36 documents it. Confirmed.

#### STO-10 — Guided enrollment fuses samples with no identity-consistency check, no finiteness check, and pose upper bounds documented but not enforced

GitHub issue: #183

**MAJOR** · CONFIRMED · design · effort M

- **Locations**: `crates/enrollment-cli/src/guided_enrollment.rs:725`, `crates/enrollment-cli/src/guided_enrollment.rs:741-746`, `crates/enrollment-cli/src/guided_enrollment.rs:762-767`, `crates/enrollment-cli/src/guided_enrollment.rs:783-788`, `crates/enrollment-cli/src/guided_enrollment.rs:605-612`, `crates/enrollment-cli/src/guided_enrollment.rs:807-848`, `crates/enrollment-cli/tests/guided_enrollment_tests.rs:27-117`
- **Evidence**: process_sample pushes `embedding.to_vec()` unconditionally once pose gates pass (lines 725, 746, 767, 788); nothing compares a new sample to the frontal mean (cosine_similarity is available in soos_vision). Enum docs (607-612) specify yaw in [-25,-10]/[10,25] and pitch in [-25,-8], but the code checks only `pose.yaw > -10.0` (742), `pose.yaw < 10.0` (763), `pose.pitch > -5.0` (784): yaw -80 or pitch -60 are accepted and TiltUp uses -5 not -8. compute_composite_embedding (807-848) sums and L2-normalizes; NaN in any sample propagates (no is_finite check; BiometricTemplate::new will then reject the whole fused vector at app.rs:827 with no user feedback). The GUI worker (worker.rs:98-112) feeds every frame's embedding; the session is a single 4x4-sample buffer (app.rs:794). The only test (guided_enrollment_tests.rs) covers the happy path with identical embeddings and never exercises a second identity, extreme poses, or NaN.
- **Impact**: A second person in front of the camera during the TurnLeft/TurnRight/TiltUp steps (or a handed-over device) contaminates the stored template with another identity, degrading FAR for the enrolled user; extreme poses produce low-quality samples that pull the composite away from frontal appearance.
- **Recommendation**: Reject a sample whose cosine similarity to the running frontal mean is below a conservative bound (e.g. 0.5) and return a new `IdentityMismatch` feedback; enforce the documented upper pose bounds; reject non-finite embeddings; document the fusion formula (mean direction). Tests: inject an orthogonal embedding during TurnLeft and assert it is rejected and the composite equals the frontal direction; yaw -80 -> Prompt feedback; NaN sample -> rejected.
- **Verifier notes**: guided_enrollment.rs (267 lines; finding's line numbers are wrong): process_sample pushes `embedding.to_vec()` unconditionally at :143, :164, :185, :206 after pose gates; gates are only `pose.yaw > -10.0` (:160), `pose.yaw < 10.0` (:181), `pose.pitch > -5.0` (:202) — no upper bounds and TiltUp uses -5 while the enum docs at :25-30 specify [-25,-10], [10,25], [-25,-8]. No cosine/identity check against frontal samples; no is_finite check anywhere in the file (grep). compute_composite_embedding :225-266 sums then L2-normalizes; a NaN sample makes norm NaN, `norm <= 1e-6` is false, and a NaN vector is returned; BiometricTemplate::new (template.rs:55) then rejects it, and app.rs:815-818 turns `.ok()` into None silently. Only test: guided_enrollment_tests.rs has a single fn test_guided_enrollment_full_workflow (:27). worker.rs:98-112 feeds every frame's embedding; app.rs:794 `GuidedEnrollmentSession::new(4)`. Confirmed.

#### STO-11 — Under sudo, soos-enroll defaults the target to real UID 0 and silently enrolls root's template

GitHub issue: #184

**MAJOR** · CONFIRMED · bug · effort S

- **Locations**: `crates/enrollment-cli/src/args.rs:947-963`, `crates/enrollment-cli/src/service.rs:296`, `Docs/ENROLLMENT_CLI.md:27`
- **Evidence**: args.rs:962 `Ok(nix::unistd::getuid().as_raw())` when neither --uid nor --username is given; main.rs:65 requires EUID 0, so in practice the tool always runs under sudo/root where getuid() is also 0 (sudo sets real uid to 0). Docs/ENROLLMENT_CLI.md:27 "Defaults to caller UID if unspecified" and the enroll summary (main.rs:73) prints "Target UID: 0" only after frames were captured. SUDO_UID is never consulted.
- **Impact**: An administrator running `sudo soos-enroll enroll` enrolls their face as root; with pam_soos in the sudo/su stacks this grants facial root authentication that the admin did not intend, and their own account remains unenrolled.
- **Recommendation**: Require --uid/--username explicitly (error if absent) or resolve SUDO_UID/PKEXEC_UID only when present and refuse uid 0 without an explicit `--allow-root` flag. Test: resolve_target_uid(None, None) with SUDO_UID set returns that uid; with none set returns an error.
- **Verifier notes**: args.rs:158-173 resolve_target_uid falls through to `nix::unistd::getuid().as_raw()` at :172 (finding cited 947-963); service.rs:164-169 check_privileges requires geteuid()==0 and main.rs:32 enforces it before parsing; sudo sets real uid to 0 as well, so the default target is 0. SUDO_UID/PKEXEC_UID never consulted (grep on crates/enrollment-cli: none). Docs/ENROLLMENT_CLI.md:32 'Defaults to caller UID if unspecified'. Enrollment captures frames (service.rs:304-347) before the summary/prompt (:372-384), so the uid is only shown after capture. No test calls resolve_target_uid(None, None). Confirmed.

#### STO-12 — GUI import path writes the plaintext fused embedding to a world-readable file in /tmp for the duration of the pkexec prompt (import-flow boundary, shared with GUI reviewer)

GitHub issue: #156

**MAJOR** · CONFIRMED · security · effort S

- **Locations**: `crates/gui/src/app.rs:842-853`, `crates/gui/src/app.rs:857-865`, `crates/enrollment-cli/src/service.rs:589`
- **Evidence**: app.rs:842-846 `std::env::temp_dir().join(format!(".soos_gui_import_{}_{}.json", uid, pid))`; :848-852 `serde_json::to_string(&fused_embedding)` then `std::fs::write(&tmp_path, json)` (umask-default mode, follows symlinks, predictable name); :857-865 then spawns `pkexec soos-enroll import --uid .. --file ..` and waits for the interactive authentication; :867 removes the file afterwards. service.rs:589 reads the file as root with `std::fs::read` (no size bound, no ownership check). No polkit .policy file exists in packaging/ (find returned nothing), so pkexec falls back to admin authentication.
- **Impact**: Any local user can read the 512-D biometric template of the enrolling user while the password dialog is open (seconds to minutes), and can pre-create the predictable path as a symlink so the GUI user overwrites one of their own files. Zero-trust invariant "never store embeddings in cleartext" is violated.
- **Recommendation**: Pass the embedding over a pipe (stdin of the pkexec child) or write it with create_new(true).mode(0o600) into a per-user private directory ($XDG_RUNTIME_DIR), and make `import` cap the file size (e.g. 64 KiB) and verify the file's owner equals PKEXEC_UID. Test: import rejects a file larger than the cap and a file not owned by the invoking user.
- **Verifier notes**: gui/src/app.rs:841-846 `std::env::temp_dir().join(format!(".soos_gui_import_{}_{}.json", uid, pid))`; :848-853 `serde_json::to_string(&fused_embedding)` then `std::fs::write(&tmp_path, json)` (umask mode, symlink-following, predictable name); :857-866 spawns `pkexec soos-enroll import --uid .. --file ..` and `.status()` blocks on the polkit prompt; :868 removes the file. service.rs:589 `std::fs::read(&args.file)?` with no size cap and no owner/PKEXEC_UID check. `find . -name '*.policy'` -> nothing; no pkexec/polkit files under packaging/ or scripts/. Cleartext 512-D template readable by any local user during the prompt — confirmed.

#### STO-05 — Multi-frame enrollment re-reads the same cached frame N times (no sequence/timestamp check) and aborts on the first PAD-rejected frame

GitHub issue: #228

**MINOR** · PARTIAL · bug · effort S · (reviewer severity: MAJOR)

- **Locations**: `crates/enrollment-cli/src/service.rs:272-277`, `crates/enrollment-cli/src/service.rs:304-306`, `crates/enrollment-cli/src/service.rs:346`, `crates/camera-v4l/src/v4l_impl.rs:76-81`, `crates/camera-v4l/src/frame.rs:56-60`
- **Evidence**: service.rs:272-277 `for _ in 0..200 { if let Some(frame) = camera.latest_frame() { return Ok(frame); } sleep(10ms) }` returns immediately whenever a frame is cached; service.rs:304-306 loops `for idx in 0..frames_to_capture { let frame = self.acquire_frame()?; match pipeline.process_frame(&frame) {` with no sleep and no comparison of `frame.sequence`/`frame.timestamp_mono_ns` (fields exist, frame.rs:56-60). v4l_impl.rs:80 `self.latest_frame.load_full()` returns the same Arc until the capture thread swaps it. At 30 FPS the 5-iteration loop completes in a few ms, so candidates are typically identical. service.rs:346 `Err(e) => return Err(e.into())` covers VisionError::PadFailed (vision/error.rs:50-55), so one spoof-flagged frame aborts the whole enrollment instead of counting as an invalid candidate. The mock camera used by enroll_tests cannot detect this.
- **Impact**: The advertised quality selection across N frames (Docs/ENROLLMENT_CLI.md `--frames`, `frames_evaluated` in the summary) is illusory; enrollment quality is a single frame. Transient PAD rejections (which STO-01 makes systematic) abort enrollment.
- **Recommendation**: Track the last `sequence` and wait (bounded, e.g. up to 500 ms) for a frame with a greater sequence before each evaluation; treat PadFailed like NoFaceDetected (invalid candidate, counted in the summary). Test: a mock camera that swaps frames with increasing sequence only every 50 ms; assert the N candidates carry N distinct sequences; a test where the PAD mock rejects the first frame asserts enrollment still succeeds on later frames.
- **Verifier notes**: Code confirmed: service.rs:272-277 returns the first cached frame; :304-306 loops `for idx in 0..frames_to_capture { let frame = self.acquire_frame()?; match pipeline.process_frame(&frame)` with no sleep and no sequence/timestamp comparison (fields exist at camera-v4l/src/frame.rs:52-60); v4l_impl.rs:76-80 `latest_frame.load_full()` returns the same Arc until swapped; :346 `Err(e) => return Err(e.into())` aborts on PadFailed. REFUTED detail: 'the 5-iteration loop completes in a few ms, so candidates are typically identical' — each iteration runs the full ONNX pipeline (AI/ARCHITECTURE.md:198-203 budgets ~100 ms per frame: 40 ms SCRFD + 30 ms PAD + 30 ms ArcFace), i.e. ~3 frame periods at 30 FPS, so successive iterations usually see different frames; duplicates occur only when inference is faster than the frame interval or the camera runs at low FPS. The quality selection is therefore not 'illusory', just unguaranteed. The PadFailed-abort is real but, once STO-01 is fixed, is a transient-only edge; overall MINOR (robustness). Recommendation (wait for `sequence` to advance, count PadFailed as invalid candidate) is sound.

#### STO-13 — Log redaction is bypassed by any character whose lowercase form changes UTF-8 length (index mismatch between lowered and original string)

GitHub issue: #229

**MINOR** · CONFIRMED · security · effort S

- **Locations**: `crates/admin-cli/src/redact.rs:489-493`, `crates/admin-cli/src/redact.rs:439-446`, `crates/admin-cli/src/redact.rs:595-600`
- **Evidence**: redact_kv_field lowercases into `lower` (489) and searches it (493) but slices the original `input` with the same byte offsets (500-501, 507, 515). Scratch probe: `default_redact("user=bob password=hunter2 done")` -> `password=[REDACTED]` but `default_redact("user=İbob password=hunter2 done")` -> `user=İbob password=hunter2 done` (U+0130 lowercases to 3 bytes). Same pattern in redact_brackets (440) and redact_bearer_token (597).
- **Impact**: `soos-admin logs` promises redaction of secrets, keys and embeddings; a single non-ASCII character earlier on the line (e.g. in a logged username or service string) disables it for the rest of the line.
- **Recommendation**: Do a case-insensitive search that preserves byte positions (compare `input.as_bytes()` windows with eq_ignore_ascii_case, or iterate char_indices on the original), never index `input` with offsets computed from `to_lowercase()`. Test: the İ and KELVIN SIGN cases above must redact.
- **Verifier notes**: redact.rs:108-138: `let lower = input.to_lowercase(); ... lower.get(cursor..).find(key)` then `input.get(match_idx-1..match_idx)` / `input.get(cursor..key_end)` — byte offsets from `lower` applied to `input` (finding's 489-515 line numbers are wrong; same pattern in redact_brackets :55-60 and redact_bearer_token :214-217). Scratch probe output: `redact("user=bob password=hunter2 done") -> "user=bob password=[REDACTED] done"`, `redact("user=İbob password=hunter2 done") -> "user=İbob password=hunter2 done"` (no redaction), `redact("İ Bearer abcdef123456 x") -> "İ Bearer a[REDACTED] x"` (off-by-one leak of the first token byte). Confirmed.

#### STO-14 — Master key loading accepts world-readable/foreign-owned key files, evidence key open lacks O_NOFOLLOW, and parent-dir creation forces 0700 on /var/lib/soos (spec 0755)

GitHub issue: #230

**MINOR** · CONFIRMED · security · effort S

- **Locations**: `crates/biometric-store/src/crypto.rs:133-143`, `crates/biometric-store/src/crypto.rs:145-149`, `crates/evidence-store/src/crypto.rs:209-215`, `crates/evidence-store/src/crypto.rs:217-222`, `packaging/debian/postinst:25`
- **Evidence**: biometric crypto.rs:133-140 reads an existing key with O_NOFOLLOW but never inspects mode/owner; scratch probe: key file chmod 0644 -> `load_or_create on 0644 key: true`. evidence crypto.rs:209-210 `if path.exists() { let mut file = File::open(path)?;` (no O_NOFOLLOW after the symlink_metadata check at 200-207, TOCTOU) unlike the biometric crate. Both crates (bio 145-149, ev 217-222) `create_dir_all(parent)` then `set_permissions(parent, 0o700)`; for the default `/var/lib/soos/master.key` the parent is `/var/lib/soos`, which project-facts §3 and postinst:25 (`chmod 0755 /var/lib/soos`) define as 0755 (models dir must stay traversable). `read_to_end` (bio 138-139, ev 211-212) is unbounded.
- **Impact**: A key leaked to 0644 by an operator mistake keeps being used silently; evidence key open is not symlink-safe; a first run of soos-enroll on an unprovisioned host makes /var/lib/soos 0700 and later breaks non-root access to models.
- **Recommendation**: After open, fstat the key: require regular file, uid 0, mode & 0o077 == 0, len == 32 (read at most 33 bytes); use custom_flags(O_NOFOLLOW) in the evidence crate; do not chmod pre-existing parents and create only the leaf dir. Tests: 0644 key -> KeyError; symlinked evidence key path -> error; existing 0755 parent unchanged.
- **Verifier notes**: biometric-store/src/crypto.rs:79-89 opens an existing key with O_NOFOLLOW but never checks mode/owner/len (read_to_end unbounded) — scratch probe with a 0644 key: `load_or_create on 0644 key: true`. :91-95 `create_dir_all(parent)` + `set_permissions(parent, 0o700)` when parent is missing; for DEFAULT_KEY_PATH the parent is /var/lib/soos, which project-facts.md:65 and packaging/debian/postinst:25 (`chmod 0755 /var/lib/soos`) define as 0755. evidence-store/src/crypto.rs:78-84 `if path.exists() { let mut file = File::open(path)?;` — no O_NOFOLLOW after the symlink_metadata check at :68-76 (TOCTOU), unlike the biometric crate; :86-89 same parent chmod. Line numbers in the finding are wrong but every claim holds.

#### STO-15 — soos-admin test-pam sends a wall-clock deadline in a CLOCK_MONOTONIC field, so --timeout-ms never bounds the daemon and 0 causes EINVAL

GitHub issue: #231

**MINOR** · CONFIRMED · bug · effort S

- **Locations**: `crates/admin-cli/src/test_pam.rs:779-783`, `crates/admin-cli/src/test_pam.rs:698-704`, `crates/daemon/src/pipeline.rs:68-74`, `crates/daemon/src/dispatcher.rs:608-617`, `Docs/IPC_PROTOCOL.md:54`
- **Evidence**: test_pam.rs:779-783 `fn get_monotonic_ns() -> u64 { SystemTime::now().duration_since(UNIX_EPOCH)... }` (wall clock, ~1.7e18 ns) is used for deadline_monotonic_ns (710-711). The daemon's clock is CLOCK_MONOTONIC (pipeline.rs:68-74, dispatcher.rs:68). dispatcher.rs:608-617 computes `client_budget = deadline - now_ns` (huge) then `min(connection_timeout - 50ms)`, so the CLI's timeout is ignored server-side. test_pam.rs:698-704 passes `Duration::from_millis(timeout_ms)` to set_read_timeout without clamping; `--timeout-ms 0` yields an InvalidInput error instead of a validation message.
- **Impact**: The diagnostic reports latencies against a budget the daemon never applies, so it does not reproduce PAM behaviour (which uses the clamped timeout_ms), and misleads operators tuning timeout_ms.
- **Recommendation**: Use nix::time::clock_gettime(CLOCK_MONOTONIC) (already a dependency) and clamp timeout_ms to the PAM MIN/MAX (10..5000). Test: assert the request's deadline is within [now_mono, now_mono + timeout] using the same clock.
- **Verifier notes**: admin-cli/src/test_pam.rs:197-199 `fn get_monotonic_ns() -> u64 { SystemTime::now().duration_since(UNIX_EPOCH)` (wall clock); :128-129 deadline = that + timeout; :116-118 `Duration::from_millis(timeout_ms)` passed to set_read_timeout unclamped — scratch probe: `set_read_timeout(0) -> InvalidInput "cannot set a 0 duration timeout"`. Daemon uses CLOCK_MONOTONIC (pipeline.rs:68-74, pam/src/ipc.rs:427). Server side (corrected lines): dispatcher.rs:484 rejects only if now >= deadline (never true with a ~1.7e18 wall-clock value) and :532-545 clamps remaining to min(1200 ms, connection_timeout-100 ms) — there is no `client_budget` variable and the finding's 608-617 / '-50ms' details are inaccurate, but the conclusion (CLI timeout is never applied by the daemon) holds. Note for the fix: admin-cli's nix dependency lacks the `time` feature (admin-cli/Cargo.toml:21 vs daemon/Cargo.toml:20 `features = ["time"]`); PAM clamps to 10..5000 (pam/src/config.rs:12-15).

#### STO-16 — Hand-rolled JSON output without escaping in three places although serde_json is a dependency

GitHub issue: #232

**MINOR** · CONFIRMED · bug · effort S

- **Locations**: `crates/enrollment-cli/src/main.rs:166-169`, `crates/admin-cli/src/status.rs:36-48`, `crates/admin-cli/src/test_pam.rs:635-645`
- **Evidence**: main.rs:166-169 formats `"username": "{}", "model_id": "{}", "model_version": "{}"` from CLI-provided strings (model_id/model_version are free-form args); status.rs:38-47 interpolates socket_path and unit; test_pam.rs:636-641 interpolates `service` and `{:?}` enum names. A value containing `"` or `\` produces invalid JSON. EnrolledUserSummary already derives serde::Serialize (service.rs:216).
- **Impact**: `--format json` consumers (scripts, the GUI's refresh_profiles at app.rs:163) break on paths/ids containing quotes or backslashes.
- **Recommendation**: Use serde_json::to_string_pretty on the report structs (derive Serialize where missing). Test: a socket path containing a double quote round-trips through serde_json::from_str.
- **Verifier notes**: enrollment-cli/src/main.rs:133-135 hand-formats `"username": "{}", "model_id": "{}", "model_version": "{}"` from free-form CLI strings (finding cited 166-169); admin-cli/src/status.rs:35-45 `to_json` interpolates socket_path and systemd_unit; test_pam.rs:53 format! JSON. No escaping. serde_json is a dependency of both crates (Cargo.toml) and EnrolledUserSummary derives Serialize (service.rs:216). Confirmed.

#### STO-17 — Dead or duplicated code: encode_bmp, build_service alias, AlreadyEnrolled never produced, symlink-unsafe secure_shred_file used only by its own tests

GitHub issue: #233

**MINOR** · CONFIRMED · dead-code · effort S

- **Locations**: `crates/enrollment-cli/src/html_report.rs:413-454`, `crates/enrollment-cli/src/service.rs:788-790`, `crates/enrollment-cli/src/error.rs:15-17`, `crates/enrollment-cli/src/service.rs:386-388`, `crates/enrollment-cli/src/shred.rs:348-361`, `crates/enrollment-cli/src/lib.rs:33`
- **Evidence**: `grep -rn encode_bmp crates/ --include='*.rs'` matches only its definition; html_report.rs:577 comment "Actually just raw RGB base64 now". `build_service` (service.rs:788-790) is referenced only by lib.rs:27. `AlreadyEnrolled` is never constructed (only matched in enroll_tests.rs:170); service.rs:386-388 is an empty `else if already_enrolled { // Overwriting silently allowed }` branch and the summary never tells the admin an existing template will be replaced. `secure_shred_file` is only called from shred_tests.rs; it duplicates BiometricStore::delete's overwrite with 2 passes instead of 3, uses `std::fs::metadata` (follows symlinks) and `OpenOptions::write(true).open(path)` without O_NOFOLLOW (shred.rs:357-361), so a symlink argument would make root overwrite the target.
- **Impact**: Two divergent shred implementations with different guarantees; a public symlink-unsafe root primitive; misleading error variant and docs.
- **Recommendation**: Delete encode_bmp, build_service and shred.rs (or make delete() the single implementation and have shred.rs call it with O_NOFOLLOW + symlink_metadata); either return AlreadyEnrolled when enrolled and !yes before capturing frames, or remove the variant and surface `already_enrolled` in EnrollmentSummary so the prompt mentions overwrite. Retire shred_tests.rs with the code (test removal justified by the dead-code ADR, not by weakening).
- **Verifier notes**: `grep -rn encode_bmp crates` -> only html_report.rs:12 definition; html_report.rs:176 'Actually just raw RGB base64 now'. `build_service` (service.rs:788-790) referenced only by lib.rs:27. `AlreadyEnrolled` (error.rs:15) never constructed; only matched in enroll_tests.rs:170; service.rs:386-388 empty `else if already_enrolled {}` branch. `secure_shred_file` (shred.rs:13-66) called only from tests/shred_tests.rs; uses `std::fs::metadata` (:22, follows symlinks) and `OpenOptions::new().write(true).open(path)` (:26, no O_NOFOLLOW), 2 passes vs BiometricStore::delete's 3 (store.rs:179). Confirmed.

#### STO-18 — Evidence daily-cap counter is in-memory only, never pruned, and consumed before the write succeeds

GitHub issue: #234

**MINOR** · CONFIRMED · reliability · effort S

- **Locations**: `crates/evidence-store/src/store.rs:498`, `crates/evidence-store/src/store.rs:576-588`, `crates/evidence-store/src/store.rs:646-667`
- **Evidence**: store.rs:498 `daily_counts: Mutex<HashMap<(u32, String), u32>>`; 578-587 increments the entry before any I/O; nothing removes keys for past dates (scratch probe: after rotate_retention pruned 2026-09-21, `daily_count(1000, "2026-09-21")` still returned 1). A daemon restart resets all counts; a failed write (646-667) still consumes a slot.
- **Impact**: Cap is not enforced across restarts (disk-exhaustion protection is weaker than documented) and the map grows by one entry per (uid, day) for the daemon's lifetime; transient I/O failures burn the user's quota.
- **Recommendation**: Derive the count from the number of files for (date, uid) on disk (or persist a small counter file per date dir), prune map keys in rotate_retention, and increment only after a successful rename. Test: restart-simulation (new EvidenceStore on same dir) still enforces the cap.
- **Verifier notes**: evidence-store/src/store.rs:29 `daily_counts: Mutex<HashMap<(u32, String), u32>>`; :108-118 increments the entry before any I/O; rotate_retention (:262-324) removes directories but never touches daily_counts; write failures at :178-198 do not decrement. In-memory only, reset on restart. Confirmed (line numbers corrected).

#### STO-19 — list decrypts every full template (embedding included) to print metadata; template/evidence reads are unbounded

GitHub issue: #235

**MINOR** · CONFIRMED · performance · effort S

- **Locations**: `crates/enrollment-cli/src/service.rs:558-576`, `crates/biometric-store/src/store.rs:383-391`, `crates/evidence-store/src/store.rs:683-685`
- **Evidence**: service.rs:561-576 calls `self.store.get(uid)` per uid, which reads (`read_to_end`, store.rs:387-388) and decrypts the whole template into memory just to copy model_id/model_version/timestamp/dim. No maximum file size is enforced before allocation in either store (store.rs:387, evidence store.rs:684).
- **Impact**: Every `list` (and the GUI's periodic profile refresh through it) materialises all users' embeddings in memory; a corrupted/huge file causes an unbounded allocation in a root process.
- **Recommendation**: Cap reads (e.g. 64 KiB for templates, configurable MiB for evidence) and return CorruptFile beyond it; optionally store metadata in an authenticated header (AAD) so list can avoid decrypting embeddings. Test: a 10 MiB junk `.cbor.enc` file makes get() return CorruptFile quickly.
- **Verifier notes**: service.rs:558-576 list() calls `self.store.get(uid)?` per uid and copies only metadata; store.rs:129-134 get() reads the whole file with `read_to_end` (no size cap) and decrypts; evidence store.rs:214-216 load_snapshot likewise unbounded `read_to_end`. Confirmed (line numbers corrected).

#### STO-20 — verify reports pad_result "PASSED" as a literal instead of the real PAD score/class; gdm status treats commented-out lines as installed

GitHub issue: #236

**MINOR** · CONFIRMED · bug · effort S

- **Locations**: `crates/enrollment-cli/src/service.rs:467-468`, `crates/enrollment-cli/tests/verify_tests.rs:125`, `crates/admin-cli/src/gdm.rs:385-388`
- **Evidence**: service.rs:468 `pad_result: "PASSED".to_string()` although PipelineOutput carries `pad_result: PadResult` with score/is_live (vision/pipeline.rs:63-64); verify_tests.rs:125 asserts the literal. gdm.rs:386-387 `content.contains("pam_soos.so")`; scratch probe with only `# auth sufficient pam_soos.so` -> `installed: true`, so `gdm enable` (gdm.rs:451) would also skip insertion on a commented line.
- **Impact**: Operators cannot see the liveness score that governs the daemon verdict (which hides STO-01), and gdm status/enable misreport a disabled configuration as installed.
- **Recommendation**: Report `PASSED (score=0.97)` / include score in the report struct (extend, do not weaken, the test); parse PAM lines ignoring `#` comments. Tests: mock PAD score is echoed; commented line -> installed=false and enable inserts a real line.
- **Verifier notes**: service.rs:468 `pad_result: "PASSED".to_string()` and verify_tests.rs:125 `assert_eq!(report.pad_result, "PASSED")`. gdm.rs:27 and :91 `content.contains("pam_soos.so")` (finding cited 385-388). Scratch probe with a file containing only `# auth sufficient pam_soos.so`: `gdm status with commented line: installed=true enabled=true`, and `configure_gdm(Enable)` left the file unchanged (no real line inserted). Confirmed.

#### STO-21 — soos-enroll requires root even for --help/--version, and import silently overwrites an existing template

GitHub issue: #237

**MINOR** · CONFIRMED · design · effort S

- **Locations**: `crates/enrollment-cli/src/main.rs:64-66`, `crates/enrollment-cli/src/service.rs:584-628`
- **Evidence**: main.rs:65-66 `check_privileges(true)?; let cli = Cli::parse();` so a non-root `soos-enroll --help` prints `[ERROR] Root privileges (EUID 0) are required` instead of help. import() (584-628) never calls `store.exists(uid)` and has no --yes/confirm path, unlike enroll and delete.
- **Impact**: Poor operability for the pkexec/GUI flow and shell completion; an import can clobber a good template without any notice.
- **Recommendation**: Parse args first, then check privileges; add an `already_enrolled` check to import with `--yes` semantics matching enroll. Test: `Cli::try_parse_from(["soos-enroll","--help"])` succeeds without root; import onto an existing uid without --yes returns AlreadyEnrolled.
- **Verifier notes**: main.rs:32-33 `check_privileges(true)?; let cli = Cli::parse();` (finding cited 64-66) — root required before --help/--version. import() at service.rs:583-637 never calls `store.exists(uid)` and ImportArgs has no --yes; enroll (:299) and delete (:541-547) do. Confirmed.

#### STO-22 — AES-GCM payloads carry no associated data (uid, file name, format version); rollback of an older template for the same uid is undetectable

GitHub issue: #266

**SUGGESTION** · CONFIRMED · improvement · effort S

- **Locations**: `crates/biometric-store/src/crypto.rs:183-207`, `crates/biometric-store/src/store.rs:393-398`, `crates/evidence-store/src/crypto.rs:260-284`
- **Evidence**: encrypt_payload/decrypt_payload use `cipher.encrypt(nonce, plaintext)` with no AAD (crypto.rs:191-193, 249-251). Cross-user file swap is caught only by the plaintext uid check after decryption (store.rs:393-398; scratch probe: `get(1001)` on a copied 1000 file -> CorruptFile). Evidence records have no binding to their path/date at all. Nonces are 96-bit random per write, which is fine for these volumes.
- **Impact**: Defense-in-depth gap only (root attackers are out of scope): an older, still-valid ciphertext for the same uid can be restored undetected; evidence files can be moved between date partitions.
- **Recommendation**: Pass `format!("soos-bio-v1:{uid}")` (and for evidence `date/uuid`) as AAD via `Payload { msg, aad }`, keeping the plaintext uid check; consider a monotonically increasing enrollment counter in the AAD to detect rollback. Test: decrypt with a different uid AAD fails before CBOR parsing.
- **Verifier notes**: biometric-store/src/crypto.rs:137-139 `cipher.encrypt(nonce, plaintext)` and :195-197 `cipher.decrypt(nonce, ciphertext_slice)` with no Payload/AAD; evidence-store/src/crypto.rs:137-139 / :193-195 identical. Plaintext uid check after decrypt at store.rs:139-144 is the only binding; evidence records have none. Random 96-bit nonce per write is fine. Defense-in-depth only; SUGGESTION is right.

### 4.7 Vision & Inference Core

*Coverage*: Read in full: crates/vision/src/{lib,error,color,letterbox,crop,align,matcher,pipeline,pose}.rs; crates/inference-ort/src/{lib,error,detector,embedding,manifest,registry,landmarks}.rs; crates/inference-ort/src/pad.rs (tensor preparation, softmax, evaluate_liveness; decision semantics only as far as needed for wiring); models/manifest.toml; scripts/download_models.sh; Docs/VISION_CRATE.md; Docs/INFERENCE_ORT_CRATE.md; .agents/skills/dev-workflow/references/project-facts.md; models/README.md lines 1-60 plus grep. Read partially (grep + targeted sed): crates/daemon/src/pipeline.rs:165-205, crates/gui/src/main.rs:84-100, crates/enrollment-cli/src/service.rs:745-780, crates/daemon/src/dispatcher.rs:360-372, crates/camera-v4l/src/{frame,sensor}.rs, AI/ARCHITECTURE.md §7, AI/DECISIONS.md (vision ADRs), AI/VERIFICATION_MATRIX.md (V*, NGM*, PAD*, BIO1, ASG1 rows), walkthroughs 53/58/66/68/72/75/77 (grep only), test function lists of all vision and inference-ort test files, body of scrfd_tests::test_scrfd_supports_preactivated_probabilities and zeroize_tests imports. Verified externally: jpeg-decoder 0.3.2 source (default decoding_buffer_size_limit = usize::MAX), ort 2.0.0-rc.13 source (Session::run(&mut self), commit_from_memory exists), sha256sum and `strings` of /var/lib/soos/models/arcface_w600k_mbf.onnx (matches manifest; tf2onnx ResNet34). NOT covered: mock.rs bodies (API surface only), pad.rs decision policy (other reviewer), test bodies beyond the ones cited, no cargo build/test run (shared target dir), no model execution (scrfd and minifasnet ONNX files are not present on this machine, so the actual MiniFASNet class ordering could not be empirically re-verified), daemon config/dispatcher beyond the wiring lines cited.

<details><summary>Reviewer improvement notes</summary>

Ordered plan for the vision/inference core (each step is a separate topic branch following the 4-phase TDD cycle):

1. fix/pad-live-class-wiring (VIS-01, VIS-09): add `live_class_index` and `nms_iou_threshold` to `VisionPipelineConfig`; introduce one `VisionPipeline::from_registry(&mut ModelRegistry, &VisionPipelineConfig)` builder used by daemon, GUI and enrollment; delete the hard-coded `2`/`0.45`/`0.40`/`0.80` literals; tests asserting the built detectors' fields in each binary; resolve walkthrough 66 vs 72 with a model-gated fixture test and an ADR entry.

2. fix/mjpeg-bounds (VIS-02, VIS-11): `read_info` + dimension equality + `set_max_decoding_buffer_size`; drop or validate CMYK/L16; reject odd YUYV width; negative tests.

3. chore/model-contract-attestation (VIS-03, VIS-04): decide ResNet34 vs true MobileFaceNet, fix manifest/README/ADR; registry validates session input/output dims against manifest; load via `commit_from_memory` after hashing the same bytes; single hash pass; real-model `#[ignore]` tests for shapes and latency; update ARCHITECTURE §7 with measured p95.

4. chore/remove-legacy-inference (VIS-07, VIS-08): delete UltraFace/LandmarkDetector/mock landmark code and legacy-only tests, drop `ndarray`, purge legacy URLs, call `validate_output_shapes` at startup, sweep docs (live class, 0.70 threshold, shape-based parsing, pose.rs) and add an invariants test that docs quote the constant.

5. feat/letterbox-unification (VIS-06, VIS-05, VIS-12): make SCRFD consume the bilinear vision letterbox with integer padding; remove the sigmoid fallback (validate score range); non-finite guards in decode_stride and align_face_112.

6. feat/inference-latency (VIS-10, VIS-13): borrow ORT outputs instead of copying, reusable scratch tensors, configurable `intra_threads`, honest zeroization docs (and in-place output wipe if ort exposes it); benchmark on real models to close the loop on the 150 ms budget.

7. test/model-contract-suite (VIS-14, VIS-15): real-model gated tests, embedding dimension check, NHWC fallback fail-closed, installer script hardening.

</details>

#### VIS-01 — Daemon and enrollment CLI hard-code MiniFASNet live_class_index = 2, contradicting the tested default (1) and the ASG1 anti-spoof fix

GitHub issue: #146

**CRITICAL** · CONFIRMED · security · effort S

- **Locations**: `crates/daemon/src/pipeline.rs:189-193`, `crates/enrollment-cli/src/service.rs:773`, `crates/inference-ort/src/pad.rs:80`, `crates/gui/src/main.rs:92`
- **Evidence**: crates/daemon/src/pipeline.rs:189-193: `OrtPadDetector::new_with_class_index(pad_session, config.vision.pad_threshold, 2)`. crates/enrollment-cli/src/service.rs:773: `OrtPadDetector::new_with_class_index(pad_session, 0.80, 2)`. crates/inference-ort/src/pad.rs:80: `pub const DEFAULT_MINIFASNET_LIVE_CLASS_INDEX: usize = 1;` (doc comment: Class 0 PrintPhoto, Class 1 Live, Class 2 ScreenReplay). GUI uses the default (gui/main.rs:92 `OrtPadDetector::new(pad_session, 0.80)` -> index 1). `git show 084ce5c -- crates/daemon/src/pipeline.rs` shows `-        1,` / `+        2,` (walkthrough 66, when the default was 2). Walkthrough 72 (`AI/walkthroughs/72_anti_spoof_ir_stability_gdm.md:20-37`) states: 'previously set DEFAULT_MINIFASNET_LIVE_CLASS_INDEX = 2 ... model output high probability for Class 2 (Screen Replay) ... enabling attacks' and reset the constant to 1; walkthrough 75 fixed only gui/main.rs. `grep -rn "class_index\|live_class" crates/daemon/tests crates/daemon/src crates/enrollment-cli/tests` -> only the two hard-coded call sites, no test. project-facts.md §2/§4 and VERIFICATION_MATRIX ASG1 both say live class = 1. Walkthroughs 66 and 72 contradict each other on the empirical ordering; the daemon currently follows 66 while the crate default, tests, GUI and matrix follow 72.
- **Impact**: The PAM authentication path (daemon) and the enrollment path use a different liveness class than the GUI and the tested constant. If class 2 is ScreenReplay (per walkthrough 72, matrix ASG1 and the upstream Silent-Face convention label 1 = real), the daemon treats the replay probability as liveness: screen replays pass PAD while genuine faces are rejected. Whichever ordering is actually right, daemon and GUI cannot both be correct, and no automated test binds the production wiring to the attested constant.
- **Recommendation**: Remove both hard-coded `2` literals: build the PAD detector with `OrtPadDetector::new(session, threshold)` (crate default) or from a single `VisionPipelineConfig.live_class_index` field defaulting to `DEFAULT_MINIFASNET_LIVE_CLASS_INDEX`; add `OrtPadDetector::live_class_index()` assertions in a daemon unit test on the pipeline builder and in enrollment-cli (e.g. `test_daemon_pad_uses_default_live_class_index`). Resolve the 66-vs-72 contradiction with a fixture test gated on the real ONNX file (`#[ignore]` unless `/var/lib/soos/models/minifasnet_v2_80x80.onnx` exists) that runs a known live crop and asserts argmax == DEFAULT_MINIFASNET_LIVE_CLASS_INDEX, and record the verified ordering in AI/DECISIONS.md. (Overlaps with the PAD-policy reviewer; reported here because it is inference wiring.)
- **Verifier notes**: Re-opened crates/daemon/src/pipeline.rs:189-193 (`OrtPadDetector::new_with_class_index(pad_session, config.vision.pad_threshold, 2)`), crates/enrollment-cli/src/service.rs:773 (`new_with_class_index(pad_session, 0.80, 2)`), crates/gui/src/main.rs:92 (`OrtPadDetector::new(pad_session, 0.80)` -> default), crates/inference-ort/src/pad.rs:80 (`DEFAULT_MINIFASNET_LIVE_CLASS_INDEX: usize = 1`, doc: 0=PrintPhoto, 1=Live, 2=ScreenReplay). `git blame -L 189,193` shows the literal `2` comes from 084ce5c6 (2026-09-26 02:01, walkthrough 66); the walkthrough-72 commit 72469ba (22:15 same day, `git show --stat`) touched crates/daemon/src/pipeline.rs by 4 lines but its diff contains no class-index change (grep of the diff for `class`/`  1,`/`  2,` only hits pad.rs doc comment), so the daemon was never realigned. `grep -rn class_index crates/daemon crates/enrollment-cli crates/gui` -> only the two hard-coded call sites; `crates/daemon/tests/pipeline_init_tests.rs` uses MockPadDetector, no wiring assertion. AI/walkthroughs/66 (lines 15-28: index 2 = live, measured [0.0003,0.0054,0.9943]) and 72 (lines 14-21: index 1 = live) contradict each other; project-facts.md:52,76 and matrix ASG1 (line 393) say 1. I could not empirically settle the ordering: the three ONNX files are now present in /var/lib/soos/models (sha256 match), `protoc --decode_raw` confirms MiniFASNet I/O `input [batch,3,80,80]` / `output [batch,3]`, but no onnxruntime Python module, no face fixture (`find` for jpg/png in repo -> none) and no `#[ignore]` real-model tests exist. The contradiction between the authentication path (index 2) and the crate default/GUI/tests/matrix (index 1) is unambiguous and untested, and the PAM path is the one that trusts the unattested literal, so CRITICAL stands. Additional detail: matrix ASG1 cites `pad_tests::test_minifasnet_live_class_index_is_1` / `test_minifasnet_rejects_screen_replay_spoof`, which do not exist (`grep -rn` -> no hits; actual names are `test_pad_default_live_class_index_is_one` and `test_screen_replay_detected_as_spoof_with_default_index`). Note walkthrough 66's measurement predates the crop aspect-ratio fix of walkthrough 72 (crates/vision/src/crop.rs), so the two observations are not comparable; the fix must include an on-device measurement, not a choice by document precedence.

#### VIS-02 — MJPEG decode is unbounded and never checks decoded dimensions against the frame header

GitHub issue: #190

**MAJOR** · CONFIRMED · reliability · effort S

- **Locations**: `crates/vision/src/color.rs:155-220`
- **Evidence**: color.rs:156-159: `let mut decoder = jpeg_decoder::Decoder::new(raw_buffer); let decoded_bytes = decoder.decode()...` with no `set_max_decoding_buffer_size` and no `read_info` pre-check. jpeg-decoder 0.3.2 `src/decoder.rs:152`: `decoding_buffer_size_limit: usize::MAX` by default. color.rs:165-174 (RGB24) and 175-181 (L8) only compare `decoded_bytes.len()` with the expected length; `info.width`/`info.height` are never compared to `width`/`height`, so a 320x480 JPEG is accepted for a 480x320 frame (same byte count). color.rs:190-218: the CMYK32 and L16 branches do not validate length at all and return a buffer of whatever size the JPEG had.
- **Impact**: A malformed or hostile MJPEG frame (BadUSB / faulty camera firmware) whose SOF declares e.g. 65535x65535 makes the root daemon try to allocate ~12.9 GB and abort on OOM (Rust aborts on allocation failure) -> authentication daemon crash (PAM falls back to PAM_IGNORE, so fail-safe, but a DoS of biometric auth). Transposed or CMYK/L16 frames produce a wrong-size RGB buffer that fails later with a misleading `InvalidBufferSize` from the detector instead of at the source.
- **Recommendation**: In the Mjpeg branch: `decoder.read_info()?`, take `info()`, reject when `info.width as u32 != width || info.height as u32 != height` (`InvalidDimensions`), call `decoder.set_max_decoding_buffer_size(expected_rgb_len)` before `decode()`, and either drop CMYK32/L16 (return `ColorConversionFailed`) or validate their lengths. Tests: `test_mjpeg_dimension_mismatch_fails_closed` (encode a tiny JPEG fixture with the `jpeg-encoder`/hand-made bytes, request swapped dims), `test_mjpeg_oversized_header_rejected` (SOF with 65535x65535 must return Err quickly without allocating).
- **Verifier notes**: crates/vision/src/color.rs:156-159: `Decoder::new(raw_buffer); decoder.decode()` with no `read_info`/`set_max_decoding_buffer_size`. Cargo.lock:1630 pins jpeg-decoder 0.3.2; ~/.cargo/registry/src/*/jpeg-decoder-0.3.2/src/decoder.rs:152 `decoding_buffer_size_limit: usize::MAX`, :163 `set_max_decoding_buffer_size` and :265 `read_info` exist, :636 is the only guard and never fires at the default. color.rs:165-181 compare only byte lengths (`decoded_bytes.len() != expected_rgb_len`), `info.width`/`info.height` unused, so transposed dims pass; color.rs:190-218 CMYK32/L16 return buffers of arbitrary length with no check. Reachability: crates/camera-v4l/src/v4l_impl.rs:191-197 `FORMAT_PRIORITY` puts MJPEG 4th (after RGB24, YUYV, NV12), so the path is only hit on MJPEG-only cameras/resolutions, but the daemon does negotiate it (dispatcher.rs:368 maps Mjpeg). Allocation size is controlled entirely by the SOF header of a device-supplied frame; MAJOR (unbounded allocation in the root daemon) holds. Recommendation is valid: both jpeg-decoder APIs exist in 0.3.2.

#### VIS-03 — Attested ArcFace model is a 136 MB tf2onnx ResNet34 NHWC network, not the 'MobileFaceNet w600k ~3.6 MB NCHW RGB' described by manifest, README, ADRs and latency budget; manifest shapes are never validated against the session

GitHub issue: #191

**MAJOR** · CONFIRMED · doc-drift · effort M

- **Locations**: `models/manifest.toml:28-38`, `models/README.md:16`, `models/README.md:29`, `models/README.md:89`, `AI/DECISIONS.md:16`, `AI/ARCHITECTURE.md:201`, `crates/inference-ort/src/registry.rs:107-130`, `crates/inference-ort/src/manifest.rs:28-30`, `crates/inference-ort/src/embedding.rs:147-161`
- **Evidence**: `sha256sum /var/lib/soos/models/arcface_w600k_mbf.onnx` = ffe014a4...683db == manifest.toml:31 (attested file). `ls -la` size 136,619,444 bytes. `strings` shows `tf2onnx`, `input_1`, `StatefulPartitionedCall/ResNet34/conv1_conv/Conv2D` (Keras/deepface ResNet34 ArcFace). manifest.toml:35 `input_shape = [1, 3, 112, 112]`, README.md:16 '`[1, 3, 112, 112]` RGB', README.md:29 'Color Format: RGB channel ordering', DECISIONS.md:16 'ArcFace MobileFaceNet w600k ... ~3.6MB'. Code truth: embedding.rs:150 detects NHWC (`shape.last() == Some(3)`), embedding.rs:204-214 writes B,G,R (BGR); project-facts §4 says NHWC/BGR. `grep -rn input_shape crates --include=*.rs` outside tests -> only the struct field in manifest.rs:28; registry.rs:107-130 only checksums and never compares session inputs/outputs to `input_shape`/`output_shapes`. registry.rs:26-27 `intra_threads: 1` and no override in crates/daemon/src/config.rs (`grep -n thread` -> none). ARCHITECTURE.md:201 budgets 30 ms for alignment + embedding.
- **Impact**: Every downstream document (manifest description, README preprocessing rules, ADR, Docs) describes a different network than the one attested and shipped; calibrators and auditors are misled about layout (NCHW vs NHWC), colour order and cost. The manifest's shape fields are decorative: a model with the right hash but unexpected I/O shapes is only caught at first inference (or silently mis-fed in the NCHW fallback path). A ResNet34 at 112x112 on one ORT thread is far heavier than a MobileFaceNet; the 30 ms budget row and 'estimated ~80ms on hardware' (Docs/VISION_CRATE.md §3) are unsubstantiated for this model.
- **Recommendation**: Decide: (a) keep the shipped model and correct manifest.toml (`input_shape = [1,112,112,3]`, description 'ArcFace ResNet34 (tf2onnx, deepface) NHWC BGR'), README, DECISIONS and Docs, or (b) switch to the real InsightFace buffalo_sc `w600k_mbf.onnx` (NCHW, ~13 MB) and re-attest. In both cases make `ModelRegistry::get_or_load_session` compare `session.inputs()[0]` dims and each output dims with the manifest entry and fail closed on mismatch (`InferenceError::TensorError`), with a unit test using a tiny hand-written ONNX or the mock manifest. Add a real-model benchmark test (`#[ignore]` unless the model exists) recording p50/p95 for the embedding step and update ARCHITECTURE §7 with measured numbers.
- **Verifier notes**: `sha256sum /var/lib/soos/models/arcface_w600k_mbf.onnx` = ffe014a4...683db == models/manifest.toml:31; size 136,619,444 bytes (`ls -la`). `protoc --decode_raw < arcface_w600k_mbf.onnx | tail` shows producer string `converted from arcface_saved_model`, `strings` shows `tf2onnx` and `StatefulPartitionedCall/ResNet34/conv1_conv/Conv2D`, graph input `input_1` dims [unk__556, 112, 112, 3] (NHWC) and output `embedding` [unk__557, 512]. manifest.toml:34-35 says 'ArcFace MobileFaceNet w600k' with `input_shape = [1, 3, 112, 112]`; models/README.md:16 `[1, 3, 112, 112]` RGB, :29 'RGB channel ordering', :89 'ArcFace MobileFaceNet w600k'; AI/DECISIONS.md line '[2026-09-20] Next-Generation AI Models ... ArcFace MobileFaceNet w600k (512D, ~3.6MB)'; AI/ARCHITECTURE.md §7 item 3 '~3.6 MB' and budget row 30 ms. Code: embedding.rs:147-158 detects NHWC via `shape.last() == Some(3)`, :204-214 writes B,G,R. registry.rs:107-130 only checksums (`verify_model_checksum`) and never reads `input_shape`/`output_shapes`; manifest.rs:28-30 is the only use. Severity MAJOR justified as doc/attestation drift on a security-relevant contract plus decorative manifest shape fields. Caveat for the recommendation: the shipped models declare symbolic batch dims (`unk__556`, `batch_size`), so any session-vs-manifest dim check must treat dynamic dims as wildcards or it will fail closed on the attested models.

#### VIS-04 — Checksum-then-load TOCTOU: model is hashed by path, then re-opened by path for the ORT session; models are hashed twice at startup

GitHub issue: #246

**MINOR** · CONFIRMED · security · effort S

- **Locations**: `crates/inference-ort/src/manifest.rs:103-107`, `crates/inference-ort/src/registry.rs:112-124`, `crates/daemon/src/pipeline.rs:178-182`
- **Evidence**: manifest.rs:103 `Self::compute_sha256(p)` opens and hashes the file; registry.rs:123 `.commit_from_file(&path)` re-opens the same path afterwards. daemon pipeline.rs:178 `registry.verify_integrity()?` hashes all models, then lines 180-182 `get_or_load_session` hashes each again (registry.rs:115). ort 2.0.0-rc.13 `src/session/builder/impl_commit.rs:93` provides `commit_from_memory(&[u8])`.
- **Impact**: The bytes ORT loads are not provably the bytes that were hashed. Exploitation needs write access to /var/lib/soos/models (root:root 0755), so this is defence-in-depth rather than a live bypass, but the 'attestation before session creation' invariant in Docs/INFERENCE_ORT_CRATE.md is stronger than what the code guarantees. Double hashing costs a few tens of ms at startup for the 136 MB model.
- **Recommendation**: Read the model into a `Vec<u8>` once, hash those bytes, then `commit_from_memory(&bytes)`; drop the second hash by caching verified digests from `verify_integrity`. Test: load a model via the registry, overwrite the file on disk with garbage, and assert the already-created session still runs (proves the in-memory path) plus `test_registry_rejects_tampered_bytes` for the hash-before-load ordering.
- **Verifier notes**: manifest.rs:103 `Self::compute_sha256(p)` hashes by path; registry.rs:115 `verify_model_checksum(id, &path)` then :123 `.commit_from_file(&path)` re-opens the path. daemon pipeline.rs:178 `registry.verify_integrity()` (manifest.verify_directory hashes all) then :180-182 `get_or_load_session` hashes each again. ort-2.0.0-rc.13/src/session/builder/impl_commit.rs:93 `pub fn commit_from_memory(&mut self, model_bytes: &[u8])` exists. Docs/INFERENCE_ORT_CRATE.md:8 and :21 state verification 'before any execution session is instantiated' — literally true (hash precedes session), so the gap is the identity of bytes, i.e. defence-in-depth; models dir is root:root 0755 (`ls -la /var/lib/soos/models`). MINOR is right. Note: `commit_from_memory` on the 136 MB ResNet34 keeps a second copy resident during load; acceptable but should be measured.

#### VIS-05 — SCRFD score activation heuristic is non-monotonic (per-element sigmoid fallback)

GitHub issue: #247

**MINOR** · CONFIRMED · bug · effort S

- **Locations**: `crates/inference-ort/src/detector.rs:597-602`
- **Evidence**: detector.rs:597-601: `let conf = if (0.0..=1.0).contains(&raw_score) { raw_score } else { 1.0 / (1.0 + (-raw_score).exp()) };`. Mapping: 1.0 -> 1.0 but 1.01 -> 0.733; 0.0 -> 0.0 but -0.01 -> 0.4975. project-facts §4 and walkthrough 65 state the attested model already emits probabilities, so the `else` branch is dead for the shipped model; test `scrfd_tests::test_scrfd_supports_preactivated_probabilities` (line 401) only covers the pass-through case.
- **Impact**: If a logit-output SCRFD variant is ever attested, scores are silently misranked (a logit of 0.9 outranks a logit of 3.0), corrupting NMS ordering and the single-face invariant. For the current model it is dead code that obscures the contract.
- **Recommendation**: Remove the fallback and treat the output as probabilities (fail closed with `TensorError` if any score is outside [0,1] or non-finite), or make activation an explicit constructor parameter validated once at startup. Keep the existing pass-through test and add `test_scrfd_rejects_out_of_range_scores`.
- **Verifier notes**: detector.rs:597-601 exactly as quoted. Arithmetic re-checked: sigmoid(1.01)=0.733 < 1.0, sigmoid(-0.01)=0.4975 > 0.0, so the mapping is non-monotonic across the boundary. Model evidence: `protoc --decode_raw < scrfd_500m_kps.onnx | grep -c '4: "Sigmoid"'` = 3, i.e. the attested model applies Sigmoid on its three score heads and emits probabilities, so the `else` branch is dead for the shipped model. scrfd_tests.rs:401 `test_scrfd_supports_preactivated_probabilities` covers only in-range scores. MINOR stands (latent, not reachable with the attested model).

#### VIS-06 — Two letterbox implementations: the tested bilinear one in soos-vision is unused in production, the production one is nearest-neighbour with inconsistent pad rounding

GitHub issue: #248

**MINOR** · CONFIRMED · dead-code · effort M

- **Locations**: `crates/vision/src/letterbox.rs:104-203`, `crates/inference-ort/src/detector.rs:386-449`, `crates/vision/src/letterbox.rs:141-142`, `crates/inference-ort/src/detector.rs:405-406`
- **Evidence**: `grep -rn "letterbox_resize\|letterbox_params" crates tests --include=*.rs` outside tests -> only the re-export in crates/vision/src/lib.rs:25; the only callers are crates/vision/tests/letterbox_tests.rs (cited as NGM14 evidence). Production detection uses detector.rs:691 `letterbox_pad(rgb, width, height, 640)`, which samples with `floor` (lines 413, 420: `(y as f32 / scale).floor()`) i.e. nearest neighbour. Pad rounding: letterbox.rs:141 `params.pad_x.round() as u32` vs detector.rs:405 `pad_x as usize` (truncation), while both unproject with the fractional `pad_x` (detector.rs:377-378). Example 640x479 input: pad_y = 80.5 -> placed at row 81 (vision) or 80 (detector) but unprojected with 80.5.
- **Impact**: Duplicate, divergent code paths; the acceptance evidence for NGM14 validates a function the daemon never runs. Nearest-neighbour downscaling of 1280x720/1920x1080 frames to 640 aliases small faces and lowers SCRFD recall versus the reference `cv2.resize(INTER_LINEAR)` preprocessing; half-pixel pad mismatch introduces a systematic 0.5/scale px landmark offset on odd padding.
- **Recommendation**: Keep one implementation (vision's bilinear resize) and let `OrtScrfdDetector` pack the tensor from the letterboxed RGB buffer; compute the integer pad first and derive the unprojection offsets from the integer values. Tests: parity test between the two paths on a gradient image before deletion, `test_letterbox_odd_padding_unproject_exact` asserting integer-pad round-trip within 1e-4.
- **Verifier notes**: `grep -rn letterbox_resize\|letterbox_params crates tests --include=*.rs` excluding tests -> only the re-export at crates/vision/src/lib.rs:25; production uses detector.rs:691 `letterbox_pad(rgb, width, height, self.input_size.0)`. detector.rs:413/420 sample with `(y as f32 / scale).floor()` (nearest neighbour); vision letterbox.rs:155-198 is bilinear. Pad rounding: letterbox.rs:141 `params.pad_x.round() as u32` vs detector.rs:405 `pad_x as usize`, while both unproject with the fractional value (detector.rs:377-378, letterbox.rs:47-48). Worked example 640x479: scale 1.0, pad_y = 80.5 -> vision places at row 81 (Rust rounds half away from zero), detector at row 80, both unproject with 80.5: confirmed 0.5 px systematic offset in the detector path. Matrix NGM14 (line 337) cites only letterbox_tests, i.e. the unused function. MINOR is appropriate; the recall claim vs. cv2 INTER_LINEAR is plausible but not measured here.

VERIFICATION_MATRIX NGM17 asserts all legacy references were eliminated.
