Update lib.rs docstring and Docs/INFERENCE_ORT_CRATE.md:61.
- **Verifier notes**: `grep -rn 'OrtFaceDetector\|LandmarkDetector\|validate_output_shapes\|prepare_input' crates --include=*.rs | grep -v /tests/` -> only definitions (detector.rs:178/185/231, landmarks.rs:103, mock.rs:142-183, detector.rs:473) and lib.rs re-exports (lib.rs:22,27,29); no binary uses them. detector.rs:512-517 `OrtScrfdDetector::new` calls only `validate_output_count`, while Docs/INFERENCE_ORT_CRATE.md line 'Startup validation verifying session output count (9) and shape patterns' overstates it. `grep -rn ndarray crates/inference-ort/src crates/inference-ort/tests` -> none, yet crates/inference-ort/Cargo.toml:15 `ndarray = { workspace = true }`. lib.rs:5 still says 'UltraFace Slim 320'. scripts/download_models.sh:176-187 still resolves ultraface_slim_320/landmark_5point/mobilefacenet_arcface/minifasnet_pad; models/README.md §5 documents them as legacy. Docs/INFERENCE_ORT_CRATE.md line 61 explicitly says OrtFaceDetector is 'retained for reference', so the docs are internally consistent with the code but contradict matrix NGM17 (line 362). Caveat for the recommendation: matrix NGM8 (line 317) cites `zeroize_tests::test_inference_input_buffers_zeroized`, whose first block (zeroize_tests.rs:32-37) uses `OrtFaceDetector::prepare_input`; that test is acceptance evidence and must be re-pointed to the SCRFD input path, not deleted (AGENTS.md zero-test-weakening rule).

#### VIS-09 — Detector/PAD thresholds are duplicated as literals per binary and diverge (iou 0.45 vs 0.40, conf 0.70 vs 0.60, PAD 0.85 vs 0.80)

GitHub issue: #251

**MINOR** · CONFIRMED · design · effort S

- **Locations**: `crates/daemon/src/pipeline.rs:184-193`, `crates/gui/src/main.rs:91-92`, `crates/enrollment-cli/src/service.rs:772-773`, `crates/vision/src/pipeline.rs:39-52`, `crates/vision/src/pipeline.rs:336`
- **Evidence**: Daemon: `OrtScrfdDetector::new(det_session, config.vision.min_face_confidence, 0.45)`, PAD threshold = `config.vision.pad_threshold` (0.85). GUI: `OrtScrfdDetector::new(det_session, 0.60, 0.40)`, `OrtPadDetector::new(pad_session, 0.80)`. Enrollment: `OrtScrfdDetector::new(det_session, 0.70, 0.40)`, PAD 0.80. `VisionPipelineConfig::default()` = min_face_confidence 0.70, pad_threshold 0.85 and pipeline.rs:336 re-checks `!pad_result.is_live || pad_result.score < self.config.pad_threshold`, so GUI/enrollment run two different PAD thresholds for one decision (`is_live` at 0.80, then 0.85). VERIFICATION_MATRIX BIO1 claims thresholds are calibrated consistently across VisionPipelineConfig and GUI/CLIs.
- **Impact**: Enrollment templates are captured under looser detection/NMS settings than authentication uses; the GUI preview can show 'live' where the daemon rejects, confusing users and calibration. NMS iou differences change which duplicate boxes survive and therefore the single-face rejection behaviour between tools.
- **Recommendation**: Add `nms_iou_threshold` (and the PAD live class) to `VisionPipelineConfig`, provide one constructor (e.g. `VisionPipeline::from_registry(&mut registry, &config)`) used by daemon, GUI and enrollment, and have `OrtPadDetector` take its threshold from the same config; test `test_all_binaries_share_pipeline_thresholds` comparing the built detectors' public fields.
- **Verifier notes**: daemon pipeline.rs:184-193: conf = `config.vision.min_face_confidence`, iou 0.45, PAD threshold = `config.vision.pad_threshold`; gui/main.rs:91-92: `OrtScrfdDetector::new(det_session, 0.60, 0.40)`, `OrtPadDetector::new(pad_session, 0.80)` with `VisionPipelineConfig::default()` (line 98); enrollment service.rs:772-773: `(det_session, 0.70, 0.40)`, PAD 0.80, default config. vision/pipeline.rs:39-52 defaults 0.70/0.70/0.85 and :336 `!pad_result.is_live || pad_result.score < self.config.pad_threshold`, so GUI/enrollment apply is_live@0.80 then score@0.85. daemon config.rs:67 `vision: soos_vision::VisionPipelineConfig` has no nms/class fields (`grep -n thread\|nms` -> none). Matrix BIO1 (line 381) claims consistency 'across VisionPipelineConfig and GUI/CLIs' but its test `test_vision_pipeline_default_thresholds_calibrated` (pipeline_tests.rs:466-479) only asserts the struct defaults, not the binaries' literals. Severity MINOR is right; the live-class part is already VIS-01.

#### VIS-10 — Per-frame 4.9 MB tensor allocation, full copy of all 9 SCRFD outputs, and single-thread ORT with no config knob

GitHub issue: #252

**MINOR** · CONFIRMED · performance · effort M

- **Locations**: `crates/inference-ort/src/detector.rs:393`, `crates/inference-ort/src/detector.rs:713-720`, `crates/vision/src/color.rs:47`, `crates/inference-ort/src/registry.rs:26-27`, `crates/inference-ort/src/registry.rs:119-121`
- **Evidence**: detector.rs:393 `vec![0.0f32; 3 * target * target]` (3*640*640*4 = 4.9 MB) on every `detect`. detector.rs:719 `extracted.push((shape_vec, slice.to_vec()))` copies 12800*15 + 3200*15 + 800*15 = 252,000 f32 (~1 MB) per frame although each slice is read exactly once by `decode_stride`. color.rs:47 `raw_buffer.to_vec()` copies RGB24 frames (921 KB at 640x480). registry.rs:26-27 hard-code `intra_threads: 1, inter_threads: 1`; `grep -n thread crates/daemon/src/config.rs` -> no override. ARCHITECTURE.md:199 budgets 40 ms for SCRFD 640x640 and 30 ms for the (ResNet34, see VIS-03) embedding.
- **Impact**: Avoidable memory churn on the multi-frame authentication loop; on low-power laptops single-threaded ORT for SCRFD-640 plus a ResNet34 embedding is likely to exceed the 150 ms decision budget (not measured here, no models available on this host).
- **Recommendation**: Decode directly from borrowed `try_extract_tensor` slices inside the `outputs` scope; keep a reusable `Zeroizing<Vec<f32>>` scratch tensor per detector (Mutex-protected alongside the session); make `intra_threads` a daemon config field (default `min(4, available_parallelism)`); accept `Cow`/borrow for RGB24 in `convert_to_rgb`. Add a model-gated timing test to produce real p95 numbers for the walkthrough.
- **Verifier notes**: detector.rs:393 `Zeroizing::new(vec![0.0f32; 3 * target * target])` per `detect` (3*640*640*4 B = 4.9 MB); detector.rs:713-720 `extracted.push((shape_vec, slice.to_vec()))` copies all 9 outputs; color.rs:47 `Ok(raw_buffer.to_vec())` for RGB24; registry.rs:26-27 and :38-39 `intra_threads: 1, inter_threads: 1` with no override in daemon config.rs. ort rc.13 `Session::run(&'s mut self, ...) -> SessionOutputs<'s>` (session/mod.rs:236) borrows the session, so decoding inside the lock scope (recommendation) is feasible. Latency claim is unmeasured (no benchmark run here) but the shipped embedding model is a ResNet34 (VIS-03), so the concern is well-founded. Caveat: raising intra_threads enables ORT spin-waiting between runs; the plan should set `allow_spinning=0` or measure idle CPU.

#### VIS-11 — YUYV conversion silently returns a short buffer when width*height is odd

GitHub issue: #253

**MINOR** · CONFIRMED · bug · effort S

- **Locations**: `crates/vision/src/color.rs:65-106`
- **Evidence**: color.rs:66-75 accept any buffer of `pixel_count * 2` bytes; color.rs:80 `for chunk in raw_buffer.chunks_exact(4)` drops the trailing 2 bytes. For width 3, height 1: expected 6 bytes -> one chunk -> 6 RGB bytes returned instead of 9, `Ok`. NV12 by contrast rejects odd dimensions (color.rs:109-111) and has a test (`test_nv12_odd_dimensions_rejected`); there is no YUYV odd-width test (color_tests.rs list). YUYV macro-pixels also straddle rows when width is odd.
- **Impact**: Fail-safe (the detector later returns `InvalidBufferSize`) but the error is raised at the wrong layer with misleading numbers, and any future direct consumer of `convert_to_rgb` output (GUI preview) would index out of range.
- **Recommendation**: Reject `width % 2 != 0` for `PixelFormat::Yuyv` with `InvalidDimensions` (mirroring NV12) and add `test_yuyv_odd_width_rejected`.
- **Verifier notes**: color.rs:66-75 only checks `raw_buffer.len() == pixel_count*2`; :80 `for chunk in raw_buffer.chunks_exact(4)` drops the remainder. Width 3, height 1: expected 6 bytes, one 4-byte chunk, 6 RGB bytes returned with Ok (9 expected). NV12 rejects odd dims at :109-111 with test `test_nv12_odd_dimensions_rejected` (color_tests.rs:207); color_tests.rs has no YUYV odd-width test (fn list: lines 75, 101 only). Fail-safe downstream (detector.rs:685 InvalidBufferSize), so MINOR is right.

#### VIS-12 — Non-finite landmarks are not rejected: NaN passes the degeneracy guards and yields an all-black aligned crop that is embedded (and, in enrollment, could be stored)

GitHub issue: #254

**MINOR** · CONFIRMED · reliability · effort S

- **Locations**: `crates/vision/src/align.rs:99-118`, `crates/vision/src/align.rs:141`, `crates/inference-ort/src/detector.rs:643-646`, `crates/vision/src/pipeline.rs:345-354`
- **Evidence**: align.rs:99 `if sum_xx_yy <= 1e-6` and align.rs:114 `if det <= 1e-12` are both false for NaN, so no `AlignmentFailed`; align.rs:141 range checks are false for NaN so every pixel takes the black branch and a valid-looking 112x112 buffer is returned. detector.rs:644-645 `orig_lm_x.clamp(0.0, orig_w as f32)` propagates NaN (f32::clamp returns NaN for NaN). pipeline.rs:345-354 then embeds the black crop without any sanity check; enrollment (service.rs:306) uses `process_frame` output for templates.
- **Impact**: A corrupted/degenerate detector output produces a deterministic 'black image' embedding instead of an error; if enrolled, that template would match any other black-crop failure. Fail-closed today only by accident (PAD and matching usually fail), not by design.
- **Recommendation**: In `align_face_112` return `AlignmentFailed` when any landmark coordinate is non-finite; in `decode_stride` skip candidates with non-finite score/box/kps; optionally reject aligned crops whose mean intensity is 0. Tests: `test_align_rejects_nan_landmarks`, `test_decode_stride_skips_non_finite`.
- **Verifier notes**: align.rs:99 `if sum_xx_yy <= 1e-6` and :114 `if det <= 1e-12` are false for NaN; :141 `xs >= 0.0 && xs <= max_x && ys >= 0.0 && ys <= max_y` is false for NaN so every pixel takes the black branch and a 112x112x3 buffer is still returned. detector.rs:644-645 `orig_lm_x.clamp(0.0, orig_w as f32)` (f32::clamp propagates NaN); decode_stride 602-636 has no finiteness check (`grep -rn is_finite\|is_nan crates/vision/src crates/inference-ort/src` -> only pad.rs:205 softmax). pipeline.rs:311 only checks landmarks presence, :345 aligns, :350 embeds without a crop sanity check. Requires a corrupted detector output and PAD to pass, so MINOR (fail-closed by accident) is the right severity.

#### VIS-13 — Zeroization covers only the input tensors; ORT-owned output buffers holding the raw embedding are dropped unwiped, and the docs overstate the guarantee

GitHub issue: #255

**MINOR** · PARTIAL · security · effort S

- **Locations**: `crates/inference-ort/src/embedding.rs:286-301`, `crates/inference-ort/src/detector.rs:709-720`, `crates/inference-ort/src/pad.rs:299-320`, `Docs/INFERENCE_ORT_CRATE.md:12`
- **Evidence**: embedding.rs:293-298: `emb_data = emb_tensor.try_extract_tensor::<f32>()...; BiometricEmbedding::new(emb_data.to_vec())` copies the vector into a `Zeroizing` container while the ORT `SessionOutputs` allocation is dropped normally. The explicit `input_data.zeroize()` calls (detector.rs:710, embedding.rs:284, pad.rs:301) are redundant with the `Zeroizing` wrapper's Drop (harmless). Docs/INFERENCE_ORT_CRATE.md:12 and matrix NGM8 describe 'deterministic post-inference buffer zeroization' without this caveat. I did not verify whether ort rc.13 exposes a mutable output extraction that would allow in-place wiping.
- **Impact**: The unnormalised 512-D embedding and SCRFD landmark tensors may linger in ORT arena memory (and in ORT's internal activation buffers) after the request; not a leak over IPC or logs, but the documented invariant is stronger than the implementation.
- **Recommendation**: Document the boundary honestly in Docs/INFERENCE_ORT_CRATE.md (inputs zeroized, ORT-owned outputs/activations not), remove the redundant explicit `zeroize()` calls, and investigate `ort` mutable tensor extraction to zero the output slice before dropping `SessionOutputs`; if available add `test_embedding_output_buffer_zeroized` using a mock session is not feasible, so document as accepted limitation with an ADR.
- **Verifier notes**: embedding.rs:293-298 copies the ORT output into `BiometricEmbedding::new(emb_data.to_vec())` and drops `SessionOutputs` unwiped; explicit `input_data.zeroize()` at detector.rs:710, embedding.rs:284, pad.rs:306 duplicate the `Zeroizing` Drop — confirmed. Docs/INFERENCE_ORT_CRATE.md:12 and :116 claim 'deterministic post-inference zeroization of input tensors' (line 116 explicitly says *input*), so the docs are narrower than the finding implies; only line 12's wording is ambiguous. The reviewer's open question is resolved: ort-2.0.0-rc.13/src/value/impl_tensor/extract.rs:185 provides `try_extract_tensor_mut<T>(&mut self) -> Result<(&Shape, &mut [T])>`, so wiping the ORT-owned output slice before dropping `SessionOutputs` is possible today (ORT internal activation buffers remain out of reach). Correction: implement the in-place wipe rather than documenting it as an accepted limitation.

#### VIS-14 — Missing tests around model I/O contracts and production wiring

GitHub issue: #268

**SUGGESTION** · CONFIRMED · test-gap · effort M

- **Locations**: `crates/inference-ort/src/embedding.rs:298`, `crates/inference-ort/src/embedding.rs:147-161`, `crates/inference-ort/tests/embedding_tests.rs`, `crates/vision/tests/color_tests.rs`, `crates/vision/tests/letterbox_tests.rs`
- **Evidence**: embedding.rs:298 accepts an output of any length (`BiometricEmbedding::new(emb_data.to_vec())`), mismatch is only caught later by `matcher::cosine_similarity`; embedding.rs:147-161 silently falls back to NCHW when the session lock fails or has no inputs; no test covers either. color_tests.rs has no MJPEG dimension/size-limit test (VIS-02) and no YUYV odd-width test (VIS-11). letterbox_tests.rs validates a function with no production caller (VIS-06). No daemon/enrollment test binds PAD class index, NMS iou or thresholds to the shared constants (VIS-01, VIS-09). No `#[ignore]`d real-model tests exist to validate SCRFD/ArcFace/MiniFASNet I/O shapes and class ordering when models are present on the host (walkthroughs 65/66/68/71/72 each record a shipped bug that mocks could not catch).
- **Impact**: The four historical model-contract regressions listed in project-facts §4 remain undetectable by CI; wiring drift like VIS-01 recurs silently.
- **Recommendation**: Add: `test_extract_embedding_rejects_wrong_dimension` (expect 512), `test_nhwc_detection_fails_closed_without_inputs`, MJPEG/YUYV negative tests, daemon `build_pipeline` wiring assertions, and a `real_models` test module gated on `SOOS_MODELS_DIR` that asserts input/output shapes against manifest.toml and the MiniFASNet live class on a fixture crop; wire it into `run_tests.sh` on hosts with models.
- **Verifier notes**: embedding.rs:298 accepts any output length; `normalize()` (embedding.rs:64-70) only rejects zero norm, no 512 check. embedding.rs:147-158 silently defaults `is_nhwc=false` when the lock fails or inputs are empty. embedding_tests.rs fn list (lines 21-264) has no dimension or fallback test; color_tests.rs has no MJPEG dimension/limit or YUYV odd-width test; letterbox_tests.rs targets the unused function (VIS-06). `grep -rln '#\[ignore\]' crates/inference-ort/tests crates/vision/tests` -> none and no `SOOS_MODELS_DIR`/`/var/lib/soos/models` references in those tests, so no real-model contract tests exist despite all three models being installed on this host. Confirmed as a SUGGESTION-level test gap.

#### VIS-15 — download_models.sh: phantom TOML fallback module, no download time limit

GitHub issue: #269

**SUGGESTION** · CONFIRMED · improvement · effort S

- **Locations**: `scripts/download_models.sh:134-140`, `scripts/download_models.sh:265`
- **Evidence**: Line 140 `import tomllib_fallback` refers to a module that does not exist in the repository or PyPI, so on Python < 3.11 without `tomli` the user sees `ModuleNotFoundError: No module named 'tomllib_fallback'` instead of an actionable message. Line 265 `curl -fSL --retry 3 --connect-timeout 15` has no `--max-time`, so a stalled 136 MB transfer hangs the (soon one-command) installer indefinitely. Checksum flow itself (tmp file, verify, `mv`, `chmod 0644`) is correct.
- **Impact**: Confusing failure on older distros and a potential hang in the unattended installer the user is asking for.
- **Recommendation**: Replace the fallback with a clear `sys.exit('python3 >= 3.11 or the tomli package is required')`; add `--max-time 900` (or a per-model size-scaled limit) and print the expected file size from the manifest; keep the fail-closed checksum path unchanged.
- **Verifier notes**: scripts/download_models.sh:134-140 `import tomllib` -> `import tomli as tomllib` -> `import tomllib_fallback`; `find . -name 'tomllib_fallback*'` (excluding target) -> nothing, so on Python < 3.11 without tomli the failure is `ModuleNotFoundError: No module named 'tomllib_fallback'` (host here is Python 3.14.7 so unaffected locally). Line 265 `curl -fSL --retry 3 --connect-timeout 15 -o ...` has no `--max-time` (`grep -n max-time` -> none). Checksum flow at :272+ unchanged. SUGGESTION is appropriate.

#### TCI-01 — [profile.release] panic = "abort" makes every catch_unwind in pam_soos.so a no-op: a panic inside the .so aborts gdm/sudo/login instead of returning PAM_IGNORE

GitHub issue: #148

**CRITICAL** · CONFIRMED · security · effort M

- **Locations**: `Cargo.toml:122-128`, `crates/pam/src/lib.rs:84`, `crates/pam/src/lib.rs:167`, `crates/pam/src/lib.rs:285-305`, `Docs/SECURITY_AND_QUALITY_GUIDELINES.md:134-153`, `tests/docker/test_suite.sh:61-65`, `scripts/build_deb.sh:90`, `packaging/rpm/soos.spec:31`, `packaging/arch/PKGBUILD:19`, `AI/VERIFICATION_MATRIX.md:51`, `AI/VERIFICATION_MATRIX.md:271`
- **Evidence**: Cargo.toml:122-128 sets `[profile.release] ... panic = "abort"` and `overflow-checks = true`; no crate overrides it (Cargo cannot set `panic` per package; `grep -rn '\[profile' crates` returned nothing). crates/pam/Cargo.toml:13 builds `crate-type = ["cdylib", "rlib"]`. Every shipped artifact is a release build: tests/docker/test_suite.sh:64 `cargo build --release -p soos-pam`, scripts/build_deb.sh:90, packaging/rpm/soos.spec:31, packaging/arch/PKGBUILD:19 `cargo build --release --workspace`. With panic=abort the runtime never unwinds, so `catch_unwind(AssertUnwindSafe(...))` at crates/pam/src/lib.rs:84,167,291 cannot catch anything. Scratch repro (scratchpad/tests-ci-tooling/abort_repro.rs, `catch_unwind` around an out-of-bounds index): `rustc -C panic=unwind` → prints `caught=true`, exit 0; `rustc -C panic=abort` → exit 134 (SIGABRT). Docs/SECURITY_AND_QUALITY_GUIDELINES.md:150 claims overflow-checks cause "runtime panics (caught by catch_unwind)" and :153 says abort only applies "if any unexpected panic escapes catch_unwind" — both false under panic=abort. VERIFICATION_MATRIX PA3/PFT1 are marked Verified only by unit/integration tests, which run under `[profile.test]` (panic=unwind), so the evidence never exercises the release artifact. The panic hook (crates/pam/src/syslog.rs:21-23) still logs before abort, but the host process dies.
- **Impact**: Any panic path in the PAM module (an arithmetic overflow — now trapping in release because overflow-checks=true — an index out of bounds, a slice/CString failure, an ORT-unrelated bug) kills the privileged host process: gdm-password, swaylock/hyprlock, sudo, login. That is a denial-of-service/lockout of the machine's authentication path and directly violates the AGENTS.md invariant "Panic Safety: Mandatory catch_unwind ... systematically returning PAM_IGNORE" and ARCHITECTURE.md invariant 5 ("any ... panic ... degrades silently to password fallback"). The whole documented panic-safety story (PA3, PFT1, PA12 syslog-and-continue) is only true in test builds.
- **Recommendation**: Switch the release profile to `panic = "unwind"` (or at minimum build the PAM cdylib with unwind: e.g. a dedicated `[profile.pam-release]` inherited from release with `panic = "unwind"` used by every packaging script/spec/PKGBUILD/test_suite.sh, since `panic` cannot be overridden per package). Keep `-C panic=abort` only for binaries that do not run inside foreign hosts (daemon), if desired. Correct Docs/SECURITY_AND_QUALITY_GUIDELINES.md §2 rationale. Add an invariant test in tests/invariants that parses root Cargo.toml and fails if the profile used to build `soos-pam` has `panic = "abort"`, and add a Docker matrix case (mock_daemon mode that makes the module panic, or a `panic_test=1` argument compiled only under a test feature) proving that the *release* .so returns PAM_IGNORE (25) rather than aborting the pam_test_runner process (exit 134). Record the decision as an ADR in AI/DECISIONS.md.
- **Verifier notes**: Re-read Cargo.toml:122-128: `[profile.release] ... panic = "abort"`, `overflow-checks = true`. `grep -rn '\[profile' crates tests` -> only tests/invariants/src/lib.rs:261-267, which asserts `[profile.release]` and `overflow-checks = true` exist but says nothing about `panic` (so fixing it does not break an invariant). crates/pam/Cargo.toml:13 `crate-type = ["cdylib", "rlib"]`; packaging builds are all `cargo build --release` (scripts/build_deb.sh:90, packaging/rpm/soos.spec:31, packaging/arch/PKGBUILD:19, tests/docker/test_suite.sh:64). catch_unwind at crates/pam/src/lib.rs:84,167,291 confirmed. Own scratch repro (scratchpad/quality-verify/abort.rs, catch_unwind around an OOB index): `rustc -C panic=unwind` -> `caught=true`, exit 0; `rustc -C panic=abort` -> exit 134 (SIGABRT). Location correction: the misleading rationale is at Docs/SECURITY_AND_QUALITY_GUIDELINES.md:48 (profile listing), :60 ("runtime panics (caught by catch_unwind)") and :63 ("if any unexpected panic escapes catch_unwind"), not :134-153 (that range is the CI overview box). Additional context the reviewer missed: AI/walkthroughs/11_security_guardrails_and_code_quality_harness.md:19 records `panic = "abort"` as a deliberate defense-in-depth choice, so the fix must supersede it with an ADR. Severity CRITICAL stands: with overflow-checks=true in release, any arithmetic overflow in the .so aborts gdm/sudo/login instead of PAM_IGNORE.

#### TCI-02 — Packaged PAM configurations and the invariant test still pin timeout_ms=250 while the calibrated PAM default is 1000 ms and the daemon decision budget is 900 ms (+800 ms camera wake)

GitHub issue: #185

**MAJOR** · CONFIRMED · doc-drift · effort M

- **Locations**: `tests/invariants/src/lib.rs:603`, `tests/invariants/src/lib.rs:623`, `tests/invariants/src/lib.rs:654`, `tests/invariants/src/lib.rs:670-674`, `packaging/pam/debian/soos:7-9`, `packaging/pam/fedora/soos/system-auth:12`, `packaging/pam/fedora/soos/password-auth:12`, `packaging/pam/arch/system-auth:5`, `packaging/pam/arch/system-auth.snippet:5`, `crates/pam/src/config.rs:9`, `crates/daemon/src/pipeline.rs:19`, `crates/daemon/src/dispatcher.rs:532-544`, `crates/daemon/src/dispatcher.rs:607-618`, `crates/admin-cli/src/gdm.rs:97`, `AI/ARCHITECTURE.md:141`, `Docs/PAM_MODULE.md:20`, `Docs/PAM_MODULE.md:86`, `Docs/DISTRIBUTION_DEPLOYMENT.md:20`, `Docs/PACKAGING_AND_PROVISIONING.md:65`, `AGENTS.md:72`, `AI/DECISIONS.md:8`
- **Evidence**: `grep -rn 'timeout_ms='` shows every shipped PAM profile (packaging/pam/debian/soos:7-9, fedora system-auth:12, password-auth:12, arch system-auth:5, snippet:5) uses `pam_soos.so timeout_ms=250`, and tests/invariants/src/lib.rs:603/623/654/671 hard-assert that literal string, so the packaging cannot be updated without editing the invariant test. Meanwhile crates/pam/src/config.rs:9 `DEFAULT_TIMEOUT_MS = 1000`, crates/daemon/src/pipeline.rs:19 `DECISION_BUDGET_MS = 900`, crates/admin-cli/src/gdm.rs:97 writes `timeout_ms=2500` for GDM, VERIFICATION_MATRIX ASG4 (line 396) states the budget was calibrated "ensuring reliable first-attempt authentication within PAM 1000ms deadline" with an 800 ms camera wake, and crates/daemon/src/dispatcher.rs:608-618 clamps the daemon's decision budget to the client's `deadline_monotonic_ns`. project-facts.md:53-56 already records the 200–250 ms prose drift in AGENTS.md/ARCHITECTURE/DECISIONS/PAM_MODULE/SECURITY guidelines but the packaging and the invariant test were not updated.
- **Impact**: Users who install via .deb/.rpm/PKGBUILD/install.sh get a 250 ms client deadline; with the default camera idle standby (10 s, CAM2) the 800 ms wake alone exceeds it, so the daemon answers Unavailable and every first login/sudo attempt silently falls back to password — facial auth effectively works only when the camera is already hot. The invariant test enshrines the stale value, so a developer fixing packaging breaks CI. Docs advertise a 200–250 ms budget that the code no longer implements.
- **Recommendation**: Decide the canonical console/sudo timeout (likely the 1000 ms default, or omit `timeout_ms=` so the module default applies), update all packaging templates, tests/docker Dockerfiles, Docs and the ADR, and change `test_pam_config_ordering_matches_spec` to derive the expected value from a single constant (or to assert only ordering plus a regex `timeout_ms=[0-9]+` whose value equals `soos_pam::config::DEFAULT_TIMEOUT_MS`). Add an ADR entry in AI/DECISIONS.md replacing the "200–250ms" rule with "explicit deadline derived from clamped timeout_ms".
- **Verifier notes**: `grep -rn 'timeout_ms=' packaging tests/docker crates/admin-cli/src`: packaging/pam/debian/soos:7,9; packaging/pam/arch/system-auth:5, system-auth.snippet:5; packaging/pam/fedora/soos/system-auth:12, password-auth:12 and README:7 all use `timeout_ms=250`; tests/docker/Dockerfile.{ubuntu,fedora,arch} likewise. tests/invariants/src/lib.rs:603,623,654,671 hard-code the literal `pam_soos.so timeout_ms=250`. crates/pam/src/config.rs:9 `DEFAULT_TIMEOUT_MS: u64 = 1000` (MIN 10 / MAX 5000, clamped at :116); crates/daemon/src/pipeline.rs:19 `DECISION_BUDGET_MS = 900`; crates/daemon/src/dispatcher.rs:607-618 clamps the budget to the client deadline; crates/admin-cli/src/gdm.rs:97,105 writes `timeout_ms=2500`; crates/daemon/tests/config_tests.rs:183-188 asserts 900 ms "to allow camera cold-start while staying within 1000ms PAM deadline". scripts/install.sh:326 copies packaging/pam, so installed systems really get 250 ms. One detail is unverified prose: the "800 ms camera wake" figure appears only in AI/VERIFICATION_MATRIX.md:396 (ASG4); `grep -rn 'from_millis(8\|800' crates/daemon/src crates/camera-v4l/src` finds no such constant (only warmup_frames default 20 at config_tests.rs:199). The core mismatch (250 in packaging vs 1000 default / 900 daemon budget) and the invariant test enshrining the stale literal are confirmed.

#### TCI-03 — Physical validation scripts invoke soos-enroll with the removed --skip-root-check flag, so the suite cannot run against the current CLI

GitHub issue: #186

**MAJOR** · PARTIAL · bug · effort S

- **Locations**: `tests/physical/enrollment_test.sh:212-217`, `tests/physical/multi_user_test.sh:213`, `tests/physical/adversarial_test.sh:214`, `tests/physical/adversarial_test.sh:236`, `tests/physical/adversarial_test.sh:258`, `tests/physical/adversarial_test.sh:280`, `AI/VERIFICATION_MATRIX.md:167`, `AI/VERIFICATION_MATRIX.md:245-249`, `tests/invariants/src/lib.rs:1182-1248`
- **Evidence**: tests/physical/enrollment_test.sh:212-217 builds `CLI_COMMON_FLAGS=( ... "--skip-root-check" )` for every soos-enroll call; adversarial_test.sh:214/236/258/280 and multi_user_test.sh:213 do the same. `grep -rn 'skip.root.check' crates/enrollment-cli/src` returns nothing, and VERIFICATION_MATRIX EN11 (line 167) states the flag was removed with `test_cli_rejects_skip_root_check_argument` (exists: 1 match) proving clap rejects it. The invariant `test_physical_hardware_validation_suite_spec` (lib.rs:1182-1248) only greps for the words enroll/verify/list/delete/photo/screen/video and runs `--help`, so it cannot detect that the scripts are broken.
- **Impact**: PH1, PH3 and PH5 are marked Verified but the scripts fail at the first soos-enroll invocation with a clap "unexpected argument" error; the adversarial (PAD) evaluation the user asked to improve has no runnable harness. Acceptance evidence in the matrix is stale.
- **Recommendation**: Remove `--skip-root-check` from the three scripts (they already require root or --dry-run) and re-run them on hardware; extend the invariant test to assert the scripts do not reference CLI flags that `soos-enroll --help` does not list (e.g. run `soos-enroll --help` from target if present, or grep-deny the removed flag). Update PH1/PH3/PH5 evidence dates.
- **Verifier notes**: Confirmed the bug: tests/physical/enrollment_test.sh:216, multi_user_test.sh:213, adversarial_test.sh:214,236,258,280 pass `--skip-root-check`; `grep -rn 'skip.root.check' crates/enrollment-cli/src` -> nothing; crates/enrollment-cli/tests/path_validation_tests.rs:168-173 asserts clap rejects it. tests/invariants/src/lib.rs:1182-1190 only greps subcommand words. Recommendation is wrong: the reviewer says the scripts "already require root or --dry-run", but `grep -n 'id -u\|EUID\|sudo\|DRY' tests/physical/*.sh` finds no root gate or dry-run mode in any of the three scripts (enrollment_test.sh header :11-21 lists only --device/--biometrics-dir/--key-file/--models-dir/--uid/--mock). crates/enrollment-cli/src/service.rs:163-165 enforces EUID 0, so simply deleting the flag leaves the scripts failing with the root-privileges error when run as a normal user (their evident design, with temp dirs). The fix must also add an explicit root/sudo requirement (or a documented `sudo` invocation) to the scripts, and the invariant test should deny the removed flag.

#### TCI-04 — AI/VERIFICATION_MATRIX.md cites tests and files that do not exist and contradicts itself on live class index, decision budget and the removed landmark detector

GitHub issue: #187

**MAJOR** · CONFIRMED · doc-drift · effort M

- **Locations**: `AI/VERIFICATION_MATRIX.md:393`, `AI/VERIFICATION_MATRIX.md:383`, `AI/VERIFICATION_MATRIX.md:405`, `AI/VERIFICATION_MATRIX.md:396`, `AI/VERIFICATION_MATRIX.md:428`, `AI/VERIFICATION_MATRIX.md:187`, `AI/VERIFICATION_MATRIX.md:318`, `AI/VERIFICATION_MATRIX.md:78`, `AI/VERIFICATION_MATRIX.md:223`, `AI/VERIFICATION_MATRIX.md:301`, `AI/VERIFICATION_MATRIX.md:372`, `AI/VERIFICATION_MATRIX.md:385`, `AI/VERIFICATION_MATRIX.md:235-237`, `AI/DECISIONS.md:20`, `Docs/INFERENCE_ORT_CRATE.md:12`, `Docs/INFERENCE_ORT_CRATE.md:114`, `Docs/INFERENCE_ORT_CRATE.md:147`
- **Evidence**: `grep -rl 'fn <name>' crates tests` gives 0 matches for four cited tests: `test_minifasnet_live_class_index_is_1` (ASG1, line 393; the real test is `test_pad_default_live_class_index_is_one` at crates/inference-ort/tests/pad_tests.rs:298), `test_default_timeout_is_1000ms` (CAM2, line 383), `test_gdm_pam_line_includes_timeout_ms_2500` (LSF1, line 405; real gdm_tests are `test_gdm_subcommand_parsing`/`test_gdm_disable_and_enable_lifecycle`), `test_daemon_pipeline_decision_budget_calibrated_for_warmup` (ASG4, line 396; real: `test_decision_budget_calibrated_to_pam_deadline` at crates/daemon/tests/config_tests.rs:183). GARP2 (line 428) cites `tests/physical_hardware_tests.rs` — `find crates -name physical_hardware_tests.rs` returns nothing. Contradictions: PAD1 (187) and NGM9 (318) say `live_class_index = 0`, ASG1 (393) says index 1 (code: DEFAULT_MINIFASNET_LIVE_CLASS_INDEX = 1 per project-facts); AI/DECISIONS.md:20 and Docs/INFERENCE_ORT_CRATE.md:12/114/147 still say 0. D7 (78) says "150ms decision budget enforced" while ASG4 (396) and crates/daemon/src/pipeline.rs:19 say 900 ms. VZF3 (223) lists `OrtLandmarkDetector` although NGM6 (301) records its removal. GEPU3 (372) and PRX2 (385) cite source files/methods (`SoosApp::pause_daemon`, `crates/gui/src/ipc_camera.rs`) as "tests". PK5-PK7 (235-237) cite `tests/docker/test_packages.sh`, which no script or workflow invokes (`grep -rn test_packages` only hits the matrix).
- **Impact**: The matrix is the contractual acceptance record (AGENTS.md, traceability-agent skill). Non-existent test names mean criteria are marked Verified without evidence; contradictory constants (class index, budget) will mislead the next spoofing/latency work the user requested and can cause an agent to "fix" code toward stale prose.
- **Recommendation**: Correct the four test names and the GARP2 path, resolve the class-index rows (PAD1/NGM9/DECISIONS/INFERENCE_ORT_CRATE → index 1 with an ADR), update D7 to 900 ms, drop `OrtLandmarkDetector` from VZF3, replace non-test evidence in GEPU3/PRX2 with real test names or mark as manual verification, and either wire test_packages.sh into run_matrix.sh/CI or remove it from PK5-PK7. Add a CI/invariant check that every backticked `test_*` identifier in the matrix resolves to a `fn` in the workspace (a shell loop like the one used here).
- **Verifier notes**: Ran `grep -rln "fn <name>" crates tests` for each cited test: test_minifasnet_live_class_index_is_1, test_default_timeout_is_1000ms, test_gdm_pam_line_includes_timeout_ms_2500, test_daemon_pipeline_decision_budget_calibrated_for_warmup -> 0 matches; the real tests are test_pad_default_live_class_index_is_one (crates/inference-ort/tests/pad_tests.rs) and test_decision_budget_calibrated_to_pam_deadline (crates/daemon/tests/config_tests.rs:183). `find . -name physical_hardware_tests.rs` -> none. #### TCI-05 — scripts/sync_issue.py maps two different backlog issues to #22, runs after commit/push (leaving BACKLOG.md dirty and unpushed) and marks every sub-issue complete on any push

GitHub issue: #188

**MAJOR** · CONFIRMED · bug · effort S

- **Locations**: `scripts/sync_issue.py:128-129`, `scripts/sync_issue.py:104`, `scripts/sync_issue.py:48`, `scripts/sync_issue.py:296-314`, `scripts/sync_issue.py:304`, `AI/BACKLOG.md:822`, `AI/BACKLOG.md:1713`, `save.sh:403`, `save.sh:419`, `save.sh:429`, `scripts/pr_loop.sh:101`, `AI/VERIFICATION_MATRIX.md:366`
- **Evidence**: AI/BACKLOG.md has two headings numbered 22: line 822 `### Issue #22 — feat(camera-v4l): Automatic format negotiation` and line 1713 `### Issue #22: admin-cli — Visual Debugging GUI`. sync_issue.py:104 maps `feat/camera-format-negotiation` → 22 and :128-129 map `feat/admin-debug-gui` and `feat/guided-enrollment-production-unlock` → 22 as well; BACKLOG_TO_GITHUB[22] = 61 (line 48). VERIFICATION_MATRIX:366 labels the GUI work "Issue #22 / GitHub #61". In `--auto` mode (lines 296-314) the regex `#22\.[0-9]+` (line 304) collects sub-issues of BOTH headings and PATCHes GitHub issue #61's body plus posts "All sub-issues for Issue #22 completed". save.sh runs `git commit` (403) and `git push` (419) and only then `python3 ./scripts/sync_issue.py --auto` (429), which rewrites AI/BACKLOG.md in the working tree; pr_loop.sh:101 does the same after save.sh returned. There is no completion check: any push on a mapped branch checks off all sub-issues.
- **Impact**: Traceability is corrupted: GUI branches tick the camera-format-negotiation GitHub issue; BACKLOG.md edits are never part of the PR (dirty tree after push, so pr_loop.sh:221-225 also refuses to sync main); partial work is reported as complete on GitHub.
- **Recommendation**: Renumber the second Issue #22 (e.g. #22b→#51) in AI/BACKLOG.md, VERIFICATION_MATRIX and BRANCH_TO_ISSUE; move the `sync_issue.py --auto` call before `git add .`/commit in save.sh (or make it a dry-run that only prints the `Closes #N` id) and make completion explicit (`--subissue` per delivered item, as the traceability-agent skill describes) instead of complete-all on push. Add a unit test for the script (python -m unittest) that fails on duplicate backlog numbers and on branch→issue collisions.
- **Verifier notes**: AI/BACKLOG.md:822 and :1713 are both `### Issue #22`, and both have sub-issues `**#22.1**..**#22.4**` (lines 829-847 and 1724-1738). scripts/sync_issue.py:48 `22: 61`, :104 `feat/camera-format-negotiation: 22`, :128-129 `feat/admin-debug-gui` and `feat/guided-enrollment-production-unlock` -> 22; :304 regex `#22\.[0-9]+` collects sub-ids from both headings in --auto mode (:296-314) and PATCHes GitHub #61 with a "All sub-issues ... completed" comment with no completion check. Additional detail: sync_local_backlog_subissues (:181-184) uses `re.sub(..., count=1)`, so only the first (camera) heading's boxes are ever ticked locally. save.sh:277 `git add .`, :403 `git commit`, :419 `git push`, :429 `sync_issue.py --auto` order confirmed; scripts/pr_loop.sh:101 same; pr_loop.sh:221-225 refuses to switch main when the tree is dirty. AI/VERIFICATION_MATRIX.md:366 labels the GUI component "Issue #22 / GitHub #61", so the camera issue's true GitHub number must be looked up before renumbering.

#### TCI-06 — Docker PAM matrix: CI runs Ubuntu only, T6/T8 cannot fail, T2 never asserts the deadline, Deny/truncated modes are unused, and test_packages.sh is never executed

GitHub issue: #189

**MAJOR** · PARTIAL · test-gap · effort M

- **Locations**: `.github/workflows/ci.yml:206-211`, `tests/docker/test_suite.sh:132-140`, `tests/docker/test_suite.sh:236-240`, `tests/docker/test_suite.sh:279-283`, `tests/docker/test_suite.sh:87`, `tests/docker/mock_daemon.py:59`, `tests/docker/mock_daemon.py:129-137`, `tests/docker/test_packages.sh:1-12`, `tests/invariants/src/lib.rs:445-501`, `AI/VERIFICATION_MATRIX.md:58`, `Docs/PAM_DOCKER_TEST_MATRIX.md:46-58`
- **Evidence**: ci.yml:206-211 runs only `docker run ... soos-sandbox:ci bash /workspace/tests/docker/test_suite.sh` (Ubuntu image from root Dockerfile); Fedora/Arch (tests/docker/Dockerfile.fedora, Dockerfile.arch, run_matrix.sh) never run in CI, yet PA10 (matrix line 58) is "Verified" by `test_pam_docker_matrix_files_and_distro_configs_exist`, which only checks file existence/non-emptiness and a grep (lib.rs:445-501). test_suite.sh:132-140 computes `DIFF_MS` for T2 but only prints it — the >250 ms timeout invariant (PA2) is not asserted, and the mock delay is 0.5 s so a 1000 ms default would also "pass". T6 nominal failure (236-240) and T8 (279-283) end in `warn` and never `exit 1`. mock_daemon.py supports `deny` and `crash-truncated` (lines 59, 129-137) but test_suite.sh never uses them, so Verdict::Deny → PAM_IGNORE → password (ARCHITECTURE §3 state matrix) has no in-stack test. test_suite.sh:87 `chmod 777 /run/soos` contradicts the 0750 invariant (sandbox only, but hides a potential permission check). tests/docker/test_packages.sh (`cargo build --release --workspace` + package install) is referenced only by VERIFICATION_MATRIX PK5-PK7.
- **Impact**: Regression in Fedora/Arch PAM stacks, in the timeout bound, or in Deny handling would pass CI; three matrix rows (PA10, PK5-7, PA2's Docker half) overstate the evidence.
- **Recommendation**: Assert `DIFF_MS < timeout_ms + margin` in T2 and add a T2b with `--delay` above the configured timeout; make T6 nominal and T8 hard failures (or move T8 to a documented expected-warn with explicit exit code assertion); add T10 (deny → PAM_IGNORE + password OK) and T11 (crash-truncated); add a `pam-matrix` CI job (matrix strategy ubuntu/fedora/arch, can be nightly) that runs run_matrix.sh, and either call test_packages.sh from it or delete the script and PK5-7 references; drop the 777 chmod in favour of `chmod 750` + `chgrp`.
- **Verifier notes**: Confirmed: .github/workflows/ci.yml:206-211 runs only the Ubuntu `soos-sandbox:ci` image; no fedora/arch job. tests/docker/test_suite.sh:128 uses `--mode timeout --delay 0.5`, :131-136 computes DIFF_MS and only prints it. mock_daemon.py:124-128 shows timeout mode sleeps then sends ALLOW, so T2 (valid password) passes whether or not the module timed out. T6 nominal (:236-240) and T8 (:279-283) only `warn`; T6's password-fallback half does `exit 1` (:246-249). `chmod 777 /run/soos` at :87. mock modes `deny`/`crash-truncated` exist (mock_daemon.py:59,132-136) and are never used (:107,128,165,182,202,232 use allow/timeout/crash-immediate/crash-partial). Overstated part: T3 (:146-154) runs during the same timeout-mode daemon with a wrong password and `exit 1`s if accepted, which does functionally prove the module returned PAM_IGNORE rather than the delayed ALLOW; so the timeout *behaviour* is asserted, only the elapsed-time bound is not. Note for the fix: if Docker configs move to timeout_ms=1000 (TCI-02) the 0.5 s mock delay must be raised or T3 starts failing (ALLOW would arrive before the deadline and a wrong password would be accepted).

#### TCI-11 — Text-grep invariants and candid_review.sh have lexing holes: cfg(test) brace skipping, `grep -v tests` dropping real hits, English words flagged as French, two-dot diff against a moving origin/main, text-only Tokio check

GitHub issue: #242

**MINOR** · CONFIRMED · reliability · effort M

- **Locations**: `tests/invariants/src/lib.rs:403-443`, `tests/invariants/src/lib.rs:97-107`, `tests/invariants/src/lib.rs:121-137`, `scripts/candid_review.sh:108`, `scripts/candid_review.sh:159`, `scripts/candid_review.sh:171`, `scripts/candid_review.sh:51`, `scripts/candid_review.sh:65`, `scripts/candid_review.sh:78`
- **Evidence**: lib.rs:411-434 `extract_production_code` treats any line containing `#[cfg(test)]` as the start of a block and skips until the next `{` then to the matching `}` — an attribute on `mod tests;` or on a `use` line would swallow following production code; the unwrap check (97-107) only matches `.unwrap()`/`.expect(` substrings and ignores `panic!`, `unreachable!`, `[i]` (those rely on clippy lints, which the PAM crate can `#[allow]`, cf. lib.rs:318). lib.rs:134 `!content.contains("tokio")` inspects only crates/pam/Cargo.toml text; a transitive Tokio via soos-protocol would pass (`cargo tree -p soos-pam -i tokio` currently confirms none). candid_review.sh:108 and :159 pipe through `grep -v 'tests'`, so an added production line like `let attests = x.unwrap();` or any line containing the substring "tests" is excluded. :171 lists two French markers that are also ordinary English words (the verb meaning "to decant" and the noun for "focus"; see the regex at candid_review.sh:171) → an English comment using them is a false positive that fails pre-commit (this very report hit it). :51 uses `origin/main...HEAD` but :65/:78/:108/:119/:130/:159/:171 use two-dot `git diff origin/main`, so once main advances past the branch the diff contains reversed upstream hunks and audits fire on other people's code until rebase.
- **Impact**: The gates can both miss real violations (masking) and block legitimate commits (false positives), weakening trust in the "Layer 1 deterministic" guarantee.
- **Recommendation**: Use `git diff $(git merge-base origin/main HEAD)` everywhere in candid_review.sh; drop `grep -v tests` in favour of path filtering (`:!crates/pam/tests`) plus the cfg(test) extractor; remove ambiguous English words from the French list; implement the Tokio invariant with `cargo metadata`/`cargo tree -p soos-pam -e normal` in the invariant test; harden `extract_production_code` to require `mod ... {` after `#[cfg(test)]` (add unit tests for the extractor).
- **Verifier notes**: tests/invariants/src/lib.rs:411-434 `extract_production_code`: any line containing `#[cfg(test)]` triggers skip-to-next-`{`-then-matching-`}`, so `#[cfg(test)] mod tests;` or a cfg(test) `use` would swallow following production code; :97-107 matches only `.unwrap()`/`.expect(`; :134 `!content.contains("tokio")` on crates/pam/Cargo.toml text only. scripts/candid_review.sh:51 uses `"$BASE_REF"...HEAD` while :65,78,108,119,130,159,171 use two-dot `git diff "$BASE_REF"`; :108 and :159 end with `| grep -v 'tests'`; :171 French regex includes two alternatives that are also valid English words (see the regex itself). crates/pam/src/lib.rs:318 shows the PAM crate does use `#[allow(clippy::...)]` with reasons.

#### TCI-12 — Dependency hygiene: stale deny.toml skip reasons, ort pinned to a release candidate, Dependabot covers only github-actions while the daily advisory job has no cargo counterpart

GitHub issue: #243

**MINOR** · PARTIAL · design · effort S

- **Locations**: `deny.toml:64-65`, `deny.toml:70`, `Cargo.toml:45`, `Cargo.toml:61`, `.github/dependabot.yml:1-14`, `.github/workflows/ci.yml:31-33`, `Docs/CI_CD_AND_SECURITY.md:167-168`
- **Evidence**: `cargo tree -i getrandom@0.3.4` shows it is a direct dependency of soos-admin-cli, soos-biometric-store, soos-enrollment-cli, soos-evidence-store and soos-pam (Cargo.toml:61 `getrandom = "0.3"`), not "Transitive dependency of rand_core 0.9 via proptest" as deny.toml:65 states; the third version 0.4.3 (via tempfile) is not documented. `cargo deny --locked check bans licenses sources` → ok, so the skip set is functionally consistent but the rationale is wrong. Cargo.toml:45 `ort = "2.0.0-rc.13"` (Cargo.lock confirms) — a release-candidate ABI in the production inference path. .github/dependabot.yml lists only `package-ecosystem: github-actions`; ci.yml:31-33 runs cargo-deny daily but nothing proposes cargo updates, and Docs/CI_CD_AND_SECURITY.md:167-168 acknowledges Dependabot PRs cannot pass the layer-2 gate without a hand-written report.
- **Impact**: Skip entries with wrong reasons get cargo-culted and hide real duplicate-version regressions; rc pins can change API between rc's; advisory findings have no automated remediation path.
- **Recommendation**: Rewrite the skip reasons from `cargo tree -i` output (and add `getrandom@0.4.3`'s origin); bump getrandom to 0.4 if the direct users allow it; track ort GA and pin `=2.0.0-rc.13` explicitly until then; add a `cargo` ecosystem entry to dependabot.yml (grouped, weekly) and document how the candid report is produced for bot branches.
- **Verifier notes**: Confirmed: `cargo tree --locked -i getrandom@0.3.4 --depth 1` -> direct dependency of soos-admin-cli, soos-biometric-store, soos-enrollment-cli, soos-evidence-store, soos-pam (plus ahash, rand_core), so deny.toml:65's "Transitive ... via proptest" reason is wrong; getrandom@0.4.3 comes from tempfile (dev-dep) and is absent from the skip list (Cargo.lock:1150/1161/1173 show three versions). Cargo.toml:45 `ort = "2.0.0-rc.13"`, Cargo.lock:2383 confirms; with `--locked` builds the rc is already effectively pinned, so the `=` pin adds little. Refuted part: the Dependabot recommendation contradicts a documented decision — .github/dependabot.yml:12-14 states "Cargo dependencies are intentionally not auto-bumped: deny.toml enforces multiple-versions = "deny", so crate updates are reviewed through the normal dev-workflow"; adding a cargo ecosystem would also collide with the fingerprint-bound candid gate (Docs/CI_CD_AND_SECURITY.md:167-168). Keep only the skip-reason rewrite and the getrandom@0.4.3 entry.

#### TCI-13 — Docker sandbox reuses a stale host-built libpam_soos.so from the bind mount and builds an unpinned 'stable' toolchain inside images

GitHub issue: #244

**MINOR** · PARTIAL · reliability · effort S

- **Locations**: `tests/docker/test_suite.sh:61-65`, `run_tests.sh:104-108`, `Dockerfile:60-63`, `tests/docker/Dockerfile.ubuntu:26-28`, `tests/docker/Dockerfile.fedora:28-30`, `tests/docker/Dockerfile.arch:23-25`, `rust-toolchain.toml:1-3`, `.github/workflows/ci.yml:196-204`
- **Evidence**: test_suite.sh:61-65: `SO_PATH=target/release/libpam_soos.so; if [[ ! -f ... ]]; then cargo build --release -p soos-pam; fi` — with the workspace bind-mounted (run_tests.sh:106, ci.yml:209) any pre-existing host release artifact (possibly from an older commit or a different glibc) is deployed instead of rebuilding. Dockerfiles install `--default-toolchain stable` at image build time (Dockerfile:60-63 etc.) while the repo toolchain is `channel = "stable"` resolved at checkout; the GHA layer cache (ci.yml:203-204) freezes whatever stable was current when main last built the image.
- **Impact**: Local `./run_tests.sh` can validate an outdated .so (false green after a PAM change); the container toolchain can drift from the one used by clippy/test jobs, and a `--locked` Cargo.lock requiring a newer rustc would fail only in the cached image.
- **Recommendation**: Always run `cargo build --locked --release -p soos-pam` in test_suite.sh (or compare the .so mtime with `git ls-files -z crates/pam | xargs stat`), and install the toolchain in the sandbox from /workspace/rust-toolchain.toml at run time (`rustup show` inside the container) so all jobs use the same version.
- **Verifier notes**: tests/docker/test_suite.sh:61-65 reuses `target/release/libpam_soos.so` if present and only builds when missing; run_tests.sh:104-108 bind-mounts `$(pwd)` as /workspace and `grep -n 'cargo build' run_tests.sh` -> none, so a local run can deploy a stale host artifact (confirmed for local use). Dockerfile:60-63 and tests/docker/Dockerfile.ubuntu:26-28 install `--default-toolchain stable` at image build; rust-toolchain.toml is `channel = "stable"`. Overstated part: in CI (ci.yml:190-192 fresh checkout, no restored target dir) there is no stale .so, so the false-green risk is local-only; the GHA layer cache (ci.yml:203-204) only freezes the container toolchain, and rustup inside the container will honour /workspace/rust-toolchain.toml at run time only if it is newer than the installed stable, which it is not (same channel). Toolchain drift is therefore a cache-age issue, not a correctness bug today.

#### TCI-15 — save.sh auto-generates misleading Conventional Commit subjects and stages everything with `git add .`

GitHub issue: #245

**MINOR** · CONFIRMED · improvement · effort S

- **Locations**: `save.sh:343-349`, `save.sh:351-365`, `save.sh:277`, `save.sh:199-206`, `Docs/COMMIT_CONVENTION.md:1-20`
- **Evidence**: save.sh:343-349 emits `feat(<crate>): update component implementation` for any change to a crate's src (including bug fixes or refactors), :351-365 appends body bullets like `- test: update test suites...` that look like additional subjects, and :384-386 appends `[N file(s) modified]`. These subjects become the squash-merge subject on main (pr_loop.sh:129 uses the first line as PR title). save.sh:277 `git add .` stages every untracked file, which is how the root scratch scripts (TCI-07) were committed.
- **Impact**: Changelog quality on main degrades (wrong type `feat` for fixes), and accidental artifacts get committed.
- **Recommendation**: Require `-m` (fail if no message) or infer the type from the branch prefix (`fix/` → fix, `chore/` → chore, `test/` → test) and the scope from the crate; replace `git add .` with `git add -u` plus explicit `git add` of new files under crates/, tests/, Docs/, AI/, scripts/, packaging/.
- **Verifier notes**: save.sh:299 uses a custom message only when `-m` is given; otherwise :343-349 emits `feat(<crate>): update component implementation` / `feat(workspace): ...` regardless of change type, :351-365 appends `test:`/`docs:`/`chore:`-prefixed bullets, :384-386 appends `[N file(s) modified]`. save.sh:277 `git add .` stages every untracked file (how the TCI-07 scratch files entered history). scripts/pr_loop.sh:129 `gh pr create --title "$FIRST_LINE"` makes that subject the squash-merge subject.

#### TCI-14 — Stale AI review artifacts committed with inconsistent names (plan_evaluation_report.md vs plan_evaluator_report.md; candid report bound to an already-merged branch)

GitHub issue: #267

**SUGGESTION** · CONFIRMED · dead-code · effort S

- **Locations**: `AI/plan_evaluation_report.md:1-5`, `AI/plan_evaluator_report.md:1-6`, `AI/candid_review_report.md:1-6`, `scripts/candid_subagent.sh:57`, `scripts/candid_subagent.sh:129`
- **Evidence**: AI/plan_evaluation_report.md is dated 2026-09-13 for Issue #3 (last touched in commit 21629d6) while AI/plan_evaluator_report.md is for Issue #50 (commit 94d9ebe); the plan-evaluator skill writes the latter name, so the former is an orphan. AI/candid_review_report.md:4-6 targets branch `chore/skills-ci-hardening` with merge-base 9ab7470 (already merged as #142). candid_subagent.sh:57/129 excludes only AI/candid_review_report.md from the fingerprint, so every plan-report rewrite changes the reviewed diff.
- **Impact**: Confusing history and one dead file; report churn inflates every PR diff and fingerprint.
- **Recommendation**: Delete AI/plan_evaluation_report.md; consider excluding AI/plan_evaluator_report.md from the fingerprint like the candid report (or moving both reports under AI/reviews/<issue>/ so they are per-issue artifacts rather than overwritten singletons).
- **Verifier notes**: AI/plan_evaluation_report.md:1-5 is for Issue #3 dated 2026-09-13; `git log --oneline -1 -- AI/plan_evaluation_report.md` -> 21629d6 (Issue #3 PR). AI/plan_evaluator_report.md is Issue #50, 2026-09-29; .agents/skills/plan-evaluator/SKILL.md:8,63 and dev-workflow/SKILL.md:33 name only plan_evaluator_report.md, so the former is orphaned. AI/candid_review_report.md:4-5 targets `chore/skills-ci-hardening` at merge-base 9ab7470 (merged as #142). scripts/candid_subagent.sh:57 `REPORT_FILE="AI/candid_review_report.md"` and :129 excludes only that file from the fingerprint diff.

## Appendix A — Refuted Findings

- **PAD-15** (pad) — warmup_frames is forced to 0 whenever a [pipeline] section exists, feeding unconverged AEC frames to PAD. *Reason*: crates/daemon/src/config.rs:244 `warmup_frames = pipe.warmup_frames.unwrap_or(0)` is intentional, documented and contractually tested: AI/walkthroughs/73:35 ('defaulting to 0 for instant capture while allowing host overrides'), AI/walkthroughs/74:33 and :84 (criterion CLP2 'daemon.toml defaults warmup_frames = 0 for instant wake'), and crates/daemon/tests/config_tests.rs:232-240 `test_daemon_toml_default_warmup_frames_is_zero`, plus :199-200 asserting `CameraConfig::default().warmup_frames == 20` (C5). Walkthrough 72:61 talks about `CameraConfig::default()`, which is still 20 (camera-v4l config.rs:49), so there is no contradiction. The reviewer's recommendation ('keep the crate default') would require changing an existing contract test, which AGENTS.md forbids. The residual concern (AEC-unconverged first frames reaching PAD) is a design trade-off already accepted for lock-screen latency and is better addressed by the PAD-02 aggregator (discount the first frames / require consecutive live frames).
